# Current sprint tracker and design review

This document is the repository's current engineering tracker. It began as the August 2026 design
review and intentionally retains the detailed evidence and decision records below. Keep the status line
of each task and the sprint summary current when work lands; the older analysis remains useful context.
Line references and quoted code may drift, so trust the surrounding description over an old line number.

Severity: **P0** correctness/data-loss · **P1** API or protocol fidelity · **P2** ergonomics/hygiene.
Status: `todo` · `in-progress` · `done` · `deferred` · `wontfix`.

## Current sprint

**Objective:** expand T-9 schema-driven generation one bounded command family at a time, using the
schema-conformance and XML-fidelity oracles as adoption gates.

**Unblocked: T-15 and T-16a's blocking scope are both done.** Family adoption was gated on precisely
those two, and not the rest of T-16 — both were properties of the pipeline that would have gotten
retroactively expensive once more families depended on them, and both were measurable enough to say
when they were finished. T-15 (fail closed on schema defects) replaces a human reading
`CONFORMANCE.md` with a build error, which the remaining families need: every family adopted so far was
hand-picked for a low defect count, and that supply is exhausted. T-16a's blocking concern was never
"every field of provenance must be reacquirable" — it was that without *any* fail-closed provenance
check, a family could be generated from stale or silently mixed source bytes and nothing would catch
it, and that risk compounds with every family added while the gap stays open. Content-addressed
provenance (SHA-256 of the source document, checked for equality across every command in a batch)
closes exactly that detection gap, independent of whether `SourceRevision` is populated. So T-16a was
scoped to content-addressed local extraction provenance and is **done** on that scope (see T-16a
below). `SourceRevision` staying empty is a separate, bounded cost: it means a future pinned-vendoring
process or T-16b's live extraction will still require a one-time mechanical backfill of
already-committed snapshots, the same kind of value backfill T-16a itself just did for `Source`. That
cost doesn't grow with each additional family adopted in the meantime, so it doesn't block them.
Runtime provenance remains partial pending T-16c and does not gate a family either, since adoption
gates on the fixtures already committed, not on resolving their container identity retroactively. The
next action is T-9's ranking step, not further T-15/T-16a work — see T-9's **Next** field below.

**Not blocking:** T-16b (migrating Stage A to live schema extraction) and T-16c (pinning the integration
stack). Both are worth doing and neither gates a family. T-16b is a source migration with its own risk
surface, and if it lands before T-16a's re-extraction it saves doing that re-extraction twice — a
sequencing preference, not a dependency. T-16c gates trusting a *new* live capture as evidence, not
adopting a family against the fixtures already committed.

**Current baseline (after `asset`'s adoption, T-15, and T-16a).** Read from the committed reports, not
from the per-family narratives below; the parenthesised figure is the previous baseline, taken after
`tls_certificate`, five families back:

- 122 registered commands and 321 request/response fixtures;
- request fidelity (`REQUEST-FIDELITY.txt`): 300 clean, 19 with loss, 2 decode errors, 0 advisory;
  16 commands carry loss, and all 122 have a recorded request;
- response fidelity (`FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`): 188 clean (186), 120 with loss (120), 12 decode errors
  (14), 1 advisory, 321 total; 37 of 122 commands carry loss; 238 distinct dropped paths (249);
- schema conformance (`CONFORMANCE.md`): 66 fully conformant commands (59), 56 with actionable findings,
  512 actionable (584) split 248 request / 264 response, 589 suppressed (698), 67 independently confirmed
  by the fidelity corpus;
- generated and adopted families, in adoption order: `note`, `override`, `role`, `group`, `tag`,
  `schedule`, `alert`, `filter`, `tls_certificate`, `permission`, `ticket`, `credential`, `port_list`,
  `asset` — 14 families, 49 generated files (42 command files plus 7 `*_shared.go`; the manifest's
  conventional maximum is 56, since a family whose commands share no nested shape emits no shared file);
- working generator safeguards: committed snapshots behind the AGPL boundary, manifest-owned scope,
  family-local naming/deduplication, review-only generation, deterministic `-check`, CI drift checks,
  and — new since the previous baseline — fail-closed gates on schema defects (T-15) and on absent,
  malformed, or mixed schema provenance (T-16a).

Keep this block current at each adoption. It was five families stale when T-16a landed, and the family
selection method below ranks candidates against these numbers.

**Family selection method (this milestone):** ranked all ten create/get/modify command families against
the six candidate criteria (small generated response graph, few unexplained schema defects, findings
confirmed by both oracles, existing fixture coverage, limited public-API impact, create/get/modify shape)
by rank-summing four quantifiable proxies drawn from `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md` and the
fidelity corpus: combined `create_X`/`get_Xs`/`modify_X.go` line count (response-graph size), SCHEMA-DEFECT
count, `[fidelity]`-confirmed finding count, and total actionable finding count, with fixture-completeness
as a tie-break. `role` ranked first (score 11, vs. `group`'s 18 and `tag`'s 19.5) — smallest combined
source (83 lines), zero SCHEMA-DEFECT findings, the highest fidelity-confirmation ratio of any candidate,
and purely-additive findings (all `MISSING`/`TYPE`, no `EXTRA`/`KIND`), so adopting it carries no field
removal or type-restructuring risk. `permission`, one of the two families suggested at the start of this
milestone, ranked worst (score 31) once calculated rather than picked intuitively.

**`role` adopted:** went through the same review-only stage `override` did — added to
`internal/codegen/manifest.json` as `review_only`, schema snapshots and golden output extracted and
committed (`internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_role,get_roles,modify_role}.*`), generated
API and oracle deltas reviewed — then `review_only` was dropped and `hack/generate.sh` written into
`commands/`. `create_role`/`modify_role` generate byte-identical to the hand-written originals (already
fully conformant, so nothing to review there beyond confirming the byte-identity). `get_roles` gains
request filter attributes (`role_id`, `filter`, `filt_id`, `trash`) and response elements (`filters`,
`sort`, the `roles` pagination wrapper, `role_count`, plus `owner`/`permissions`/`user_tags` on each
`role`), fixes two `bool`→`GMPBool` lexical-boolean findings, and closes all 11 actionable `MISSING`, 2
`TYPE`, and 6 suppressed `OMITEMPTY` findings the family carried — confirmed via
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidateRole*` gate pair, which now pins "before" and "after"
as the same source (the same post-swap pattern `note` and `override` use). `get_roles` moves from 13
findings to `conformant` in `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md`. The `roles`/`role` wrapper-vs-list
naming split repeats the precedent already accepted for `note` and `override`; the one call-site break was
`GetRolesResponse.Roles` → `.Role`, fixed in `client/client_test.go` and `client/client_role_test.go` (no
non-test callers — `client.GetRoles` is an opaque pass-through, same as override). `role` has zero
SCHEMA-DEFECT findings (unlike `note`/`override`'s two upstream duplicate-element bugs each), so
`schema.Comparer` now reports nothing at all for the family — but that measures conformance to what the
schema's `<pattern>` declares, not byte-for-byte fidelity to the wire. One further gap review surfaced,
invisible to Comparer for that reason: `role_count`'s declared `<pattern>` is only
`<e>filtered</e><e>page</e>`, with no text token anywhere in it (contrast `sort`, whose pattern literally
contains the bareword `text`, which `Node.Chardata` does detect, reflected in `RoleSort.Value`) — yet both
of `command_get_roles.xml`'s own bundled examples, and a live capture, send a bare total ahead of those two
children (`1` bundled, `6` live). The generated `RoleCount` silently drops it on decode, with no error.
This is the same class of gap as `create_override`'s `result`-as-text loss above (declared pattern and the
schema's own example disagree), not a new one, and per the same generate-only-what-the-schema-declares
principle it is accepted and documented rather than patched into `RoleCount` as a field the schema never
asked for. Pinned by `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestRoleCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` against
both fixtures — the test decodes each fixture twice, once into a probe with a `,chardata` field to prove
the wire really carries the total, once into the exact generated shape to prove it has nowhere to put it.
This is also visible directly in the regenerated `internal/gmptest/testdata/{live,upstream}/get_roles/
001_fidelity.txt`, though the two fixtures aren't identical: the **live** fixture drops exactly the two
accepted chardata losses (`role_count/#text`, `sort/field/#text`), down from 8 before adoption. The
**upstream** fixture drops those same two plus two unrelated `<truncated>` placeholder elements
(`/get_roles_response/truncated` and `/get_roles_response/filters/keywords/truncated` — literal
`@IF_..._CVSS3_RATINGS@`-style template markers left in gvmd's own bundled example, not a modelling gap),
and separately *adds* three elements the round trip invents that the sparse upstream example never
included (`role/owner`, `role/owner/name`, `role/permissions` — required-but-omitted-from-the-example
fields the generated `Role` unconditionally emits on re-encode, the same "inert on decode" OMITEMPTY
category documented elsewhere in this file). Added `internal/gmptest/cases_role.go` with three constructed
`get_roles` request cases (`filter_and_trash_true`, `by_id`, `filter_and_trash_false`) exercising
`RoleID`/`Filter`/`FiltID` and proving `Trash`'s true/false/unset states round-trip distinctly — in
particular that `Trash: commands.Bool(false)` re-emits `trash="0"` rather than being dropped, matching the
precedent already set for `get_credentials`/`get_filters`/`get_groups`.

**`group` adopted:** went through the same review-only stage `role` did — added to
`internal/codegen/manifest.json` as `review_only`, schema snapshots and golden output extracted and
committed (`internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_group,get_groups,modify_group}.*`), generated
API and oracle deltas reviewed — then `review_only` was dropped and `hack/generate.sh` written into
`commands/`. `modify_group` generates byte-identical to the hand-written original (already fully
conformant, so nothing to review there beyond confirming the byte-identity). `create_group` keeps its one
pre-existing SCHEMA-DEFECT (`/create_group/specials/full`, `empty-pattern` — one of 44 such sites across 23
commands, the same generic upstream-schema class already carried by `note`/`override`, not new);
`schema.Comparer` skips structural comparison inside an empty-pattern node entirely, so `GroupSpecials.Full`
losing its `omitempty` tag produces no finding either way. `get_groups` carried far fewer findings than
`get_roles` did before its own adoption (3 vs. 13), because the hand-written `GetGroups`/`Group` types had
already independently converged on most of the schema's shape (`owner`/`permissions`/`user_tags`, filter
attributes); generation closes what remained — the 2 `TYPE` findings (`Group.Writable`/`Group.InUse`,
`bool`→`GMPBool`, the same lexical-boolean fix every other family needed) and the 1 suppressed `OMITEMPTY`
finding (`GroupFilters.Name` gains `omitempty`, tracking the schema's optional `<name>`) — confirmed via
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidateGroup*` gate pair, which now pins "before" and "after"
as the same source (the same post-swap pattern `note`, `override`, and `role` use). `get_groups` moves from
3 findings to `conformant` in `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md`. The `groups`/`group`
wrapper-vs-list naming split repeats the precedent already accepted for `note`/`override`/`role`; the one
call-site break was `GetGroupsResponse.Groups` → `.Group`, fixed in `client/client_test.go` and
`client/client_group_test.go` (no non-test callers — `client.GetGroups` is an opaque pass-through, same as
role and override). One further gap review surfaced, invisible to `schema.Comparer` for the same reason as
`role_count`: `group_count`'s declared `<pattern>` is only `<e>filtered</e><e>page</e>`, with no text token
anywhere in it, and `field` (inside `sort`) is likewise `<e>order</e>` with no text token — yet both
`command_get_groups.xml`'s own bundled example and a live capture send a bare total/name ahead of those
children. The generated `GroupCount`/`GroupField` silently drop them on decode, with no error — the same
class of gap as `create_override`'s `result`-as-text loss and `role_count`'s identical shape, accepted and
documented rather than patched in as fields the schema never asked for. Pinned by
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestGroupCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` against both fixtures,
following the same decode-twice-into-two-shapes proof `TestRoleCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` uses. The
`sort/field` half was pinned the same way by the now-removed `TestGroupSortFieldNameIsAnAcceptedLoss`,
until T-18a's chardata relocation closed it (see T-18a below). This is also visible
directly in the regenerated `internal/gmptest/testdata/{live,upstream}/get_groups/*_fidelity.txt`: all
three live fixtures and the upstream fixture drop their `changed=2`/`changed=0` boolean mismatches (the
`Writable`/`InUse` `"0"`/`"1"` → `"false"`/`"true"` re-encodes are now byte-identical), leaving only the two
accepted `dropped` chardata losses per fixture (`group_count/#text`, `sort/field/#text`, plus the upstream
fixture's unrelated `<truncated>` placeholder descendants) and, on the upstream fixture, three `added`
elements the round trip invents that the sparse example never included (`group/owner`, `group/owner/name`,
`group/permissions` — the same "inert on decode" OMITEMPTY category `role`'s adoption also produced).

**`tag` adopted:** went through the same review-only stage `group` did — added to
`internal/codegen/manifest.json` as `review_only`, schema snapshots and golden output extracted and
committed (`internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_tag,get_tags,modify_tag,tag_shared}.*`),
generated API and oracle deltas reviewed — then `review_only` was dropped and `hack/generate.sh` written
into `commands/`. Unlike `role`/`group`, `tag_shared.go` is actually emitted: `create_tag` and `modify_tag`
share a request-side `TagResource{ID string}`, a narrower shape than the hand-written `TagResource` (which
also carried `Type`/`Name`/`Trash` for the response side, since the hand-written code conflated the
request- and response-side `resource` shapes the schema keeps distinct). Both `create_tag` and `modify_tag`
close all 3 of their EXTRA findings (`resources/resource`'s undeclared `name`/`trash`/`type`) via this
narrower shared type, and their OMITEMPTY findings close too (the suppressed `resources/resource/@id` keeps
its `omitempty`; `create_tag`'s actionable one on `.../resources` closes because the hand-written
`*CreateTagResources` becomes non-pointer `TagResources`). `modify_tag`'s one MISSING finding
(`resources/@action`) closes via a new `Action` field on `ModifyTagResources`. The hand-written
`CreateTagResources` type name is gone entirely, replaced by the generated `TagResources`; the one call-site
break was `commands.CreateTagResources{Type, Resources}` → `commands.TagResources{Type, Resource}` (the
field is singular, following the schema's repeated-element convention), fixed in
`internal/gmptest/cases_tag.go`, `internal/gmptest/capture_sequence_test.go`, `client/client_tag_test.go`,
and `client/client_test.go`'s mock — `client.CreateTag`/`client.ModifyTag`/`client.GetTags` are opaque
pass-throughs and needed no change.

`get_tags` carried far more findings than `create_tag`/`modify_tag` (19 actionable across 5 categories, 2
suppressed), because none of `filters`/`sort`/`tags`/`tag_count`, or a proper `tag/resources` wrapper,
existed on the hand-written `GetTagsResponse`/`Tag` types, and three request filter attributes (`filt_id`,
`trash`, `names_only`) were missing entirely. Generation closes all 8 MISSING findings by adding those
request attributes and response elements; both EXTRA findings close too — the hand-written flattened
`Tag.Resources []commands.TagResource` becomes a proper `*GetTagsResources` wrapper with its own
`TagResourcesResource{ID,Name,Trash,Permissions}` (a strictly response-shaped type, distinct from the
request-side shared `TagResource`), and the hand-written `GetTagsResponse.Truncated string` field — which
has no schema counterpart at all — is dropped entirely rather than restructured, closing its EXTRA finding
by removal, unlike `group`/`role` where an analogous gap closed by adding structure instead. `get_tags`'s
four boolean fields (`Writable`/`InUse`/`Active`/`Orphan`) each carried both a CARDINALITY finding (schema
optional, hand-written `bool` can't express absence) and a TYPE finding (lexical boolean vs. Go `bool`)
simultaneously — a pattern not seen in `role`/`group`, where the boolean findings were TYPE-only — and both
close together via a single fix: `*commands.GMPBool` pointers, via the `commands.Bool()` helper at every
call site (`client/client_test.go`'s mock; `internal/gmptest/cases_tag.go` already used it for `Active`).
Both suppressed OMITEMPTY findings close too (`tag/@id` and `tag/permissions` both gain `omitempty`,
tracking the schema's optional declarations). One pre-existing SCHEMA-DEFECT remains untouched
(`/get_tags_response/tag/resources/resource/permissions`, `empty-pattern` — the same generic upstream-schema
class already carried by `note`/`override`/`create_group`, not new); confirmed via
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidateTag*` gate pair, which now pins "before" and "after" as
the same source (the same post-swap pattern `note`, `override`, `role`, and `group` use). `get_tags` moves
from 19 actionable findings to just the one SCHEMA-DEFECT in `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md`. The
`tags`/`tag` wrapper-vs-list naming split repeats the precedent already accepted for
`note`/`override`/`role`/`group`; the one call-site break was `GetTagsResponse.Tags` → `.Tag`, fixed in
`client/client_tag_test.go` and `client/client_test.go`.

One further gap review surfaced, invisible to `schema.Comparer` for the same reason as `role_count`/
`group_count`: `tag_count`'s declared `<pattern>` is only `<e>filtered</e><e>page</e>`, with no text token
anywhere in it, and `field` (inside `sort`) is likewise `<e>order</e>` with no text token — yet all three
live captures send a bare total/name ahead of those children (no bundled upstream example exists for
`get_tags`, unlike `role`/`group`). The generated `TagCount`/`TagField` silently drop them on decode, with
no error — the same class of gap as `create_override`'s `result`-as-text loss and
`role_count`/`group_count`'s identical shape, accepted and documented rather than patched in as fields the
schema never asked for. Pinned by `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestTagCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss`
against all three live fixtures — 001 and 002 send a bare "0" (proving the loss is real evidence even at a
zero total, not just whitespace), 003 sends "1" — following the same decode-twice-into-two-shapes proof
`TestRoleCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` uses. The `sort/field` half was pinned the same way by the
now-removed `TestTagSortFieldNameIsAnAcceptedLoss`, until T-18a's chardata relocation closed it (see T-18a
below). This is also visible
directly in the regenerated `internal/gmptest/testdata/{live,upstream}/get_tags/*_fidelity.txt`: all three
live fixtures and the upstream fixture drop their boolean `changed` mismatches (the
`Writable`/`InUse`/`Active` `"0"`/`"1"` → `"false"`/`"true"` re-encodes are now byte-identical), leaving only
the accepted `dropped` chardata losses (`tag_count/#text`, `sort/field/#text`) and, on the upstream fixture,
the same "inert on decode" `added` elements pattern `role`/`group`'s adoption also produced. Separately,
`create_tag`'s own two request-fidelity fixtures regress, for two distinct and unrelated reasons:

- live/001 (`dropped=3`): this fixture's recorded request already carries `<resources><resource
  id="…"><type></type><name></name><trash>false</trash></resource><type>target</type></resources>` — empty
  `type`/`name` children plus a `trash` child under `<resource>`, a shape the narrower generated
  `TagResource{ID}` doesn't have room for and so drops on round-trip. This is not new protocol evidence: it is the *old*
  hand-written `TagResource` (which carried those same three fields) emitting them itself when
  `internal/gmptest/capture_sequence_test.go`'s `lifecycleTag` recorded this fixture against a live gvmd
  22.7, before tag's adoption. gvmd's 201 response shows it tolerated the extra empty elements, which is
  useful evidence on its own — but it is evidence of tolerance for historical client output, not evidence
  that `type`/`name`/`trash` belong on `<resource>` at all; the schema never declared them there. The
  narrower generated type is correct per-schema, and the fixture now correctly reports that fidelity loss
  instead of silently agreeing with a shape only the old client ever produced.
- upstream/001 (`dropped=1`, `added=2`, up from `added=0`): this is a genuine schema/example mismatch, not
  a generator regression. The upstream doc example puts `<resource id="…"><type>target</type></resource>`
  directly under `<create_tag>`, with no `<resources>` wrapper at all — a pre-wrapper GMP shape the current
  schema no longer describes (`resources` is a required wrapper with its own `type` and repeated
  `resource`). The bare `<resource>` was already unrecognized and dropped before this adoption; now that
  `Resources` is a required, always-marshaled value, re-encoding also emits an empty `<resources>` wrapper
  and its required `<type>` child that have no counterpart in the stale example, which is where the two new
  `added` paths come from.

Both are accepted as documenting real discrepancies rather than being patched over. Separately, the
`TestRequestGolden` cases in `internal/gmptest/cases_tag.go` for `active_false`/`active_unset` originally
left `Resources` at its zero value, which — now that it's required — produced a golden with a meaningless
empty `<resources><type></type></resources>`. Since the schema requires resources, the fix was to give all
three `create_tag` cases (`active_true`/`active_false`/`active_unset`) the same populated `Resources` value
and vary only `Active`, matching what those cases actually mean to test.

**`schedule` adopted:** the next family after `tag` per the ranking method above (score 17, ahead of
`alert`'s 21 and `filter`/`tls_certificate`'s 23 each), dropped from `internal/codegen/manifest.json`'s
`review_only` set and swapped into `commands/{create_schedule,get_schedules,modify_schedule}.go` via
`hack/generate.sh`. `create_schedule` and `modify_schedule` were already fully conformant at the review-only
stage and generated byte-identical to the hand-written originals (beyond the generated-file header and doc
comments), so adoption changed nothing structural in either. `create_schedule`/`modify_schedule`'s
`icalendar` naming was fixed a stage earlier, at review-only: the generator's `initialisms` table didn't
originally include it, so it first rendered `ICalendar` as `Icalendar` (single capital) where the
hand-written field capitalizes both letters — `schema.Comparer` can't see this, since it compares field
shapes, not identifier spelling — so that stage added `"icalendar": "ICalendar"` to `initialisms` (RFC
5545's own casing, not an acronym, but the same fixed-spelling mechanism) rather than deferring it.
`internal/codegen/manifest_test.go`'s `TestScheduleICalendarNamingIsStable` pins both the exact spelling and
that generating `schedule` alongside every other family in the manifest cannot change it (`initialisms` is a
package-level table with no per-family state); its doc comment now says "the adopted `commands/...`" rather
than describing a future adoption.

`get_schedules` carried the family's entire actionable/suppressed load (1 EXTRA, 2 TYPE, 1 SCHEMA-DEFECT
actionable; 17 suppressed OMITEMPTY), confirmed via `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s
`TestValidateSchedule*` gate pair. Adoption closed the EXTRA finding by dropping `ScheduleField`'s
hand-written `,chardata` field (the schema's `<field>` pattern never declares text there); closed both TYPE
findings via `Writable`/`InUse` becoming `commands.GMPBool` (the same lexical-boolean fix every other family
needed); and closed all 17 suppressed OMITEMPTY findings by making `Filters`/`Sort`/`Schedules`/
`ScheduleCount` and their required children non-pointer, non-omitempty values, while the optional `<tasks>`
wrapper became a pointer (`*ScheduleTasks`) — the hand-written code had instead used a flattened
`xml:"tasks>task,omitempty"` path selector, which is wire-compatible but doesn't let `schema.Comparer` check
cardinality at the `tasks` level itself the way an explicit wrapper does; `Permissions` got the same
treatment (`*SchedulePermissions{Permission []SchedulePermission}` replacing
`xml:"permissions>permission,omitempty"`). `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s "before" and "after" gates
for `get_schedules` now agree (`SCHEMA-DEFECT: 1` actionable, nothing suppressed) — the same convergence
every other family's adoption produced, since `commands/` is now the generator's own output. The one
remaining SCHEMA-DEFECT (`/get_schedules_response/schedule/tasks/task/permissions`, `empty-pattern`) stays
untouched — it is copied from `cmd.Defects` before any Go-type comparison runs, so no generated output can
move it, and it is the same generic upstream-schema class already carried by a dozen other commands, not new
here.

The naming collision flagged at review-only resolved exactly as predicted, the same way it did for
`note`/`override`/`role`/`group`/`tag`: the wrapper element took the `Schedules` name and the repeated
`<schedule>` child took `Schedule`, so `GetSchedulesResponse.Schedules []Schedule` became
`GetSchedulesResponse.Schedule []Schedule` with `Schedules` re-purposed as the pagination-wrapper type
(`Schedules{Start, Max}`, renamed from the hand-written `ScheduleInfo`) — the two call sites this touched,
`client/client_test.go` and `client/client_schedule_test.go`'s `TestGetSchedules`, were updated to match.
`ScheduleUserTag` was likewise renamed to `ScheduleTag` for the same reason `user_tags`' child element name
wins over the hand-written singular-suffix convention in every other adopted family.

Two further gaps review surfaced remain true post-adoption, invisible to `schema.Comparer` for the same
reason as `role_count`/`group_count`/`tag_count` and their `sort/field` counterparts: `schedule_count`'s
declared `<pattern>` is only `<e>filtered</e><e>page</e>`, with no text token anywhere in it, and `field`
(inside `sort`) is likewise `<e>order</e>` with no text token — yet live captures under
`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_schedules` send a bare total/name ahead of those children. Pinned by
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestScheduleCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` against the live
fixtures, following the same decode-twice-into-two-shapes proof `TestRoleCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss`
uses; it still passes unchanged, since the generated shape it pins against didn't change at adoption. The
`sort/field` half was pinned the same way by the now-removed `TestScheduleSortFieldNameIsAnAcceptedLoss`,
until T-18a's chardata relocation closed it (see T-18a below). A third gap closed no finding and
needed no test, but is worth naming: `get_schedules`'s request-side `Trash`/`Tasks` attrs were
schema-declared booleans but hand-written as plain `string`, not `bool` — and `schema.Comparer`'s
`checkType` only raises a TYPE finding when the Go field's base type is `bool`, never when it is already
`string` (a blind spot in the oracle, not a defect in `schedule`). Adoption still improved both to `*GMPBool`
for typed, schema-correct wire encoding; since no finding was ever raised for the `string` case, this
improvement moved no number in `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md`. Because none of `get_schedules`'s
captured request fixtures exercise `trash` or `tasks`, `internal/gmptest/testdata/REQUEST-FIDELITY.txt` has
no fixture to show the `string` -> `*GMPBool` change either; `internal/gmptest/cases_schedule.go` adds
curated `TestRequestGolden` coverage instead (`filter_and_trash_true`/`filter_and_trash_false`/`by_id` for
`Trash`, `tasks_true`/`tasks_false` for `Tasks`), the same follow-up pattern `role`'s adoption used for its
own newly-`*bool`-typed fields.

**`alert` adopted:** the next family after `schedule` per the ranking method above (score 21, ahead of
`filter`/`tls_certificate`'s 23 each and `permission`'s 31), dropped from
`internal/codegen/manifest.json`'s `review_only` set and swapped into
`commands/{create_alert,get_alerts,modify_alert}.go` (plus the new `commands/alert_shared.go`) via
`hack/generate.sh`. `create_alert` closes its one suppressed OMITEMPTY finding (`Filter` becomes a
required, non-pointer value instead of `*AlertFilter,omitempty` — a request-side over-constraint, the same
class `create_override`/`create_tag` already had) and is otherwise byte-identical. The
`MD5Fingerprint`/`SHA256Fingerprint` initialisms fix and `TestAlertFingerprintNamingIsStable` were already
landed a stage earlier, at review-only, the same way `schedule`'s `icalendar` spelling was fixed before its
own adoption rather than deferred; its doc comment now says alert's adoption "must not carry" an
inconsistent public API, past tense.

`modify_alert` was already fully conformant and stays conformant. The review-only doc had left one question
open before adoption could proceed: whether `Name`/`Comment`'s hand-written `*string,omitempty` — which can
marshal an explicit `&""` as `<comment></comment>`, distinct from omitting the element — encodes a real
"leave unchanged" vs. "clear the value" distinction gvmd's modify commands rely on, since a first draft of
this adoption had mechanically flattened both fields to a plain `string` (`schema.Comparer` has no category
for pointer-vs-value strings, so that draft moved no number in `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md`,
which is what let it look like a safe, like-for-like change). That draft's reasoning — that every
already-adopted `modify_X` had made the identical choice with no reported behavioral gap — turned out to be
incomplete rather than reassuring: `modify_role`/`modify_group`/`modify_tag`/`modify_schedule` were never
`*string` even before T-9 (confirmed via `git show <pre-adoption commit>^:commands/modify_X.go` for all
four), so their bare `string` fields were never evidence that removing a pointer is safe — they simply never
had one to remove. `alert` is the only family this question is live for.

Rather than defer the question again, the live experiment the review-only doc had proposed was run: a
throwaway program authenticated against a real gvmd 26.36.1 (Greenbone Community Edition) instance, created
an alert, and used `gmp.Connection.RawXML` to send `<modify_alert alert_id="...">` once with `<comment>`
omitted and once with `<comment></comment>` explicit (and the same pair for `<name>`), reading the alert
back via `GetAlerts` after each. The result was unambiguous: omitting the element leaves the stored value
unchanged; sending it explicitly empty clears it to `""`. `command_modify_alert.xml` documents no such
distinction, but gvmd's actual behavior draws it anyway, and a bare `string` with `,omitempty` can only ever
send the first form — Go's `encoding/xml` omits a zero-value string unconditionally under `omitempty`, with
no way to ask for the second. Flattening the field would have silently made "clear this alert's comment" an
inexpressible request.

`internal/codegen/overrides.go` now has `PointerOverrides`, a second, narrower table alongside
`TypeOverrides`: keyed by `"<command>.<field>"` rather than by schema type name, and consulted only for a
command's own top-level fields (`generate.go`'s `fieldOf` takes the owning command's name for exactly this,
and passes `""` for a nested struct's fields, which have no single owner for the table to key on). It has
two entries, `modify_alert.name` and `modify_alert.comment`, each documented with this experiment.
`pointerScalars`' generator-wide default — "an empty string and an absent element mean the same thing" — is
now known to be false in at least this one case; the table is deliberately not widened to every optional
string on every `modify_*` command on the strength of one family's result. Whether `modify_role`,
`modify_group`, `modify_tag`, and `modify_schedule` share the same gap is a real, still-open question, but
answering it needs the same kind of live evidence gathered here, not an inference from `alert`, and stays
tracked as independent follow-up work rather than blocking or silently piggybacking on this adoption.
`commands/modify_alert.go` regenerated with `Name`/`Comment` back to `*string,omitempty`, byte-identical to
what a hand-maintained pointer version would have looked like, and
`internal/gmptest/testdata/requests/modify_alert/{name_comment_unset,name_comment_empty,name_comment_set}.xml`
now pin all three wire shapes (`cases_alert.go`) so this distinction cannot silently collapse again.

`get_alerts` carried the family's entire actionable/suppressed load (2 MISSING, 4 TYPE, 2 SCHEMA-DEFECT
actionable; 7 suppressed OMITEMPTY), confirmed via `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s
`TestValidateAlert*` gate pair. Adoption closed both MISSING findings by modeling `method/data/credential`
and `method/data/certificate_info` as `AlertCredential`/`AlertCertificateInfo` on a get_alerts-specific
`AlertMethodData`. The shared `AlertData` in `alert_shared.go` (just `name`/text) stays exactly that shape
and keeps being reused by `condition`/`event` across all three commands, including `get_alerts`, since only
`method`'s `<data>` children need the extra fields; only `method` itself gets a get_alerts-specific type
(`GetAlertsMethod`, alongside the shared `AlertMethod` used by `create_alert`/`modify_alert`), for the same
family-local-disambiguation reason `AlertFilter` (shared, an ID-only reference type used by
`create_alert`/`modify_alert`) and `GetAlertsFilter` (the response's richer `id`/`name`/`trash`/
`permissions` shape) stay two separate types instead of merging or silently picking one — the two
`<filter>` elements across the family are structurally different. Adoption closed all 4 TYPE findings via
`InUse`/`Writable`/`Active`/`filter.Trash` becoming `commands.GMPBool` (the same lexical-boolean fix every
other family needed, here from a hand-written `*bool` rather than bare `bool`); and closed all 7 suppressed
OMITEMPTY findings by making `Filters`/`Sort`/`Alerts`/`AlertCount` and their required children
non-pointer, non-omitempty values, while the optional `<user_tags>` and `<tasks>` wrappers became pointers
— the hand-written code had instead used a flattened `xml:"tasks>task,omitempty"` path selector for tasks
and a plain `[]AlertPermission` for permissions with no wrapper at all; both got an explicit wrapper type
(`*AlertTasks{Task []AlertTask}`, `AlertPermissions{Permission []AlertPermission}`) for the same
cardinality-visibility reason `schedule`'s and `tag`'s permissions/tasks wrappers did.
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s "before" and "after" gates for `get_alerts` now agree (`SCHEMA-DEFECT:
2` actionable, nothing suppressed), the same convergence every other family's adoption produced, since
`commands/` is now the generator's own output. The two remaining SCHEMA-DEFECT findings
(`/get_alerts_response/alert/filter/permissions` and `/get_alerts_response/alert/tasks/task/permissions`,
both `empty-pattern`) stay untouched — they are copied from `cmd.Defects` before any Go-type comparison
runs, so no generated output can move them, and they are the same generic upstream-schema class already
carried by a dozen other commands, not new here.

The naming collision flagged at review-only resolved exactly as predicted, the same way it did for
`note`/`override`/`role`/`group`/`tag`/`schedule`: the wrapper element took the `Alerts` name (renamed from
the hand-written `AlertInfo`) and the repeated `<alert>` child took `Alert`, so
`GetAlertsResponse.Alerts []Alert` became `GetAlertsResponse.Alert []Alert`; `AlertUserTag` was likewise
renamed to `AlertTag` for the same reason `user_tags`'s child element name wins in every other adopted
family. The three call sites this touched — `client/client_alert_test.go`'s `TestCreateAlert`/
`TestModifyAlert`, `internal/gmptest/capture_sequence_test.go`'s `lifecycleAlert`, and
`internal/gmptest/cases_alert.go` — were updated to match: `AlertCondition`/`AlertEvent`/`AlertMethod`/
`AlertData` literals switched their chardata field from `Text` to `Value`; `CreateAlert.Filter` literals
dropped the `&` now that the field is a value, not a pointer, and `TestCreateAlert` plus
`cases_alert.go`'s `active_false`/`active_unset` `TestRequestGolden` cases — which previously left `Filter`
unset entirely — were given the same populated `AlertFilter{ID: ...}` value used by `active_true`, the same
follow-up `create_tag`'s `Resources` field needed, so the required field's request goldens exercise a real
ID instead of the zero-value `<filter id=""></filter>` shape; `TestModifyAlert`'s `name`/`comment` locals and
their `&name`/`&comment` fields were unaffected, since `Name`/`Comment` stayed `*string` (see the
`PointerOverrides` discussion above); and `ModifyAlert.Filter` kept its pointer since `modify_alert`'s
`<filter>` stayed optional.

Two further gaps review surfaced remain true post-adoption, invisible to `schema.Comparer` for the same
reason as `role_count`/`group_count`/`tag_count`/`schedule_count` and their `sort/field` counterparts:
`alert_count`'s declared `<pattern>` is only `<e>filtered</e><e>page</e>`, with no text token anywhere in
it, and `field` (inside `sort`) is likewise `<e>order</e>` with no text token — yet live captures under
`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_alerts` send a bare total/name ahead of those children. Pinned by
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestAlertCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` against the live fixtures,
following the same decode-twice-into-two-shapes proof `TestScheduleCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` uses;
it still passes unchanged, since the generated shape it pins against didn't change at adoption. The
`sort/field` half was pinned the same way by the now-removed `TestAlertSortFieldNameIsAnAcceptedLoss`,
until T-18a's chardata relocation closed it (see T-18a below).

**`filter` adopted:** the next family after `alert` per the ranking method above (score 23, tied with
`tls_certificate` and ahead of `permission`'s 31; no tie-break criterion in the ranking method distinguishes
the two, so `filter` was picked over `tls_certificate` as the reasonable default), dropped from
`internal/codegen/manifest.json`'s `review_only` set and swapped into
`commands/{create_filter,get_filters,modify_filter}.go` via `hack/generate.sh` (no `filter_shared.go` is
actually emitted — the family has no shared nested type, same as `role`/`group`/`schedule`). `create_filter`
is fully conformant and generates byte-identical to the hand-written original. Review at the review-only
stage had raised two findings against a first draft, both resolved before adoption rather than deferred: the
`filters`/`filters2` schema-parsing gap, and an unsubstantiated "no `PointerOverrides` entry needed" claim
for `modify_filter`.

`command_get_filters.xml` is the only schema file in the entire corpus that disambiguates two same-named
`<ele>` definitions using `<id>` rather than relying on `<name>` alone: it declares one `<filters>` shaped
for search-term echo (`id`/`term`/`keywords`) and a second, `<id>filters2</id>`-tagged `<filters>` shaped for
pagination (`start`/`max` attributes only), intending the response `<pattern>`'s `<e>filters2</e>` reference
to resolve to the second definition despite both sharing the wire tag `<filters>`. A first draft of this
review left `<id>` unparsed and treated the resulting `DefectDuplicateEle`/`DefectDanglingRef` pair as an
accepted, pre-existing upstream schema-authoring defect to document around — the same kind of inference
`alert`'s `modify_alert` question had already discredited once in this review. That was wrong: `<id>` is a
real, supported disambiguation mechanism, not an authoring mistake, and it is now implemented.
`internal/schema/parse.go`'s `rawEle` gained an `ID string` field (tag `xml:"id"`); `env.scope()` keys each
definition by its `<id>` when present, falling back to `<name>` otherwise (a no-op for every other def in the
corpus, since nothing else uses `<id>`); and `env.child()` sets the resolved node's `Name` from the
definition's own `<name>` rather than the lookup token, so a node reached via the disambiguating token
(`filters2`) still carries the real wire name (`filters`). `internal/schema/parse_test.go` adds coverage for
the `<id>`-disambiguated pair (no defects, both nodes resolve to `Name == "filters"` with their own distinct
children), a regression guard that an `<id>`-less duplicate `<ele>` still raises `DefectDuplicateEle`, and the
ordinary single-definition case. This closes 2 of `get_filters`'s 3 SCHEMA-DEFECT findings (down to 1,
`/get_filters_response/filter/alerts/alert/permissions`, the same generic `empty-pattern` class already
carried by a dozen other commands) and is a genuine, corpus-wide correctness improvement, independent of
`filter` adoption, reflected in the regenerated `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md`.

Correctly parsing `<id>` only gets the schema side right; representing two same-named schema children in one
Go struct still needed a codegen mechanism, since `encoding/xml` rejects two struct fields sharing one
explicit tag in both directions ("field X with tag filters conflicts with field Y with tag filters"), and the
only legal workaround — tagging the second field `xml:"-"` and driving it from a custom
`UnmarshalXML`/`MarshalXML` — has to be built, not declared. The direction chosen here was fully generic
auto-synthesis over a narrow per-case override: `internal/codegen/generate.go`'s `emitFields` now tracks wire
names seen among a struct's `KindElement` children and flags `collided = true` on a second occurrence,
tagging that field `xml:"-"`; `emitRequest`/`emitResponse`/`emitStruct` then call `emitCollisionMethods`,
which mechanically emits an `UnmarshalXML` that counts occurrences of the colliding wire name and routes the
Nth occurrence to the Nth field in declaration order, and a mirroring `MarshalXML` that encodes each field
with its own explicit `EncodeElement` call (bypassing the reflection-based tag lookup that rejects the
duplicate). Routing by occurrence order rather than by sniffing shape is safe because `<pattern>` is a
sequence and gvmd emits colliding elements in schema-declared order — confirmed on
`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_filters/001_response.xml`, whose `<filters id="">...</filters>` precedes
`<filters start="1" max="1000"/>`, matching the schema's declaration order exactly. `Generate()`'s public
signature is unchanged; no override table or hand-written support file was added. This changes exactly one
golden file in the whole corpus (`get_filters.go`, `Filters2` now `GetFiltersFilters` instead of a bare,
always-empty `string`) — every other family's `collided` stays `false`, verified by the full golden/compile
suite. `TestGetFiltersCollisionRoundTrips` (`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`) is the round-trip proof this
mechanism needed: it decodes the real fixture above into the generated `GetFiltersResponse` in a scratch
module, then re-marshals and re-decodes it, asserting both round trips land on `Filters.Term ==
"first=1 rows=10 sort=name"` with 3 keywords and `Filters2 == {Start: 1, Max: 1000}` — the exact fixture
values — proving the collision codec actually separates the two sibling `<filters>` elements correctly in
both directions.

A second review pass on the collision codec itself raised four more findings, three of them real bugs in the
first draft's `emitCollisionMethods`/`emitCollisionUnmarshal`/`emitCollisionMarshal`:

1. ~~Occurrence-order dispatch was not provably safe as a generic mechanism~~ — an intermediate fix required
   every collision member to be `schema.CardRequired`, which prevented omission-driven index shifting but did
   not satisfy fail-closed routing: a *reordered* document (both members present, but the pagination-shaped
   `<filters>` arriving first) would still be silently mis-routed, since dispatch never looked at the elements
   themselves. A third pass replaced occurrence-order dispatch entirely with **attribute-signature dispatch**.
   `collisionDiscriminators` computes, per collision member, the set of start-tag attribute names that appear
   on that member and no sibling (for `get_filters`: `id` for the search-metadata `<filters>`, `start`/`max`
   for the pagination one) and fails generation if a member has none (`"no attribute that uniquely identifies
   it"`, guarded by `TestCollisionRequiresAttributeDiscriminators`). The generated code gets one
   `classify<Type><Name>(attrs []xml.Attr) (int, error)` function per collision group, called from
   `UnmarshalXML` on every occurrence: it reports which member's signature the actual attributes match, and
   errors on zero matches (`"unrecognized ... element: no known variant's attributes are present"`) or more
   than one (`"ambiguous ... element: attributes match more than one known variant"`). The old occurrence
   counter is now a `seen[]bool` slice keyed by the classified index, so "unexpected extra occurrence" is still
   caught, but keyed off identity rather than arrival order. `TestGetFiltersCollisionRoundTrips` exercises the
   full matrix this implies: normal order (the real fixture), reversed order, each signature alone with the
   other member omitted, an unrecognized signature, and an ambiguous one — ten scenarios in total against one
   `go run` scratch program.
2. **`UnmarshalXML` never reset the receiver** — `*r = GetFiltersResponse{}` is now the method's first
   statement (before `r.XMLName = start.Name`), so decoding into an already-populated value can't leave stale
   data from whatever it held before. Proved by decoding two different fixture documents into the same
   variable and checking nothing survives from the first.
3. **`MarshalXML` unconditionally copied `r.XMLName` into `start.Name`**, so marshaling a zero-value
   `GetFiltersResponse{}` produced an empty root name and `encoding/xml` refused to encode it at all
   (`xml: start tag with no name`) — confirmed with a throwaway harness probing `encoding/xml`'s `defaultStart`
   fallback. `MarshalXML` now falls back to the literal schema root name (`get_filters_response`) when
   `start.Name.Local` comes back empty. `TestGetFiltersCollisionRoundTrips` gained a zero-value marshal
   assertion for this.
4. ~~The conformance oracle's MISSING/EXTRA acceptance hides real Go structure behind a comparer blind
   spot~~ — an intermediate response documented the gap in `validate_test.go`'s comments rather than closing
   it, which is not a fix. The actual fix is a private struct tag: a collision-routed field keeps its
   generation-mandated `xml:"-"` (the only tag `go vet`'s structtag analyzer accepts on a duplicate wire name)
   but also carries `gmpwire:"filters"` — a key `go vet` does not inspect, since its static check is scoped to
   the `"xml"` key specifically. `internal/schema/gomodel.go`'s `addField` now treats `xml:"-"` plus a
   non-empty `gmpwire` tag as visible under the `gmpwire` name instead of invisible (a field with a genuine
   `xml:"-"` and no `gmpwire` tag is unaffected); this needed no runtime coupling in either direction —
   `commands/*.go` gains no method, and `internal/schema` imports no concrete `commands` type, since a struct
   tag is read by name the same way the existing `"xml"` tag already is. That alone was not sufficient:
   `internal/schema/conform.go`'s `walk` matched schema children and Go fields by a single-valued
   `map[string]*GoNode` keyed on wire name, so both of the schema's `filters` occurrences looked up the same
   node. `walk` now keeps an occurrence-count cursor per name and pairs the schema's Nth child of a given wire
   name with the Go side's Nth field of that name, in declaration order — the same generalization
   `generate.go`'s collision codec already relies on, applied to the comparer instead. Together these close the
   gap completely: `get_filters`'s "after" gate now reports only the pre-existing `SCHEMA-DEFECT` finding, zero
   MISSING, zero EXTRA. The comparer fix is corpus-wide, not `get_filters`-specific, and it surfaced a smaller
   version of the same bug already latent in the "before" (hand-written) gate: the old single-valued lookup
   was *also* pairing the schema's second `filters` child against the wrong Go node there, producing spurious
   findings on top of the two real ones (missing pagination node, extra misnamed `Filters2` field). Fixed,
   `get_filters`'s before-gate MISSING/EXTRA drop from 2/5 to the real 1/1; a couple of other commands
   elsewhere in the 122-command corpus that happen to declare their own repeated wire names (`get_targets`,
   `get_tasks` — neither touched by T-9 yet) shift by a handful of findings each for the same reason, visible
   only in `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md`'s regenerated corpus-wide totals; no already-adopted
   family's pinned `internal/codegen/validate_test.go` expectations changed. `internal/codegen/generate_test.go`'s
   `TestGetFiltersCollisionRoundTrips` and `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s
   `TestValidateFilterGeneratedMatchesOrImproves`/`TestValidateFilterBeforeMatchesKnownConformance` carry the
   updated proof and pinned counts.

`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s pinned expectations for `get_filters` are updated accordingly (before:
2 TYPE / 1 SCHEMA-DEFECT / 1 MISSING / 1 EXTRA actionable, 1 suppressed OMITEMPTY; after: 1 SCHEMA-DEFECT
actionable only, nothing suppressed — TYPE and OMITEMPTY close via `InUse`/`Writable` becoming
`commands.GMPBool` and `Filters.Name` gaining `,omitempty`, same as every other family, and MISSING/EXTRA
close via item 4 above).

`modify_filter`'s review-only doc had claimed "no `PointerOverrides` entry needed" for `Name`/`Comment`/
`Term`/`Type` on the strength of "byte-identical to the hand-written type" — exactly the inference
`modify_alert`'s question had already discredited once (see above): byte-identical string types can still
hide an omit-vs-explicit-empty distinction `schema.Comparer` has no category for, so the claim needed the
same live-experiment treatment `modify_alert` got, not an assertion. That experiment was run: a throwaway
program (same never-committed status as `modify_alert`'s) authenticated against gvmd 26.36.1, created a
filter, and used `gmp.Connection.RawXML` to probe all four optional `modify_filter` fields — for each, set a
baseline value, omit the element and read back (unchanged, as expected), send it explicitly empty and read
back, then set a distinct value as a sanity check. Result: `Name` and `Comment` behave exactly like
`modify_alert`'s — omitting leaves the stored value unchanged, explicit-empty clears it to `""`. `Term` also
distinguishes the two, though explicit-empty doesn't clear it to `""`; it resets it to gvmd's own default
filter term (e.g. `first=1 rows=11 sort=name`) — still a real distinction a bare `string,omitempty` can never
send, since it can only ever omit the element. `Type` showed no distinction: omitting and sending it
explicitly empty both leave the stored value unchanged, matching the review-only doc's original claim for
that one field only. `internal/codegen/overrides.go`'s `PointerOverrides` table gained three entries,
`modify_filter.name`, `modify_filter.comment`, and `modify_filter.term`, each citing this experiment;
`modify_filter.type` has no entry and keeps the mechanical default. `commands/modify_filter.go` regenerated
with `Comment`/`Name`/`Term` as `*string,omitempty` (`Type` stays plain `string,omitempty`).
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `modify_filter` before/after counts are unaffected (both were already
empty and stay empty): `schema.Comparer`'s type check strips a leading `*` before comparing, so switching
between `string` and `*string` moves no number, exactly as it didn't for `modify_alert`.
`internal/gmptest/cases_filter.go` gained `name_comment_term_unset`/`_empty`/`_set` `TestRequestGolden`
cases pinning all three wire shapes for the three pointer fields, the same follow-up pattern `modify_alert`'s
adoption used for its own two.

The naming collision flagged at review-only resolved exactly as predicted, the same way it did for
`note`/`override`/`role`/`group`/`tag`/`schedule`/`alert`: the wrapper element took the `Filters` name
(renamed from the hand-written `FilterInfo`) and the repeated `<filter>` child took `Filter`, so
`GetFiltersResponse.Filters []Filter` became `GetFiltersResponse.Filter []Filter`. Elsewhere in the family,
`InUse`/`Writable` became `commands.GMPBool` (the same lexical-boolean fix every other family needed);
`FilterUserTags.Tags []FilterUserTag` was renamed to `.Tag []FilterTag` for the same child-element-name-wins
reason `AlertUserTag`/`AlertTag` was; `FilterAlert` gained a `Permissions string` field the hand-written type
never modeled; and `FilterSortField` was renamed to `FilterField` to match `sort`'s child element name
directly rather than the hand-written type's more descriptive but non-wire-matching name. The three call
sites this touched — `client/client_test.go`'s literal `GetFiltersResponse` construction,
`client/client_filter_test.go`'s `TestGetFilters`, and `internal/gmptest/capture_sequence_test.go`'s
`lifecycleFilter` — were updated to match: `.Filters` renamed to `.Filter` at the two call sites, and
`lifecycleFilter`'s `modify_filter` step given a local `modifiedTerm` variable to take `Term`'s address, the
same pattern `lifecycleAlert`'s `modifiedComment` already used. `internal/gmptest/registry.go` needed no
change — none of its type names moved.

The regenerated live fixtures under `internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_filters` confirm the collision codec
round-trips cleanly through the adopted types with no new or undocumented loss: decoding and re-marshaling
all three fixtures never produces a literal `<filters2>` tag anywhere in the output (the codec's
`GetFiltersFilters` field marshals back under the true wire name, `<filters start="..." max="..."/>`), and
the only `DROPPED` paths left are the two gaps below, unchanged from review-only.

Two further gaps review surfaced remain true post-adoption, invisible to `schema.Comparer` for the same
reason as `role_count`/`group_count`/`tag_count`/`schedule_count`/`alert_count` and their `sort/field`
counterparts: `filter_count`'s declared `<pattern>` is only `<e>filtered</e><e>page</e>`, with no text token
anywhere in it, and `field` (inside `sort`) is likewise `<e>order</e>` with no text token — yet live captures
under `internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_filters` send a bare total/name ahead of those children. Pinned by
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestFilterCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` against all three live
fixtures, following the same decode-twice-into-two-shapes proof `TestRoleCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss`
uses; it still passes unchanged, since the generated shape it pins against didn't change at adoption. The
`sort/field` half was pinned the same way by the now-removed `TestFilterSortFieldNameIsAnAcceptedLoss`,
until T-18a's chardata relocation closed it (see T-18a below).

**`tls_certificate` adopted:** the next family after `filter` per the ranking method above (score 23, tied
with `filter` — no tie-break criterion in the ranking method distinguishes the two, and `filter` was already
picked over it as the reasonable default), added to `internal/codegen/manifest.json` as `review_only` first,
with schema snapshots and golden output extracted and committed
(`internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_tls_certificate,get_tls_certificates,
modify_tls_certificate}.*`) and reviewed by hand across two review rounds (detailed below) — then
`review_only` was dropped and `hack/generate.sh` written into
`commands/{create_tls_certificate,get_tls_certificates,modify_tls_certificate}.go` (no
`tls_certificate_shared.go` is actually emitted — the family has no shared nested type, same as
`role`/`group`/`schedule`/`filter`). `create_tls_certificate` is fully conformant and generates
byte-identical to the hand-written original (`Trust` becomes `*commands.GMPBool` mechanically via the
existing lexical-boolean pointer rule; no `PointerOverrides` entry needed). `modify_tls_certificate` is also
fully conformant, but a live gvmd
26.36.1 experiment (omit/explicit-empty/set triads against `Name`, `Comment`, and `Copy`, read back via
`get_tls_certificates`) disproved this family's initial "byte-identical, no override needed" claim — the
same inference already shown wrong once for `modify_filter`. `Name`/`Comment` behave exactly like every
other adopted family's optional strings: omitting the field leaves the stored value unchanged, sending it
explicit-empty clears it, a distinction a bare `string,omitempty` cannot express. Both now have
`PointerOverrides` entries (`internal/codegen/overrides.go`) and generate as `*string`. `Copy` is different
in kind: omit, explicit-empty, and a real second certificate's UUID all returned `200 OK` but left the
target's name, comment, and both fingerprints completely unchanged in every case — unlike
`create_tls_certificate`'s `copy` (which clones at creation time), `modify_tls_certificate`'s `copy` is
observably inert in this gvmd version, so there is no omit-vs-explicit-empty distinction to encode and no
override was added for it.

`get_tls_certificates` carries the family's entire load — actionable MISSING 5, EXTRA 5, KIND 2, TYPE 3;
suppressed OMITEMPTY 33 — confirmed via `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidateTLSCertificate*`
gate pair. Uniquely among every family reviewed so far, all three of its snapshots have a nil `cmd.Defects`
(zero SCHEMA-DEFECT), and generation closes every other actionable finding, leaving zero actionable findings
on all three commands — the first family whose review-only pass ends at zero *actionable* findings. Two
suppressed EXTRA findings remain on `get_tls_certificates`, both deliberate: see the `valid`/certificate
discussion below. MISSING closes `@filt_id` on the request (`GetTLSCertificates` gains `FiltID`) and four
`/tls_certificate/sources/source/*` children (`location`/`origin`/`timestamp`/`tls_versions`) the
hand-written `TLSCertificateSource` never modeled at all (it only carries `Type`/`ID` attrs, and the schema's
actual `source` pattern has neither the `type` attr nor a bare shape — `id` attr plus three required
elements and one optional one). EXTRA closes `/filters/comment`, `/tls_certificate/sources/source/@type`
(the hand-written `Type` attr the schema never declares), and `/truncated`, plus the two discussed below.
KIND closes `tls_certificate_count/filtered`/`/page` (schema elements the hand-written type modeled as
attrs). TYPE closes `in_use`/`trust`/`writable` becoming `commands.GMPBool`, the same lexical-boolean fix
every other family needed.

Two of the EXTRA findings are worth individual comment, since neither closes the way TYPE/KIND/OMITEMPTY
did — both were initially treated as an accepted loss or an open question, and a review round rejected that
framing: the schema's own declared pattern is not evidence that gvmd never sends the data, and
`IncludeCertificateData` exists specifically to fetch one of the two. Both are now modeled via a new,
generic `FieldOverride`/`FieldOverrides` mechanism (`internal/codegen/overrides.go`,
`internal/codegen/generate.go`) that adds an evidence-backed, schema-absent field to a generated struct
while keeping it visible to `schema.Comparer` as a suppressed (not silently ignored) EXTRA finding — the
`Suppressed`/`Reason` mechanism `checkOmitEmpty` already established, extended to cover this case
(`internal/schema/gomodel.go`, `internal/schema/conform.go`). A stale or mistyped `FieldOverrides` entry
fails the generator build rather than silently doing nothing.

`/tls_certificate/certificate/#text` (the hand-written `TLSCertificateData`'s chardata field): the schema's
`certificate` pattern truly declares no chardata token (only a `format` attribute), confirmed against both
the committed snapshot and the raw `command_get_tls_certificates.xml`, yet gvmd's own bundled example sends
real base64 certificate bytes as that element's text. A live capture with `IncludeCertificateData: true`
now backs this too (`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_tls_certificates/004_response.xml`, redacted per
this command's existing secret-bearing classification in `internal/gmptest/redact.go`), closing the earlier
single-fixture evidence gap. `TLSCertificateCertificate` now generates a `Value string \`xml:",chardata"\``
field via `FieldOverrides["get_tls_certificates:/tls_certificate/certificate"]`;
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestTLSCertificateDataRoundTrips` (formerly
`TestTLSCertificateDataIsAnAcceptedLoss`) proves a decode-then-re-marshal round trip against the upstream
fixture.

`/tls_certificate/valid`: the schema's `tls_certificate` pattern never references a `valid` child anywhere.
Live traffic proves gvmd sends it regardless
(`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_tls_certificates/003_response.xml` and `004_response.xml`, plus the
upstream example, all have `<valid>1</valid>`). `TLSCertificate` now generates a `Valid GMPBool` field via
`FieldOverrides["get_tls_certificates:/tls_certificate"]`, closing what was previously left as an explicit
open question for the adopter.

The two bare-text contradictions already on record for every prior family repeat here in the same shape:
`tls_certificate_count`'s declared `<pattern>` is only `<e>filtered</e><e>page</e>`, and `field` (inside
`sort`) is only `<e>order</e>`, neither with a text token — yet live captures under
`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_tls_certificates` send a bare total/name ahead of those children (`0` for
001/002, `1` for 003; `name`/`ascending` in all three). Pinned by
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestTLSCertificateCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` against all three
live fixtures, following the same decode-twice-into-two-shapes proof
`TestFilterCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` uses. The `sort/field` half was pinned the same way by the
now-removed `TestTLSCertificateSortFieldNameIsAnAcceptedLoss` (and, for `filter` above, the now-removed
`TestFilterSortFieldNameIsAnAcceptedLoss`), until T-18a's chardata relocation closed both (see T-18a below).

Fixed in this pass (a review round rejected deferring it): the generated `SubjectDn`/`IssuerDn` field names
would otherwise lose the DN casing the hand-written `SubjectDN`/`IssuerDN` (and `get_reports.go`'s own
`SubjectDN`) use, because `internal/codegen/generate.go`'s `initialisms` table had no `dn` entry — unlike
`icalendar`/`md5`/`sha256`, which were each added at their own family's review-only stage. Added `"dn":
"DN"`; confirmed via a corpus-wide grep of every committed schema snapshot that `get_tls_certificates.json`'s
`subject_dn`/`issuer_dn` are the only occurrence, so the blast radius is exactly this one file. Golden now
generates `SubjectDN`/`IssuerDN`; `internal/codegen/manifest_test.go`'s `TestTLSCertificateDNNamingIsStable`
pins the casing the same way the `ICalendar`/`MD5Fingerprint`/`SHA256Fingerprint` naming-stability tests do
for their own families.

The wrapper-vs-list naming collision flagged at review-only resolved exactly as predicted, the same way it
did for `note`/`override`/`role`/`group`/`tag`/`schedule`/`alert`/`filter`: the hand-written
`GetTLSCertificatesResponse.TLSCertificates []TLSCertificate` became `.TLSCertificate`, and the wrapper
(previously `TLSCertificateTLS`) took the `TLSCertificates` name instead. Elsewhere in the family,
`InUse`/`Trust`/`Writable`/`Valid` became `commands.GMPBool`; `ModifyTLSCertificate.Name`/`.Comment` became
`*string` per the `PointerOverrides` entries above; and the response gained the several renamed/added nested
types the MISSING/EXTRA/KIND findings above describe (`TLSCertificateSource`'s new
`Location`/`Origin`/`Timestamp`/`TLSVersions` fields, `TLSCertificateCount.Filtered`/`.Page` as plain ints,
`TLSCertificateCertificate.Value`/`.Valid` via the `FieldOverrides` mechanism). The call sites this touched
— `client/client_tls_certificate_test.go`'s `TestModifyTLSCertificate`/`TestGetTLSCertificates`,
`client/client_test.go`'s mock command handler, and `internal/gmptest/capture_sequence_test.go`'s
`lifecycleTLSCertificate` — were updated to match, the same pattern every prior family's adoption used:
`.TLSCertificates` renamed to `.TLSCertificate` at the two client-test call sites, and
`lifecycleTLSCertificate`'s `modify_tls_certificate` step given a local `modifiedComment` variable to take
`Comment`'s address, the same pattern `lifecycleAlert`/`lifecycleFilter` already used.
`internal/gmptest/cases_tls_certificate.go` was added with `trust_true`/`_unset`/`_false` cases for
`create_tls_certificate`, `include_data_true`/`_unset`/`_false` cases for `get_tls_certificates` (moved here
from `cases_misc.go`, where they had lived since before this family had its own case file), and
`name_comment_unset`/`_empty`/`_set` plus `trust_true`/`_unset`/`_false` cases for `modify_tls_certificate` —
the same follow-up pattern `alert`'s and `filter`'s adoptions used. With that real coverage in place, the
generator-level marshal stand-in described in finding 4 below was removed from
`TestTLSCertificateGeneratedRoundTrips`, which now only proves the generated-response round trip (finding 3);
its two `decodeAndRoundTrip` calls against upstream fixture 001 and live fixture 004 remain, since those
uniquely prove review-only generation preserves certificate data, format, and validity.

**Second review round, against the `FieldOverride` mechanism itself:** the first round's resolution
introduced `FieldOverride`/`FieldOverrides` to model `valid` and certificate chardata; a follow-up review
raised four findings against that mechanism's safety and how thoroughly its evidence is exercised, all now
resolved.

1. *Generator safety* — `FieldOverride` entries were validated only at consumption
   (`checkFieldOverridesConsumed` catches a key that never matched anything), never at definition. A new
   `(g *gen) validateFieldOverrides()` (`internal/codegen/generate.go`), run from `Generate` right after
   `collect()`, now fails closed on every malformed shape: empty `Reason`; empty or syntactically invalid
   `GoType` (checked via `go/parser.ParseExpr`); `Chardata` combined with `Name`/`Attr`/`OmitEmpty`; more
   than one chardata override per key; two non-chardata entries with the same `(Name, Attr)` pair; and an
   override whose `(Name, Attr)` duplicates an existing schema-declared child
   (`schemaChildCollision`). `internal/codegen/overrides_test.go` covers each rejected shape plus a
   regression net that the real, committed `FieldOverrides` table validates cleanly.
2. *Generator correctness* — `fieldOverridesFor` used to merge every matching entry across *all* `use`s of a
   structurally-deduplicated `structType`, which would leak one use's override onto an unrelated path or
   command if two uses disagreed (dormant today: `tls_certificate`'s two overridden shapes each have exactly
   one use, but a latent trap for the next family that shares a shape). Rewritten to resolve each distinct
   key independently and require every use to resolve to the same result — no match counts as the empty
   result, so "one use overridden, one not" fails exactly like "two different overrides" does — via
   `overrideResultEqual`. `internal/codegen/generate_test.go`'s `TestFieldOverridesCrossUseMismatchOnMissingKey`,
   `TestFieldOverridesCrossUseMismatchOnDifferentEntries`, `TestFieldOverridesCrossUseIdenticalEntriesSucceed`,
   and `TestFieldOverridesSchemaChildCollision` exercise this against two synthetic commands sharing one
   structurally identical shape.
3. *Evidence coverage* — `TestTLSCertificateDataRoundTrips` decoded only the unredacted upstream fixture
   through a hand-rolled probe type, never the real generated code, and never touched the redacted live
   fixture 004. `TestTLSCertificateGeneratedRoundTrips` (`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`) now compiles
   the real generated `GetTLSCertificatesResponse` into a scratch module (the same pattern
   `TestGetFiltersCollisionRoundTrips` established) and decodes both
   `internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/get_tls_certificates/001_response.xml` and
   `internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_tls_certificates/004_response.xml` through it, asserting
   `Certificate.Value`, `.Format`, and `Valid` on both fixtures and proving a marshal/re-decode round trip.
   The hand-probe test stays alongside it as a fast, isolated sanity check.
4. *Adoption readiness* — the finding asked for `ModifyTLSCertificate` omit/explicit-empty/set request-golden
   coverage now, citing `alert`/`filter` as prior art for adding it before adoption. Git history
   (`bc418c9`, `9dd9654`) showed the opposite: `cases_alert.go` and `cases_filter.go` were both added in the
   *same commit* that flipped `manifest.json`'s `review_only` flag, not beforehand — and
   `internal/gmptest/cases_tls_certificate.go` could not be written yet regardless, since
   `RegisterRequestCase` constructs values against the real `commands` package and
   `commands.ModifyTLSCertificate` did not exist until adoption generated it there.
   `TestTLSCertificateGeneratedRoundTrips` covered the finding's own fallback ("generator-level marshal
   tests") in the interim: it marshaled `ModifyTLSCertificate` across the omit/explicit-empty/set matrix on
   `Name` and `Comment`, and unset/`false`/`true` on `Trust`, against the real generated type in a scratch
   module, asserting the exact wire output — explicitly documented in-code as a stand-in for
   `cases_tls_certificate.go`. Now that adoption has made that file possible, the real
   `internal/gmptest/cases_tls_certificate.go` supersedes it, and the stand-in matrix has been removed from
   `TestTLSCertificateGeneratedRoundTrips` (see above).

**Next milestone:** `tls_certificate` is adopted (see above) — all four first-round findings (the `valid`
field, certificate chardata, `dn` casing, and `modify_tls_certificate`'s optional-string semantics) and all
four second-round findings against the `FieldOverride` mechanism (validation, cross-use consistency,
real-generated-code coverage, and modify-request wire shapes) were resolved with live or upstream evidence
and tests before the swap, and the wrapper-vs-list naming collision and `cases_tls_certificate.go`'s
request-golden wiring for `modify_tls_certificate.name/comment/trust` landed with it. Next: the
post-`tls_certificate` revisit pass below, or move on to `permission` (score 31), the next family in the
ranking. Adopt any review-only family only after request goldens, fidelity, conformance, generation drift,
builds, vet, race tests, formatting, and lint all pass with every regression explained.

**`permission` adopted:** `permission`, the next family after `tls_certificate` per the ranking method above
(score 31, the worst of the ten families ranked at the start of this milestone — see the "Family selection
method" note), added to `internal/codegen/manifest.json` as `review_only` first, with schema snapshots and
golden output extracted and committed (`internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_permission,
get_permissions,modify_permission,permission_shared}.*`) and reviewed by hand below — then `review_only` was
dropped and `hack/generate.sh` written into
`commands/{create_permission,get_permissions,modify_permission,permission_shared}.go`. Adoption was blocked
for a period on live evidence for the `modify_permission.Name`/`.Comment` omit-vs-explicit-empty question;
see below for why that evidence turned out to be unobtainable and how adoption proceeded anyway.

`create_permission` and `modify_permission` share the same shape and the same findings: actionable EXTRA 8,
OMITEMPTY 2; suppressed OMITEMPTY 3 and 2 respectively — confirmed via `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s
`TestValidatePermission*` gate pair. All of it closes under generation. The hand-written `PermissionSubject`/
`PermissionResource` conflated the family's two different `subject`/`resource` shapes: `create_permission`'s
and `modify_permission`'s own schema patterns declare only `id` (optional attr) and `type` (required) for
both elements, while `get_permissions`'s response-side `subject`/`resource` are richer (`id`, `name`,
`permissions`, `type`, `trash`, and — `resource` only — `deleted`). The hand-written type used the richer
shape everywhere, producing the 8 EXTRA findings on the request side (`name`/`permissions`/`trash`/`deleted`
on both `resource` and `subject`) plus a `type`-omitempty mismatch (schema requires it, hand-written tagged
it optional) and a missing `id`-omitempty (schema allows omission, hand-written always emitted it).
Generation resolves this the same way `tag`/`alert` resolved their own request/response shape conflicts:
`permission_shared.go` now carries a narrow, family-local `PermissionSubject`/`PermissionResource` (`id`+
`type` only) shared by `create_permission`/`modify_permission`, while `get_permissions.go` gets its own
richer `GetPermissionsSubject`/`GetPermissionsResource`.

`get_permissions` carries the rest of the family's findings: actionable MISSING 1, EXTRA 1, TYPE 5,
SCHEMA-DEFECT 2; suppressed OMITEMPTY 29. MISSING closes `/sort/#text`: unlike every prior family reviewed
so far, `get_permissions`'s own schema declares a text token directly on `<sort>` itself
(`<pattern>text<e>field</e></pattern>` in `command_get_permissions.xml`, not just at the nested `<field>`
level), so `PermissionSort` gains a schema-derived `Value string \`xml:",chardata"\`` field, closing the
finding on schema terms. This closure should not be read as `PermissionSort.Value` capturing the sort
field's name, though: `encoding/xml` assigns a chardata field only the text that is a *direct* child of
that element, and in every fixture on record (three live captures plus gvmd's own upstream example) the
only direct-child text of `<sort>` is indentation whitespace, or nothing at all in the live fixtures'
compact encoding — `"name"` is a child of the nested `<field>`, one level down, which was the still-open
accepted loss described below until T-18a's chardata relocation closed it (see T-18a below).
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s now-removed `TestPermissionSortValueIsWhitespaceOnly` pinned
`PermissionSort.Value` as empty-after-trim against all four fixtures while this schema/example
contradiction was still on record. EXTRA closes the
hand-written `Subject.Deleted` field: `resource`'s pattern declares `deleted` but `subject`'s does not (a
dangling, unreferenced `<ele><name>deleted</name>...</ele>` sits nested inside `subject`'s schema block, but
nothing in `subject`'s own pattern references it), so `GetPermissionsSubject` correctly has no `Deleted`
field while `GetPermissionsResource` keeps one. TYPE closes `in_use`/`resource.deleted`/`resource.trash`/
`subject.trash`/`writable` becoming `commands.GMPBool`, the usual lexical-boolean fix. All 29 suppressed
OMITEMPTY findings close: the generated response tags omitempty exactly where the schema marks a field
optional (the two `id` attributes) and nowhere else, replacing the hand-written struct's inconsistent mix.
SCHEMA-DEFECT 2 does not close and is not expected to: `resource/permissions` and `subject/permissions` both
declare an empty `<pattern/>`, so the generator's fail-closed default renders them as opaque
`string,omitempty` scalars — the same empty-pattern class already on record for a dozen other commands —
replacing the hand-written type's fabricated `*PermissionList` struct, which was never grounded in the
schema.

The wrapper-vs-list naming collision resolves exactly as it has for every prior family:
`GetPermissionsResponse.Permission []Permission` (not `.Permissions`), with `Permissions{Start,Max}` taking
over as the pagination-wrapper type name.

The two bare-text contradictions already on record for every prior family repeat here in the same shape:
`permission_count`'s declared `<pattern>` is only `<e>filtered</e><e>page</e>`, and `field` (inside `sort`)
is only `<e>order</e>`, neither with a text token — yet live captures under
`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_permissions` send a bare total/name ahead of those children (`260` for
001/002, `262` for 003; `name`/`ascending` in all three) and gvmd's own bundled/upstream example
(`internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/get_permissions/001_response.xml`) shows the same shape (`1`/`name`).
Pinned by `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestPermissionCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` against
all three live fixtures plus the upstream example, following the same decode-twice-into-two-shapes proof
`TestFilterCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` uses. The `sort/field` half was pinned the same way by the
now-removed `TestPermissionSortFieldNameIsAnAcceptedLoss`, until T-18a's chardata relocation closed it
(see T-18a below).

**`modify_permission.Name`/`.Comment` omit-vs-explicit-empty: experiment attempted, blocked by a gvmd bug.**
`modify_permission.Name`/`.Comment` are plain `string,omitempty` today, the same starting point
`modify_alert`/`modify_filter`/`modify_tls_certificate` each had before a live gvmd experiment proved
omit-vs-explicit-empty was a real, distinct wire semantic for their own `Name`/`Comment` fields. Per the
policy established for those three families, the same experiment was attempted here (throwaway
`//go:build integration` test creating a subject user and a permission, then running the omit/explicit-
empty/set matrix against `modify_permission`, read back via `get_permissions`) — but every real
`modify_permission` invocation against the live gvmd instance under test (`greenbone/gvmd`, `stable` tag,
confirmed current via a fresh image pull with no digest change) crashes the gvmd worker process before a
result can be observed.

Root cause, confirmed against `gvmd`'s own source on GitHub (`src/manage_sql_permissions.c`, present on
`main`): `permission_resource_id`/`permission_subject_id` query columns named `resource_id`/`subject_id`,
neither of which exists on the `permissions` table (confirmed via `\d permissions` against the live
Postgres instance — the real columns are `resource`, `resource_uuid`, `subject`). `modify_permission`'s
