handler calls both functions on the path GMP XML clients actually take (`<subject id="...">`/
`<resource id="...">` as structured elements, not the raw string args the functions' own callers gate on),
so the query fails with a plain `column "resource_id" does not exist` (or `subject_id`) Postgres error.
gvmd does not check for that failure and keeps issuing queries against the now-aborted transaction, which
eventually kills the worker (`Received Aborted signal`; the client sees `transport: EOF`). This was
confirmed reproducible and unconditional across three independent request shapes (comment-only, name-only,
resource-scoped and resourceless permissions), confirmed present in the Postgres logs from a full day before
this experiment (not something the experiment triggered), and independently corroborated by
python-gvm issue #1047, which reports the same "Error in SUBJECT" / crash symptom against `modify_permission`.
A Postgres lock and a stale Docker image were both raised and ruled out directly against the running
containers before this conclusion was reached.

A separate, smaller schema/wire contradiction surfaced during the same experiment: `modify_permission`
unconditionally requires `<subject>` in the request body — omitting it (even when only changing
`<comment>`) returns `400 Error in SUBJECT` — despite the schema declaring `<subject>` `CardOptional` (see
`internal/codegen/testdata/schema/modify_permission.json`). This is independent of the crash above (it
still occurs when the crash is avoided by always including `<subject>`) and is recorded here rather than
acted on, since the generated `Subject *PermissionSubject` already matches the schema's own declared
cardinality.

Because the crash makes it impossible to observe how gvmd actually treats an omitted vs. explicit-empty
`Name`/`Comment`, no live evidence can be obtained, and none is invented: `ModifyPermission.Name`/`.Comment`
stay plain `string,omitempty`, with no `PointerOverrides` entries. This is different in kind from
`modify_tls_certificate.copy` (tested and observed to have no distinction) — here the wire behavior is
simply unobservable, not observed-and-negative. The upstream bug will be reported to Greenbone directly
(not by this codebase); if a fix lands and the experiment becomes runnable, revisit this pair the same way
the post-`tls_certificate` revisit pass below covers other still-unverified optional string fields.

**`ticket` added in review-only mode:** re-ran the rank-summing method over the 11 remaining un-adopted
families; `ticket` won (score 16.5, next-best `user` at 17.5) on a zero-`SCHEMA-DEFECT` profile and
already-conformant `create_ticket`/`modify_ticket`. Added to `manifest.json` as `review_only`, with schema
snapshots and golden output committed under `internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_ticket,
get_tickets,modify_ticket,ticket_shared}.*` and `commands/` left untouched.

`create_ticket`/`modify_ticket` generate with only the schema's own `assigned_to` naming
(`Assigned`→`AssignedTo`); no other findings. `get_tickets` carries the whole family's finding load —
actionable MISSING 6, EXTRA 4, KIND 3, TYPE 3, suppressed OMITEMPTY 23 — and generation closes all of it
to zero: request `filt_id`/`trash` attrs and response `filters/keywords` are now modelled; `sort` gains a
schema-derived chardata field the same way `permission`'s did, and the nested `<field>` gains its declared
`<order>` child instead of being flattened to a bare string; `ticket/user_tags/tag/comment` is modelled;
the four schema-absent hand-written fields (`filters.comment`, `sort.order`, `nvt.name`, `.truncated`) are
dropped; `user_tags/count` and `ticket_count`'s `filtered`/`page` move from attrs to the elements the
schema declares; `in_use`/`orphan`/`writable` become `commands.GMPBool`. Confirmed via
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidateTicket*` gate pair. Zero `SCHEMA-DEFECT` findings remain
— a schema-conformance result only, separate from runtime fidelity, which still intentionally loses the two
bare-text paths documented below. The wrapper/list collision resolves as usual: `Tickets{Start,Max}` is the
pagination wrapper, `Ticket` the repeated element.

The same two bare-text contradictions on record for every prior family repeat here: `ticket_count`'s
pattern declares no text token but fixtures send a bare total ahead of `filtered`/`page`, and `field`
(inside `sort`) declares only `order` but fixtures send the sort field's name as bare text. Pinned by `TestTicketCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` against the live and upstream `get_tickets` fixtures.
The `sort/field` half was pinned the same way by the now-removed `TestTicketSortFieldNameIsAnAcceptedLoss`
and `TestTicketSortValueIsWhitespaceOnly`, until T-18a's chardata relocation closed it (see T-18a below).

`modify_ticket`'s optional strings were tested live (gvmd 26.36.1) rather than deferred, per the same
policy already applied to `alert`/`filter`/`tls_certificate`. `comment`, `open_note`, `fixed_note` and
`closed_note` behave like `modify_alert`'s fields: omitting the element leaves the stored value unchanged,
while sending it explicit-empty (`<comment></comment>` via raw XML, bypassing Go's `omitempty`) clears it.
All four are pointer-typed via `PointerOverrides`. `status` does not behave the same way: gvmd rejects an
explicit-empty `<status></status>` outright with `400 Error in status` rather than accepting it as a clear,
so there is no legal empty wire state for a pointer to express, and it keeps the mechanical string default.
That rejection is still worth surfacing to callers, so it is recorded as a `CommandWarning` on
`modify_ticket` instead. Omitting `status` is a no-op, confirmed by omitting every field at once and
observing the prior populated values held.

**`ticket` adopted:** `review_only` dropped from `internal/codegen/manifest.json` and `hack/generate.sh` written
into `commands/{create_ticket,get_tickets,modify_ticket,ticket_shared}.go`, matching the golden output
reviewed above byte-for-byte. Downstream call sites updated for the field/type renames: `commands/pagination/
tickets.go` (`resp.Tickets`→`resp.Ticket`, the now-nil-proof `TicketCount.Filtered` access matching `tasks.go`'s
own pattern), its two test doubles, `internal/gmptest/cases_ticket.go` and `capture_sequence_test.go`
(`Assigned`→`AssignedTo`, non-pointer `TicketUser`), and `client/client_ticket_test.go`/`client_test.go`
(pointer-typed `ModifyTicket.ClosedNote`). `TestValidateTicket*` confirms the committed `commands/` package
now matches the zero-finding post-generation state pinned during review. `ticket` was the last family carried
in review-only mode; no family in the committed manifest is currently review-only.

**`credential` added in review-only mode:** re-ran the rank-summing method over the 7 remaining un-adopted,
non-`report`-family candidates (`credential`, `target`, `scanner`, `port_list`, `user`, `asset`, `task`;
`report`/`report_config`/`report_format`/`config` excluded as far larger and carrying their own
`SCHEMA-DEFECT` findings — `create_report` alone has MISSING 38/EXTRA 5/CARDINALITY 5/OMITEMPTY
144/TYPE 4/SCHEMA-DEFECT 10). `credential` won by a wide margin: 14 total findings against next-best
`asset`'s 22, tied-lowest SCHEMA-DEFECT count (2, both the familiar `scanner/permissions` and
`target/permissions` empty-pattern class already accepted for other families), and a purely additive
profile (MISSING/EXTRA/TYPE only, no KIND/CARDINALITY) carrying no field-removal or type-restructuring
risk — the same profile that made `role` the winning pick originally. Added to `manifest.json` as
`review_only`, with schema snapshots and golden output committed under
`internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_credential,get_credentials,modify_credential,
credential_shared}.*` and `commands/` left untouched.

`create_credential`/`modify_credential` generate with only a naming change — the per-command
`CreateCredentialKDCs`/`CreateCredentialKey`/`CreateCredentialPrivacy` duplicates collapse into one
family-shared `CredentialKdcs`/`CredentialKey`/`CredentialPrivacy` (`credential_shared.go`), and `KDC`
becomes `Kdc` (not in the initialisms table, matching the generator's default casing, no table entry
needed) — closing the 2 suppressed OMITEMPTY findings on `privacy/algorithm` and `privacy/password`
(both request-side over-constraint: the schema declares them required, matching the new
`CredentialPrivacy`). `get_credentials` carries the whole family's actionable finding load — MISSING 2,
EXTRA 1, TYPE 3, plus 2 suppressed OMITEMPTY — and generation closes all of it except the 2
SCHEMA-DEFECT findings, which stay untouched (copied from the schema's own defects, unaffected by any
Go-type comparison, the same as every other family's empty-pattern sites): `certificate_info` and
`privacy` gain full generated struct shapes instead of a bare chardata scalar; the schema-absent
`truncated` field is dropped; `allow_insecure`/`writable`/`in_use` become `commands.GMPBool`; and
`formats`/`filters.name` gain the schema-derived `omitempty` tags. Confirmed via
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidateCredential*` gate pair. The wrapper/list collision
resolves as usual: `Credentials{Start,Max}` is the pagination wrapper, `Credential` the repeated element.

The same two bare-text contradictions on record for every prior family repeat here: `credential_count`'s
pattern declares no text token but fixtures send a bare total ahead of `filtered`/`page`, and `field`
(inside `sort`) declares only `order` but fixtures send the sort field's name as bare text. Pinned by `TestCredentialCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` against the live `get_credentials` fixtures (the
upstream fixtures under `internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/get_credentials/` are gvmd's own bundled
examples and carry neither `credential_count` nor `sort`, so only live fixtures pin this defect for
`credential`). The `sort/field` half was pinned the same way by the now-removed
`TestCredentialSortFieldNameIsAnAcceptedLoss` and `TestCredentialSortValueIsWhitespaceOnly`, until T-18a's
chardata relocation closed it (see T-18a below).

Because `credential` is still review-only, `internal/gmptest/cases_credential.go` does not exist yet and
the goldens above only compile against the hand-written `commands` package — neither proves the real
generated request/response shapes. `TestCredentialGeneratedRequestRoundTrips` and
`TestCredentialGeneratedResponseRoundTrips` (`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`) close that gap using the
same scratch-module `go run` technique `ticket`'s review-only pass used. The request test pins the
`Kdc`/`Kdcs`/`Key`/`CredentialPrivacy` renames and dedup on both `create_credential` and
`modify_credential`, that a non-nil, zero-valued `CredentialPrivacy` still emits empty `<algorithm>`/
`<password>` elements rather than omitting them (generation drops their `omitempty`, matching the
schema's required-element declaration), all three `AllowInsecure` wire states, and the
`modify_credential.comment` `PointerOverride` below. The response test round-trips real fixtures
(`internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/get_credentials/001_response.xml` for repeated `Credential` and
multi-value `Formats`, `.../003_response.xml` for a `target` entry and `package` chardata+format, and
`.../live/get_credentials/004_response.xml` for `AllowInsecure`/`Writable`/`InUse`), plus a synthetic
document for the three paths no committed fixture exercises: `Kdcs`, a `scanner` entry, and a fully
populated `privacy`/`certificate_info` (every `CredentialCertificateInfo` field, including the optional
`sha256_fingerprint`/`subject`/`serial`, and `GetCredentialsPrivacy.Algorithm`). Both tests should be
superseded by ordinary request/response goldens once `credential` is adopted.

`modify_credential`'s optional strings were tested live (gvmd 26.36.1), per the same policy already
applied to `alert`/`filter`/`tls_certificate`/`ticket`, against `up`, `cc`, `snmp` and `krb5` credentials
created directly on the running instance. `comment` behaves like `modify_alert`'s fields: omitting the
element leaves the stored value unchanged, while sending it explicit-empty (via raw XML, bypassing Go's
`omitempty`) clears it, and is pointer-typed via `PointerOverrides`. `name`, `login` and `auth_algorithm`
do not: explicit-empty left each stored value unchanged, indistinguishable from omitting the element, so
they keep the mechanical string default. `realm` and `kdc` (singular) both reject an explicit-empty
element outright — `400 Invalid or empty kerberos realm value` and `400 Invalid or empty kdc value(s)`
respectively — rather than accepting it as a clear, so neither has a legal empty wire state a pointer
could express; both are recorded as a `CommandWarning` on `modify_credential` instead, the same treatment
`modify_ticket.status` got. `certificate`, `community` and `password` could not be tested at all: gvmd's
`get_credentials` response never echoes any of the three back, with or without `details="1"` (confirmed
by raw-XML inspection of a `cc` credential with `details="1"` — the certificate itself is absent even
there, only a `certificate_info` summary and an unrelated, not-yet-modelled `private_key_info` element
come back), so no live test can observe whether omission and explicit-empty differ for them. That
absence-of-evidence is recorded in the `modify_credential` `CommandWarning` rather than guessed at.

**`credential` adopted:** `review_only` dropped from `internal/codegen/manifest.json` and `hack/generate.sh`
written into `commands/{create_credential,get_credentials,modify_credential,credential_shared}.go`, matching
the golden output reviewed above byte-for-byte. Downstream call sites updated for the field/type renames:
`examples/create_credential/main.go` (`CreateCredentialKDCs`/`CreateCredentialKey`/`CreateCredentialPrivacy`→
`CredentialKdcs`/`CredentialKey`/`CredentialPrivacy`), `internal/gmptest/cases_credential.go` (new
`Kdc`/`Kdcs`/`Key`/`Privacy` and `modify_credential.Comment` pointer-override coverage),
`internal/gmptest/redact_test.go`, `client/client_credential_test.go`, and `client/client_test.go`
(`resp.Credentials`→`resp.Credential`, non-pointer `CredentialFormats`), plus `capture_sequence_test.go`.
`TestValidateCredential*` confirms the committed `commands/` package now matches the pinned post-generation
state, `get_credentials`'s 2 `SCHEMA-DEFECT` findings retained as accepted loss, the same treatment every
other family's empty-pattern sites get. Contrary to the "should be superseded" note above,
`TestCredentialGeneratedRequestRoundTrips`/`TestCredentialGeneratedResponseRoundTrips`
(`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`) were left in place rather than removed. The request side is genuinely
redundant now — the new `internal/gmptest/cases_credential.go` request goldens cover the same
`Kdc`/`Kdcs`/`Key`/`Privacy` and `AllowInsecure` shapes through the real `commands` package — but the
response side is not: no committed live/upstream fixture exercises a fully populated `privacy`/
`certificate_info`, a `Kdcs` list, or a `scanner` entry, so the scratch test's synthetic document remains
the only coverage for those schema-derived shapes. Both were kept, matching the precedent already set when
`ticket` was adopted. `credential` was the last family carried in review-only mode; no family in the
committed manifest is currently review-only.

**`port_list` added in review-only mode:** re-ran the rank-summing method over the 6 remaining un-adopted,
non-`report`-family candidates (`target`, `scanner`, `port_list`, `user`, `asset`, `task`;
`report`/`report_config`/`report_format`/`config` excluded as before, far larger and carrying their own
`SCHEMA-DEFECT` findings). `port_list` won (score 8.5, next-best `user` at 12.5) on the smallest
response-graph-size proxy of the six (188 combined `create_port_list`/`get_port_lists`/`modify_port_list.go`
lines), a tied-lowest `SCHEMA-DEFECT` count (2, tied with `user`, both the familiar empty-pattern class), and
its own `[fidelity]`-confirmed finding (`targets/target/permissions`) outranking `scanner`/`user`/`asset`'s
zero. `asset` scored fewer actionable findings outright (11 against `port_list`'s 12) but lost on the
graph-size and fidelity proxies; `target`/`task`/`scanner` trailed well behind on actionable-finding totals
(67/71/19). Added to `manifest.json` as `review_only`, with schema snapshots and golden output committed
under `internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_port_list,get_port_lists,modify_port_list}.*` and
`commands/` left untouched.

`create_port_list` carries only its 1 `SCHEMA-DEFECT` finding (unsupported-r: the schema's own
`<r>get_port_lists</r>` cross-reference inside its request pattern), which survives generation untouched,
the same as every other family's empty-pattern `SCHEMA-DEFECT` class. `modify_port_list` was already
conformant and stays that way. `get_port_lists` carries the whole family's actionable finding load —
MISSING 3, EXTRA 1, CARDINALITY 1, KIND 3, TYPE 4, plus 36 suppressed OMITEMPTY — and generation closes all
of it except its own 1 `SCHEMA-DEFECT` finding (`targets/target/permissions`, the familiar empty-pattern
class): request `details`/`targets`/`trash` become `commands.GMPBool`; `filters/keywords` gains the
structured `PortListKeywords`/`PortListKeyword` the schema declares; `sort` gains a schema-derived
`,chardata` field the same way `ticket`'s and `credential`'s did, and its nested `field` gains the declared
`order` child instead of being flattened to a bare string; the optional `port_list/deprecated` becomes
`*commands.GMPBool`; `user_tags/count` and `port_list_count`'s `filtered`/`page` move from attrs to the
elements the schema declares; and `deprecated`/`in_use`/`predefined`/`writable` become `commands.GMPBool`.
Confirmed via `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidatePortList*` gate pair. The wrapper/list
collision resolves as usual: `PortLists{Start,Max}` is the pagination wrapper, `PortList` the repeated
element — the hand-written `commands.GetPortListsResponse` currently has this backwards, naming its
repeated-list field `PortLists`.

The same two bare-text contradictions on record for every prior family repeat here: `port_list_count`'s
pattern declares no text token but fixtures send a bare total ahead of `filtered`/`page`, and `field`
(inside `sort`) declares only `order` but fixtures send the sort field's name as bare text. Pinned by `TestPortListCountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` against the live `get_port_lists` fixtures (the
upstream fixture under `internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/get_port_lists/001_response.xml` carries
neither `port_list_count` nor `sort`, so only live fixtures pin this defect for `port_list`). The
`sort/field` half was pinned the same way by the now-removed `TestPortListSortFieldNameIsAnAcceptedLoss`
and `TestPortListSortValueIsWhitespaceOnly`, until T-18a's chardata relocation closed it (see T-18a below).

`internal/gmptest`'s existing `create_port_list`/`get_port_lists`/`modify_port_list` request cases construct
the hand-written `commands` package's types (`registry.go`), so they cannot exercise generation's
`Details`/`Targets`/`Trash` `string`→`*GMPBool` conversion or the `PortListSort`/`PortLists`/`PortListTargets`
restructuring — neither proves the real generated shapes. `TestPortListGeneratedRequestRoundTrips` and
`TestPortListGeneratedResponseRoundTrips` (`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`) close that gap using the
same scratch-module `go run` technique `ticket`'s and `credential`'s review-only passes used. The request
test pins the GMP lexical `"0"`/`"1"` wire shape for `GetPortLists.Details`/`.Targets`/`.Trash` across
unset/false/true, `FiltID`, a representative `CreatePortList`, and both `ModifyPortList` wire shapes the
`modify_port_list` `CommandWarning` documents — `Name` and `Comment` set together, and either one omitted
while the other is resent, proving the other still transmits alone. `port_list` has no `PointerOverrides`
entry (see below), so there is no nil-vs-pointer-to-empty distinction to add on top of that. The response
test round-trips real fixtures (`internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/get_port_lists/001_response.xml` for
`port_ranges` and a `targets` entry, and `.../live/get_port_lists/001_response.xml` for the
repeated-`PortList`/pagination-wrapper shapes, `Filters.Keywords.Keyword` (3 entries) and
`Sort.Field.Order` (`"ascending"`)), plus a synthetic document for the two paths no committed fixture
exercises: `deprecated` and a populated `user_tags`. Every fixture's `Sort.Value` decodes empty, including
the live one, confirming the accepted loss above independently of the dedicated probe test: the bare
sort-field name lives inside `<field>`, never directly on `<sort>`. Both tests should be superseded by
ordinary request/response goldens once `port_list` is adopted.

`modify_port_list`'s optional strings were tested live (gvmd 26.36.1) against the running instance, per the
same policy already applied to `alert`/`filter`/`tls_certificate`/`ticket`/`credential`. `name` and
`comment` do not behave like `modify_alert`'s fields: omitting either element does not leave the stored
value unchanged, it clears both together, confirmed twice — once omitting both at once, and once sending
only `<comment>` while leaving `<name>` out of the request entirely, which still cleared `name`. Since
omission and explicit-empty produce the identical cleared result, neither field has a nil-vs-pointer-to-empty
distinction for `PointerOverrides` to express; both keep the mechanical string default. That clear-on-omission
hazard is still worth surfacing to callers, so it is recorded as a `CommandWarning` on `modify_port_list`
instead, the same treatment already given to `modify_role`/`modify_group`.

**Post-`tls_certificate` revisit pass:** before selecting another family, revisit the adopted families whose
optional modify-string semantics remain unverified. Run the same live omit/explicit-empty/set experiment used
for `modify_alert`, `modify_filter`, and `modify_tls_certificate` against `modify_role`, `modify_group`,
`modify_tag`, and `modify_schedule`, covering at least `Name` and `Comment` and every other optional text
field for which an empty value could plausibly clear or reset stored state (`ICalendar` included). Historical
plain-string fields are not evidence that omission and explicit empty are equivalent. Add narrow
`PointerOverrides` and request goldens only where live gvmd evidence proves a distinction. `alert`, `filter`,
and `tls_certificate` need no repeat: `modify_alert.name/comment`, `modify_filter.name/comment/term`, and
`modify_tls_certificate.name/comment` are already proven and overridden, while `modify_filter.type` and
`modify_tls_certificate.copy` were tested and showed no distinction.

**`port_list` adopted:** `review_only` dropped from `internal/codegen/manifest.json` and `hack/generate.sh`
written into `commands/{create_port_list,get_port_lists,modify_port_list}.go`, matching the golden output
reviewed above byte-for-byte (the family has no cross-command shared types, so there is no
`port_list_shared.go`). Downstream call sites updated for the breaking rename: `GetPortListsResponse`'s
repeated element is now `PortList` (was `PortLists`, which is now the `{Start,Max}` pagination wrapper) and
its count field is a non-pointer `PortListCount` (was `*PortListCount`) — fixed in
`commands/pagination/portlists.go`'s `NewPortListIterator`, `examples/basic_usage/main.go`, and
`client/client_test.go`'s mock responses. `TestPortListsIterator_TotalNilCount`
(`commands/pagination/iterator_factory_test.go`) was deleted rather than updated: it existed to prove nil-count
handling, which no longer type-checks now that the field is a value, the same call made when `ticket` was
adopted. `TestValidatePortList*` confirms the committed `commands/` package now matches the pinned
post-generation state, with `create_port_list`'s and `get_port_lists`'s 1 `SCHEMA-DEFECT` finding apiece
retained as accepted loss. `TestPortListGeneratedRequestRoundTrips`/`TestPortListGeneratedResponseRoundTrips`
(`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`) were left in place rather than removed, matching the precedent set when
`ticket` and `credential` were adopted: the request side is now genuinely redundant — the new
`internal/gmptest/cases_port_list.go` request goldens cover the same `Details`/`Targets`/`Trash` and
`modify_port_list` name/comment shapes through the real `commands` package — but the response side is not, since
no committed live/upstream fixture exercises `deprecated` or a populated `user_tags`, so the scratch test's
synthetic document remains the only coverage for those two paths. `port_list` was the only family carried in
review-only mode; `asset` is now the sole review-only family (below).

**`asset` added in review-only mode:** picked directly rather than via the rank-summing method used for
`ticket`/`credential`/`port_list` — `asset` had already scored fewer actionable findings than `port_list` in
that method's last run (11 against 12) and lost only on the graph-size/fidelity tie-breakers, so re-running
the method would not have changed the pick. Added to `manifest.json` as `review_only`, with schema snapshots
and golden output committed under
`internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_asset,get_assets,modify_asset}.*` and `commands/` left
untouched.

`create_asset` carries a single suppressed `OMITEMPTY` finding on `report/filter` (request-side
over-constraint, the same class recorded for other families' request bodies) and no actionable findings.
`modify_asset` was already conformant before generation and stays that way. `get_assets` carries the whole
family's actionable load — `EXTRA` 6, `CARDINALITY` 1, `TYPE` 4, `SCHEMA-DEFECT` 1, plus 9 suppressed
`OMITEMPTY` — and generation closes every one of them except its own 1 `SCHEMA-DEFECT` finding. Confirmed via
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidateAsset*` gate pair.

The 6 `EXTRA` findings (`hosts`, `max_hosts`, `ssh_credential`, `smb_credential`, `esxi_credential`, `tasks`
on `get_assets_response/asset`) are not generator drift: the hand-written `commands.Asset` type was modeled
on `get_assets`'s own worked examples in `vendor/XML/commands/command_get_assets.xml`, but those two
`<example>` blocks are a copy-paste bug in gvmd's upstream `GMP.xml.in` — they are byte-identical to
`get_targets`'s real worked examples (same UUIDs, same `hosts`/`max_hosts`/credential fields, even a bogus
`tasks="1"` request attribute `get_assets` never declares), just with `target`/`get_targets_response`
mechanically renamed to `asset`/`get_assets_response`. `get_assets`'s actual `<pattern>` block declares none
of these six fields. `internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/get_assets/*`'s `meta.json` already flags this
(`"byte_faithful": false`); that transcription is correct, upstream's documentation is not. Generation
follows the real schema pattern and drops all six, closing the findings.

The `CARDINALITY` finding is `identifiers/identifier`: the hand-written type declared it a slice, but the
vendored pattern is a bare `<e>identifier</e>` with no `<any>` wrapper — the convention this schema file uses
elsewhere for genuine repetition (e.g. `get_assets_response`'s own top-level `<any><e>asset</e></any>`).
Generation emits the schema-correct singular `AssetIdentifier` field, closing the finding. A live gvmd
26.36.1 probe against a single real host asset returned exactly one `<identifier>`, consistent with the
schema; if a multi-identifier asset ever surfaces, `encoding/xml` silently keeps only the last decoded
element into a scalar field, so this is worth revisiting if live evidence ever contradicts the schema, but
there is none today.

Three of the four `TYPE` findings close mechanically: `asset/in_use` and `asset/writable` become
`commands.GMPBool`, and `asset/host/severity/value` becomes `float64`. The fourth,
`asset/host/routes/route/host/@distance`, is the recurring aliased-`"number"` defect (`number` is never
defined in the vendored schema, so `internal/schema` treats it as an alias for `integer`): it converts to
`*int` but remains flagged as `SCHEMA-DEFECT` after generation rather than closing outright, the same
treatment given to this defect class elsewhere.

**Accepted loss — `identifier/source/name`:** the schema types `identifiers/identifier/source/name` as
`boolean`, but a live gvmd 26.36.1 probe (`get_assets` against a real host asset) returned
`<source id="..."><type>User</type><data></data><deleted>0</deleted><name>admin</name></source>` — a real
username, not a boolean. `commands.GMPBool.UnmarshalXML` never errors on unrecognized text; it decodes any
non-`"0"` string as `true`, so the username is silently discarded rather than merely mis-typed. No override
mechanism can fix the type: `TypeOverrides` is global by schema type name and would corrupt every legitimate
boolean sharing that type, and `FieldOverrides` cannot retype a field the schema already declares. The hazard
is instead surfaced to callers directly: `CommandWarnings` previously only attached to a request struct's doc
comment (`emitRequest`), so it could not warn about a response-only field like this one. It now also accepts
a `"<command>_response"` key, attached by `emitResponse` to the `*Response` struct's doc comment
(`internal/codegen/generate.go`); `CommandWarnings["get_assets_response"]` uses it to document this finding
directly on `GetAssetsResponse`. Neither of
`get_assets`'s two committed live fixtures (`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_assets/{001,002}_response.xml`)
exercises this path — both were captured against an asset-less gvmd, so they only pin the response envelope.
Re-running the `-capture` harness to add a proper populated fixture was judged disproportionate for this
pass, since it touches every other family's live fixtures and mutates the shared docker gvmd instance;
instead this is pinned by a synthetic fixture (embedding the live-observed `admin` value) in
`TestAssetGeneratedResponseRoundTrips` (`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`), alongside the populated
`identifiers`, `host`/`routes`/`detail`, and `os`/`hosts` paths those two live fixtures leave untouched.
`TestAssetGeneratedRequestRoundTrips` covers `create_asset`'s `asset`/`report` choice branches, `get_assets`'s
`IgnorePagination`/`Details` `*GMPBool` wire states, and `modify_asset`'s comment-set/comment-omitted shapes.

`modify_asset`'s optional `Comment` was tested live (gvmd 26.36.1) against the running instance, per the same
policy applied to `alert`/`filter`/`tls_certificate`/`ticket`/`credential`/`port_list`. It behaves like
`modify_role`/`modify_group`/`modify_port_list`, not `modify_alert`: omitting `<comment>` clears the stored
value rather than leaving it unchanged, confirmed twice (an omitted-comment modify, and an explicit-empty
`<comment></comment>` sent via raw XML). Since omission and explicit-empty produce the identical cleared
result, there is no nil-vs-pointer-to-empty distinction for `PointerOverrides` to express; instead this is
recorded as a `CommandWarning` on `modify_asset` (`internal/codegen/overrides.go`), the same treatment given
to `modify_role`/`modify_group`/`modify_port_list`.

**`asset` adopted:** `review_only` dropped from `internal/codegen/manifest.json` and `hack/generate.sh`
written into `commands/{create_asset,get_assets,modify_asset}.go`, matching the golden output reviewed above
byte-for-byte. Downstream call sites updated for the breaking renames documented above: `CreateAsset.Asset`
is now `*Asset` (was `*AssetInput`); `GetAssetsResponse`'s repeated element is now `Asset []GetAssetsAsset`
(was `Assets []Asset`, and `Assets` is now the `{Start,Max}` pagination wrapper); `AssetCount`,
`AssetFilters`, and `AssetSort` are non-pointer values; and the whole `identifiers`/`host`/`os` subtree was
renamed for schema fidelity as described above (`AssetIdentifierSource`→`AssetSource`,
`AssetIdentifierOS`→`AssetOS`, `AssetHostSeverity`→`AssetSeverity`, `AssetHostDetail`→`AssetDetail`,
`AssetHostRoute(s)`→`AssetRoute(s)`, `AssetOSHost`→`AssetHostsAsset`, `AssetUserTag(s)`→`AssetTag`/its
`.Tag` field). Fixed in `commands/pagination/assets.go`'s `NewAssetIterator`,
`commands/pagination/{assets_test.go,iterator_mocks_test.go,iterator_factory_test.go}`, and
`client/{client_test.go,client_asset_test.go}`'s mock responses and assertions.
`TestAssetsIterator_TotalNilCount` (`commands/pagination/iterator_factory_test.go`) was deleted rather than
updated, the same call made for `port_list`'s `TestPortListsIterator_TotalNilCount`: it existed to prove
nil-count handling, which no longer type-checks now that `AssetCount` is a value. Adopting also collided an
unrelated hand-written type: `commands/create_target.go`'s `target`-family `AssetHosts` (the `<asset_hosts>`
element) shared a bare name with the newly generated `asset`-family `AssetHosts` (the `<os><hosts>` wrapper),
the first cross-family Go symbol collision any adoption has hit — the generator's naming-collision tracking
is per-family and has no visibility into hand-written files for families not yet in the manifest. Renamed the
hand-written type to `TargetAssetHosts` (the `CreateTarget.AssetHosts` field name is unchanged, since Go field
and type names occupy separate namespaces); `target` itself remains hand-written and out of scope for this
adoption. `TestValidateAsset*` confirms the committed `commands/` package now matches the pinned
post-generation state, with `get_assets`'s 1 `SCHEMA-DEFECT` finding (the aliased-`"number"` `@distance`
field) retained as accepted loss. `TestAssetGeneratedRequestRoundTrips`/`TestAssetGeneratedResponseRoundTrips`
(`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`) were left in place rather than removed, matching the precedent set for
`ticket`/`credential`/`port_list`: no committed live fixture exercises the `identifier/source/name` boolean
hazard or a populated `identifiers`/`host`/`os` subtree, so the scratch tests' synthetic documents remain the
only coverage for those paths. `asset` was the last family carried in review-only mode until
`report_format` (below).

**`report_format` added in review-only mode:** re-ran the rank-summing method over the 7 remaining
un-adopted create/get/modify families (`config`, `report_config`, `report_format`, `scanner`, `target`,
`task`, `user`), as T-9's `Next` field called for once T-15 and T-16a's blocking scope landed. Combined
source lines: `report_format` 143, `report_config` 150, `user` 211, `target` 235, `task` 265, `scanner` 273,
`config` 278. `report_format` and `report_config` tied on rank sum (9 each, against `user` 10, `config` 14,
`target` 15, `task` 16, `scanner` 19) — both carry 1 SCHEMA-DEFECT, and `report_format`'s larger actionable
total (31 against 28) is offset by its stronger fidelity confirmation (3 findings against 1). The documented
fixture-completeness tie-break decides it: `report_format` has 2 live fixtures with recorded requests and 5
upstream, `report_config` 1 live and 3 upstream. `user` was next-best when `port_list` was selected and
ranks third now; the two report families displaced it purely on graph size. Added to `manifest.json` as
`review_only`, with schema snapshots and golden output committed under
`internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_report_format,get_report_formats,modify_report_format}.*`
and `commands/` left untouched.

Two generator defects surfaced in this round, both fixed before any output was reviewed on its merits.

*Mixed-provenance gate compared the clock.* T-16a's batch check compared whole `schema.Provenance` values,
including `ExtractedAt`. Adding a family re-extracts only that family's snapshots — deliberately, since
T-16a also taught `-update` to preserve committed timestamps rather than rewrite all 42 files — so the very
first adoption after T-16a presented a batch of two timestamps over one unchanged schema and failed closed
on it. The gate now compares via `sameSchemaSource` (`internal/codegen/generate.go`), which zeroes
`ExtractedAt` and leaves `SourceSHA256`/`ParsedSHA256` — the fields that actually identify the schema — to
decide. `TestCheckProvenanceAllowsDifferentExtractionTimes` pins the behaviour so a future tightening cannot
quietly restore it; `TestCheckProvenanceRejectsMixedBatch` still covers the real case.

*`<alts>`-only text content was dropped.* `internal/schema/parse.go`'s mixed-content detection required a
non-empty `Type`, but `<alts>` constrains an element's text without naming a type. An element whose text was
described only by an alt list, alongside an `<attrib>` or `<e>`, therefore parsed as having no character
data at all. Two nodes in the corpus hit this, both in `get_report_formats`:
`report_format/trust` (`<alts>` yes/no/unknown beside a `name` attrib and a `time` child) and
`report_format/param/type` (7 alts beside `min`/`max`/`options`). On the wire the live fixtures show
`<trust>yes<time>…</time></trust>` — generation would have silently discarded the trust verdict, which the
hand-written `ReportFormatTrust` models correctly. The condition now accepts either a `Type` or a non-empty
`Alts`. The second oracle confirms it independently: the conformance checker had been reporting the
hand-written `,chardata` field as an `EXTRA` finding ("schema declares no character data here"), a false
positive from the same parse bug, and `get_report_formats` drops from 30 findings to 29 with the corpus
total going 512 → 511. No adopted family contains such a node, so this is a fix rather than a migration; the
42 existing snapshots re-extract byte-identically. `<alts>` still generates a plain `string` rather than a
typed enum — that remains T-19.

`create_report_format` carries only its 1 `SCHEMA-DEFECT` finding, the same `unsupported-r` class
`create_port_list` carries: the schema's own `<r>get_report_formats</r>` cross-reference inside the request
pattern, which survives generation untouched. Allowlisted in `DefectAllowlist` on that precedent.
`modify_report_format` carries 1 suppressed `OMITEMPTY` and no actionable findings; generation restructures
only its `param` child, which gains a family-local `ModifyReportFormatParam` instead of borrowing
`get_report_formats`' unrelated `ReportFormatParam` — a hand-written type reuse that had the two commands
sharing a struct whose fields neither schema declares in common. `get_report_formats` carries the family's
whole actionable load — MISSING 18, EXTRA 2, CARDINALITY 1, KIND 2, TYPE 6, plus 14 suppressed OMITEMPTY —
and generation closes all of it: request gains `filter`/`filt_id` and the
`trash`/`alerts`/`params`/`report_configs` attrs, with `details` moving from `*int` to `*GMPBool`; the
response gains `owner`, `permissions`, `user_tags`, `alerts`/`invisible_alerts`,
`report_configs`/`invisible_report_configs`, and the `param` subtree the schema declares; `filters/keywords`
gains the structured `ReportFormatKeywords`/`ReportFormatKeyword`; `writable`/`in_use`/`active`/`predefined`/
`configurable` become `GMPBool` and `deprecated` `*GMPBool`; and the wrapper/list collision resolves as
usual, `ReportFormats{Start,Max}` being the pagination wrapper and `ReportFormat` the repeated element —
the hand-written `GetReportFormatsResponse` has this backwards today, naming its slice `ReportFormats`.
Confirmed via `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidateReportFormat*` gate pair.

Two `EXTRA` findings on the hand-written model needed a decision rather than a note, and both are resolved
here rather than deferred to adoption.

*`report_format/comment` is kept, via `FieldOverrides`.* The schema's `report_format` pattern never declares
`<comment>`, but gvmd emits one on every report format: x5 in each of
`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_report_formats/{001,002}_response.xml` and x1 upstream, all recorded as
`DROPPED` in the matching `_fidelity.txt`. Generating the schema faithfully would have discarded current
server output and regressed the hand-written API, so this takes the evidence-backed exception path built for
`tls_certificate`'s `valid`: a `FieldOverride` at `get_report_formats:/report_format` keeps the field, and
the `EXTRA` finding comes back `Suppressed` carrying the fixture citation in its `Reason` instead of
counting against `commands/`. Every captured occurrence is empty, so what the fixtures evidence is the
element's *presence*, not its content — which is exactly why the override sets no `omitempty`: that would
re-marshal to an absence gvmd never sends. `TestReportFormatCommentRoundTrips` decodes all three fixtures,
asserts the cited occurrence counts still hold, and proves the element survives a decode/marshal round trip.

*`sort/order` is not a loss at all.* An earlier draft of this section recorded it alongside `comment` as
dropped, which was wrong. The schema puts `<order>` under `<field>` and the wire agrees
(`<sort><field>name<order>ascending</order></field></sort>`); the generated model preserves the value at
`Sort.Field.Order`. Only the hand-written *placement* — `order` as a sibling of `field` — was wrong, which
is why it reports as `EXTRA` against the hand-written side. Nothing is accepted as lost here, and the ledger
should not record one.

A generic, evidence-backed override mechanism for schema-omitted character data now exists —
`FieldOverride`/`FieldOverrides` (`internal/codegen/overrides.go`), built for `tls_certificate`'s `valid` and
certificate-chardata findings above. The same pass should make one explicit decision about applying it to
the *other* schema-omitted character data still recorded as accepted fidelity loss: count totals on `role`,
`group`, `tag`, `schedule`, `alert`, and `filter`; `sort/field` text on all but `role` where applicable; and
the related `note`/`override` schema/example text contradictions. These differ from `valid`/certificate in
one respect worth weighing first: they are bare informational text (a total, a sort-field name) rather than
a real protocol field with its own semantics, so modeling them may not carry the same value even though the
mechanism to do so now exists. Either keep strict schema-only generation and retain the committed evidence
baselines for these, or extend `FieldOverrides` to cover them too — do not add family-specific handwritten
patches either way. `tag`'s historical request shapes (extra children under `<resource>` and the older
direct `<resource>` form) remain a separate compatibility question and should be revisited only if support
for older gvmd wire variants becomes an explicit requirement.

**`report_format` adopted:** `review_only` dropped from `internal/codegen/manifest.json` and
`hack/generate.sh` written into `commands/{create_report_format,get_report_formats,modify_report_format}.go`,
matching the golden output reviewed above byte-for-byte. Downstream call sites updated for the breaking
renames documented above: `GetReportFormatsResponse`'s repeated element is now `ReportFormat []ReportFormat`
(was `ReportFormats []ReportFormat`, and `ReportFormats` is now the `{Start,Max}` pagination wrapper);
`ModifyReportFormat.Param` is a family-local `*ModifyReportFormatParam`, no longer borrowing
`get_report_formats`'s `ReportFormatParam`; `Files`→`File []ReportFormatFile`; `Signature` moves from
`*string` to plain `string`; `Trust` moves from a bare value to `ReportFormatTrust{Value, Name, Time}`; and
`Writable`/`InUse`/`Active`/`Predefined`/`Configurable` become `GMPBool`. Fixed in
`client/{client_test.go,client_report_test.go}`'s mock responses and assertions. Adopting also collided an
unrelated hand-written type, the same class of issue `asset` hit above: `commands/get_reports.go`'s
`report`-family `<report_format>` child (a bare Name/Value pair, unrelated to the report_format family
itself) shared the bare name `ReportFormatParam` with the newly generated type. Renamed the hand-written
pair to `ReportContentFormat`/`ReportContentFormatParam`, since `report` remains hand-written and out of
scope for this adoption. `TestValidateReportFormat*` confirms the committed `commands/` package now matches
the pinned post-generation state. `internal/gmptest/cases_report_format.go` added on the `asset` precedent,
covering `create_report_format`'s name/copy branches, `get_report_formats`'s five `*GMPBool` attrs each in
unset/true/false, and `modify_report_format`'s param-set/param-omitted shapes; fidelity and conformance
corpora regenerated accordingly (`internal/gmptest/testdata/FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`,
`internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md`).

**`report_config` added in review-only mode:** the tie-break loser when `report_format` was selected
above, and its natural follow-on. Added to `manifest.json` as `review_only`, with schema snapshots and
golden output committed under
`internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_report_config,get_report_configs,modify_report_config}.*`
and `commands/` left untouched.

One generator defect surfaced before any output was reviewed on its merits: `fileFor`'s shared-file
name for a multi-word family lowercased the already Go-cased `family` string
(`strings.ToLower("ReportConfig")`), producing `reportconfig_shared.go` instead of
`report_config_shared.go`. Single-word families (`role`, `tag`, `asset`, …) never round-tripped through
a case change and so never exposed it. Fixed by keeping the schema's own pre-`goName` snake_case form
(`familyNoun`, `internal/codegen/generate.go`) alongside the Go-cased `family`, and having `fileFor` use
that instead of lowercasing the Go name back down.

`create_report_config` (MISSING=1, EXTRA=4) and `modify_report_config` (MISSING=1, EXTRA=3) both carry a
genuine hand-written defect, not a schema quirk: the shared hand-written `ReportConfigParam` put a bare
`UsingDefault bool` attribute on `<param>` itself, spelled with the response side's `using_default`, and
carried separate top-level `Default`/`Type` fields the request schema never declares. The request-side
schema instead nests a *differently-spelled* `use_default` attribute one level down, on `<value>` —
and gvmd's own bundled examples confirm the schema is right here, not the hand-written code:
`internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/create_report_config/001_request.xml` sends a bare `<value>10</value>`
with no attribute at all, and `internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/modify_report_config/001_request.xml`
sends `<value use_default="1"/>` — exactly the nesting and spelling the generated
`ReportConfigParam`/`ReportConfigValue` (`report_config_shared.go`) now produces, with `Default`/`Type`
correctly dropped since no request fixture or schema pattern supports them. This is the mirror image of
the `get_report_configs` finding below: there, generation matches the schema and disagrees with the
wire; here, it matches both. `modify_report_config`'s schema separately carries no id or other
target-selector attribute for the `<pattern>` — a real gap, not a hand-written omission: the same
`001_request.xml` example sends the identical shape, with no way to address which `report_config` is
being modified. Resolved with a live gvmd 26.36.1 experiment run against the local
`hack/docker-compose.yml` stack: `<modify_report_config report_config_id="00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000">`
against a nonexistent id returned `404 Failed to find Report Config '...'` — a resource-lookup failure,
not a parse rejection, reproduced on a second fresh connection — proving gvmd requires and accepts an
undocumented `report_config_id` attribute following the standard `<command>_id` convention every other
`modify_X` command uses. Sending `id` instead of `report_config_id` crashed gvmd's request-handling
process outright (a SIGSEGV, confirmed via the container's own backtrace through libglib's markup
parser). Since the schema-undeclared field is on the command's own top-level request envelope rather
than a nested struct, this also widened `emitRequest` to consult `FieldOverrides` for the first time
(previously only `emitStruct` did); `FieldOverrides["modify_report_config:/modify_report_config"]` adds
`ReportConfigID` accordingly, and `CommandWarnings["modify_report_config"]` records the `id`-attribute
crash hazard on the generated type's own doc comment.

`get_report_configs` carries the family's actionable load — MISSING 12, EXTRA 2, KIND 2, TYPE 2, plus 15
suppressed OMITEMPTY and 1 `SCHEMA-DEFECT` — and generation closes all but the defect: the pagination
wrapper is renamed from the hand-written `ReportConfigPagination` to `ReportConfigs` on the usual
`{Start,Max}` convention; `ReportConfigCount{Filtered,Page}` moves from attributes to elements, matching
the schema; and `Writable`/`InUse` become `GMPBool`. The one surviving `SCHEMA-DEFECT` is
`report_config/report_format/permissions`, an empty `<pattern/>` with no declared content — allowlisted
in `DefectAllowlist` on the same precedent as `create_report_format`'s `unsupported-r` cross-reference.
Confirmed via `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidateReportConfig*` gate pair.

That clean result hides a real hazard the schema-conformance oracle cannot see, because it only compares
generated Go against the schema and never against actual wire data. The schema's `<pattern>` declares
`using_default` as an attribute of `<value>`, nested two levels under `<param>`. Every documented and
captured example instead sends it as an attribute of the enclosing `<param>` itself —
`command_get_report_configs.xml`'s own `<example>`, and independently,
`internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/get_report_configs/001_fidelity.txt`'s
`TRUNCATED /get_report_configs_response/report_config/param/@using_default` entry. Because the generated
shape matches the schema's own (wrong) declared nesting exactly, `schema.Comparer` reports zero findings
for it — a clean count that is not proof of wire accuracy here. Decoding a real response with the
generated `ReportConfigParam.Value.UsingDefault` field left it permanently `false`. Fixed, not just
documented: a new paired-table mechanism, `FieldSuppressions` (removes a schema-declared
attribute/element from one struct) and `FieldOverride.RelocatedFrom` (marks an addition elsewhere as
that removal's replacement), validated bidirectionally by `validateFieldRelocations` so deleting either
half fails the build for every family, not just this one.
`FieldSuppressions["get_report_configs:/report_config/param/value"]` removes `using_default` from
`ReportConfigParamValue`; `FieldOverrides["get_report_configs:/report_config/param"]` adds it to
`GetReportConfigsParam` instead, where the evidence actually places it. `schema.Comparer` now honestly
reports the schema's own (wrong) declared position as one `MISSING` finding on `get_report_configs` —
`CatMissing` has no suppression mechanism, unlike `CatExtra`'s `gmpoverride` tag — surfacing the
previously-hidden truth rather than hiding it behind a clean count.
`TestReportConfigUsingDefaultDecodesOnGeneratedParam` (`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`) decodes
`internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/get_report_configs/001_response.xml` through the actual generated
`GetReportConfigsResponse` type (not a private probe struct) and asserts both recorded `using_default`
occurrences decode correctly onto `Param[i].UsingDefault`, alongside the existing
`TestReportConfigUsingDefaultOnParamAgreesWithFixture` fixture pin.

Tested, not deferred: whether `modify_report_config`'s `Name`/`Comment` need `PointerOverrides` entries
to distinguish omitted from explicit-empty follows the same question `modify_alert` and `modify_filter`
each resolved with a live experiment — deferred earlier in this review for lack of a reachable server,
now run against the local `hack/docker-compose.yml` stack once it was confirmed reachable. The selector
question above had to resolve first, and did. The Name/Comment question itself could not: this
environment's gvmd ships five report formats, all `configurable=false`, and `get_report_configs`
returns zero pre-existing configs, so there is no valid `report_config` to create or modify against.
Fabricating a custom configurable report format via raw `create_report_format` XML was considered and
deliberately not attempted, given the demonstrated crash fragility (the `id`-attribute segfault above)
relative to a scratch experiment's value. Recorded as a confirmed environmental blocker via
`CommandWarnings["modify_report_config"]`, narrower than the original "no live gvmd reachable" deferral.
The obstruction differs from `modify_credential`'s `certificate`/`community`/`password` fields above
(there, a real resource exists but its response never echoes the field back) — here, no valid resource
exists to target at all — but both land in the same "cannot observe, not untested-by-choice" category.

**`report_config` adopted:** `review_only` dropped from `internal/codegen/manifest.json` and
`hack/generate.sh` written into
`commands/{create_report_config,get_report_configs,modify_report_config,report_config_shared}.go`,
matching the golden output reviewed above byte-for-byte. Downstream call sites updated for the breaking
renames documented above: `ModifyReportConfig.Params`→`Param []ReportConfigParam`, each entry's flat
`UsingDefault bool` replaced by `Value ReportConfigValue{Value, UseDefault *GMPBool}`;
`GetReportConfigsResponse.ReportConfigs []ReportConfig`→`ReportConfig []ReportConfig` (`ReportConfigs` is
now the `{Start,Max}` pagination wrapper); `ReportConfig.Owner`/`Permissions`/`ReportFormat` move from
pointers to plain values, `Permissions.Permissions`→`Permission`, and `ReportFormat` becomes the
family-local `GetReportConfigsReportFormat{ID, Name, Permissions}`; `ReportConfig.Params`→
`Param []GetReportConfigsParam`, with `Type`/`Value`/`Default` restructured into
`ReportConfigType{Value, Min, Max}`, `ReportConfigParamValue{ReportFormat}`, and
`ReportConfigDefault{Value, ReportFormat}`, `Options` moving from `[]string` to `*ReportConfigOptions`,
and `UsingDefault` relocating onto `<param>` itself per the `FieldSuppressions`/`FieldOverrides` pair
described above. Fixed in `client/{client_test.go,client_report_test.go}`'s mock responses and
assertions; no naming collisions surfaced elsewhere in `commands/`. `TestValidateReportConfig*`
confirms the committed `commands/` package now matches the pinned post-generation state.
`internal/gmptest/cases_report_config.go` added on the `report_format` precedent, covering
`create_report_config`'s name/copy/param-set branches, `get_report_configs`'s `Trash`/`Details`
`*GMPBool` attrs each in unset/true/false, and `modify_report_config`'s param-set/param-omitted shapes
(every case sets `ReportConfigID`, now required per the live-gvmd evidence above); fidelity and
conformance corpora regenerated accordingly (`internal/gmptest/testdata/{FIDELITY-SUMMARY,
REQUEST-FIDELITY}.txt`, `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md`) — round-tripping the real
`get_report_configs` fixtures through the generated types now recovers `report_config_count`,
`filters`, `sort`, and the pagination wrapper the hand-written type previously dropped, and both
`create_report_config`/`modify_report_config`'s recorded requests round-trip clean.

**`user` added in review-only mode:** the named front-runner per this entry's own tracker note above,
picked directly rather than re-running the rank-summing method, since neither the candidate set nor the
underlying schema changed since the last round. Added to `manifest.json` as `review_only`, with schema
snapshots and golden output committed under
`internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_user,get_users,modify_user,user_shared}.*` and
`commands/` left untouched.

`create_user` (EXTRA 2, CARDINALITY 1, OMITEMPTY 1) and `modify_user` (EXTRA 1, CARDINALITY 1, OMITEMPTY
1) both close entirely on generation, and every finding traces to the schema, not a hand-written mistake:
`command_create_user.xml`'s own `<role>` pattern declares only `@id`, no nested `<e>name</e>`, and the
upstream fixture confirms gvmd is only ever sent `<role id="..."/>`, never a name — the hand-written
`UserRole.Name`/`UserGroup.Name` fields are the EXTRA findings, dropped by generation. `sources/source` is
genuinely schema-singular-optional despite the hand-written `[]string` — the CARDINALITY finding — and
`hosts/@allow` gains `omitempty` (the OMITEMPTY finding) and, as a side effect of the standing
`TypeOverrides["boolean"]` rule, moves from `string` to `*commands.GMPBool`.

`get_users` carries the family's whole actionable load (MISSING 1, EXTRA 2, CARDINALITY 2, KIND 2, TYPE
2, SCHEMA-DEFECT 2, plus 25 suppressed OMITEMPTY) and generation closes everything but the two
SCHEMA-DEFECT findings: the request gains `FiltID` (`@filt_id`, previously unmodelled — the MISSING
finding); `filters/comment` and `truncated` (EXTRA, no wire evidence in any of the 3 live + 1 upstream
fixtures) are dropped; `groups/group` narrows from a slice to the schema's declared single required
element while `role` widens to a slice on the same `<any>`-wrapper convention `User`'s own repeated
`<user>` element already uses (CARDINALITY); `user_count`'s `filtered`/`page` move from attributes to
elements, matching the schema's own `<ele>` declarations (KIND); `in_use`/`writable` become `GMPBool`,
closing the exact lexical-boolean loss `internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_users/{001,002,003}_fidelity.txt`
record as `CHANGED .../writable/#text "1" -> "true"` (TYPE); and the 25 suppressed OMITEMPTY findings
close outright rather than staying suppressed, because several hand-written response fields
(`filters`, `sort`, `users`, `user_count`) were pointers with `omitempty` where the schema declares them
required — generation matches the schema's true cardinality instead of the conservative hand-written one.
The two surviving SCHEMA-DEFECT findings — empty `<pattern/>` on `role/permissions` and
`groups/group/permissions` — are the same recurring class already allowlisted for `tag`, `schedule`,
`alert`, `filter`, `permission`, `credential`, `port_list`, and `report_config`; added to
`DefectAllowlist` on that precedent, no new mechanism needed. Confirmed via
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidateUser*` gate pair.

A live gvmd 22.7 experiment (`hack/gvmd-up.sh`'s stack; created a throwaway user with the built-in `User`
role, probed each field via `RawXML`, read `comment`/`new_name` back through `get_users`, and read
`password` back indirectly through a fresh authentication attempt, since `get_users` never echoes it)
resolved `modify_user`'s deferred presence-semantics question during review instead of leaving it for
adoption. `comment` behaves like `modify_alert`'s: omitting the element leaves the stored value
unchanged, `<comment></comment>` clears it to `""` — a real distinction a bare `string`+`omitempty` could
never send — so `PointerOverrides["modify_user.comment"]` was added and `Comment` regenerated as
`*string`. `new_name` did not distinguish: an explicit-empty `<new_name></new_name>` returns `200 OK` but
leaves the name unchanged, indistinguishable from omitting the element entirely, so it keeps the
mechanical `string` default. `password`'s existing `*UserPassword{Modify *GMPBool, Value string}` shape
already models every legal wire state structurally: omitting the whole `<password>` element,
`modify="0"`, and `modify="1"` with empty chardata all left the stored password unchanged in this gvmd
version; only `modify="1"` with real chardata changed it — so no override was needed there either. One
observed anomaly worth recording for anyone testing password rotation against this exact stack: after a
successful `modify="1"` rotation, the *prior* password kept authenticating alongside the new one (a
genuinely wrong password still failed both before and after), while a deliberately-wrong password never
succeeded at any point in the sequence — this is stack behavior local to this gvmd/pg-gvm instance, not a
wire-shape or generator concern, so no code change follows from it. Beyond the one `PointerOverride`, no
`FieldOverride`, `FieldSuppression`, or `CommandWarning` entries were needed for this family — unlike
`report_config`, no fixture or live-gvmd evidence surfaced a schema/wire mismatch of the
`using_default`/selector-attribute kind here.

**Flagged for adoption time, not resolved here:** the mechanical wrapper/list naming convention (the
`report_format`/`report_config` precedent: `ReportFormat []ReportFormat` field + `ReportFormats{Start,Max}`
wrapper) produces `User []User` + `Users{Start,Max}` here — cleaner than the hand-written `Users []User`
+ `UserUsers{Start,Max}` workaround it would replace, with no actual name collision either way.

**`user` adopted:** `review_only` dropped from `internal/codegen/manifest.json` and `hack/generate.sh`
written into `commands/{create_user,get_users,modify_user,user_shared}.go`, matching the golden output
reviewed above byte-for-byte. Downstream call sites updated for the breaking renames documented above:
`CreateUser.Roles []*UserRole`→`Role []UserRole`, with a new local `UserGroups{Group []UserGroup}`/
`UserGroup{ID}` pair replacing the old `Groups`/`UserGroup{ID,Name}` shape; `ModifyUser.Comment` becomes
`*string` per the `PointerOverrides` entry above, and `ModifyUser.Role` gets the same
`Roles []*UserRole`→`Role []UserRole` rename; `UserPassword.Text`→`Value`, `Modify string`→`*GMPBool`.
`GetUsersResponse.Users []User`→`User []User` (`Users` is now the `{Start,Max}` pagination wrapper,
resolving the "flagged for adoption" naming question above exactly as predicted — cleaner than the
hand-written `UserUsers` workaround it replaces); `Filters`/`Sort`/`UserCount` move from pointers to
plain values; `Truncated` is dropped; each `User`'s `Owner`/`Permissions`/`Groups`/`Sources`/`Hosts` move
from pointers to plain, family-local response-side types (`GetUsersGroups`, `GetUsersSources`,
`GetUsersHosts`) distinct from the create/modify-side `UserGroup`/`UserSources`/`UserHosts` types, and
`Role []GetUsersRole` replaces the singular `*UserRole` with a slice of a distinct response-side type
carrying `Name`/`Permissions` the request-side `UserRole` never had. Fixed in
`internal/gmptest/{cases_user.go,redact_test.go,capture_sequence_test.go}` and
`client/{client_test.go,client_user_test.go}`; `client.go`/`client/client.go` needed no changes —
`GetUsersRaw`/`GetUsers` already mirrored the already-adopted `GetTicketsRaw`/`GetTickets` split and only
pass whole struct pointers. `TestValidateUser*` confirms the committed `commands/` package now matches
the pinned post-generation state; fidelity and conformance corpora regenerated accordingly
(`internal/gmptest/testdata/{FIDELITY-SUMMARY,REQUEST-FIDELITY}.txt`, the `get_users` live/upstream
fixtures, the `create_user`/`get_users`/`modify_user` request goldens, `internal/schema/testdata/
CONFORMANCE.md`) — `create_user` and `modify_user` both move to fully conformant, and `get_users` closes
to only the two allowlisted `SCHEMA-DEFECT` findings.

**`target` added in review-only mode:** picked directly out of rank order rather than by re-running the
rank-summing method — on the last pass (see the `report_format` entry above) `config` ranks higher
(`user` 10, `config` 14, `target` 15, `task` 16, `scanner` 19), but this round targeted `target`
specifically rather than continuing the automated ranking. Added to `manifest.json` as `review_only`,
with schema snapshots and golden output committed under
`internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_target,get_targets,modify_target,target_shared}.*`
and `commands/` left untouched.

`create_target` (EXTRA 17, suppressed OMITEMPTY 12) and `modify_target` (EXTRA 19, suppressed OMITEMPTY
10) both close entirely on generation: the hand-written credential/port-list sub-structs
(`TargetSSHCredential`, `TargetSMBCredential`, and their siblings) carry `Name`/duplicate fields the
schema's corresponding `<pattern>`s declare only `@id` (and, for the two ssh variants, `@port`) for — the
EXTRA findings dropped by generation — and several fields the hand-written struct marks required gain
`omitempty`, matching the schema's own `<o>` wrapper (the OMITEMPTY findings).

`get_targets` carries the family's whole actionable load (EXTRA 10, KIND 3, MISSING 12, TYPE 5,
SCHEMA-DEFECT 9, plus 53 suppressed OMITEMPTY) and generation closes everything but the nine
SCHEMA-DEFECT findings: `target_count`'s `filtered`/`page` and `user_tags`'s `count` move from
attributes to elements matching the schema's own `<ele>` declarations (KIND); seven credential/port-list
sub-structs gain the `trash` element the hand-written shared `TargetCredential` type never carried,
alongside `filters/keywords`, `sort`'s character data and its nested `field` structure, `tasks/task/name`,
and `user_tags/tag/comment` (MISSING); `writable`/`in_use`/`reverse_lookup_only`/`reverse_lookup_unify`/
`allow_simultaneous_ips` become `GMPBool` where the hand-written code used a bare `bool` (TYPE); the
hand-written nested `TargetPortRange` struct is replaced by the schema's plain `port_range` string, the
`port` field five of the six credential kinds never carry on the wire is dropped from the shared
`TargetCredential` type (each kind now gets its own family-local struct instead), and a spurious
`Comment` on `TargetFilters`, `Order` on `TargetSort`, `@count` on `TargetTasks`, and a top-level
`Truncated` are all dropped (EXTRA). No generator defect was found in the process — every divergence
traced directly to the schema's own Card/Kind values.

The nine `SCHEMA-DEFECT` findings are mostly familiar, but one is new territory. Eight are the recurring
empty-`<pattern/>` class already allowlisted for `tag`/`schedule`/`alert`/`filter`/`permission`/
`credential`/`port_list`/`report_config`/`user`. The ninth is a defect class no prior family surfaced:
`get_targets`'s `<permissions>` pattern references the same `<e>permission</e>` token twice inside one
`<pattern>`, rather than colliding two *definitions* of the same name (the pre-existing
`DefectDuplicateEle` class). Fixed at the parser level — `internal/schema/parse.go`'s `refs`/`nested`/
`child` now thread a `seen map[string]bool` so a second reference to an already-visited element name
within the same pattern is reported as the new `DefectDuplicateRef` class instead of silently duplicating
the child in the output tree. All nine findings are allowlisted in `overrides.go`'s `DefectAllowlist`,
matching `internal/codegen/testdata/schema/get_targets.json`'s own `Defects` array byte-for-byte.
Confirmed via `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidateTarget*` gate pair.

Naming: `initialisms` (`internal/codegen/generate.go`) gained `esxi`→`ESXI`, `ips`→`IPs`, and
`lsc`→`LSC` — each scoped to this family alone by corpus-wide grep — producing `TargetESXICredential`,
`AllowSimultaneousIPs`, and `TargetSSHLSCCredential`/`TargetSMBLSCCredential`/`TargetESXILSCCredential`
instead of the mechanical `Esxi`/`Ips`/`Lsc` default.

A live gvmd 26.36.1 experiment (created a throwaway target with unique `hosts`/`exclude_hosts`/
`port_range`, probed each `modify_target` field via `RawXML`, and read `comment`/`name`/`hosts`/
`exclude_hosts` back through `get_targets`) resolved `modify_target`'s presence-semantics question during
review rather than deferring it to adoption. `comment` behaves like `modify_alert`'s: omitting the
element leaves the stored value unchanged, `<comment></comment>` clears it to `""` — so
`PointerOverrides["modify_target.comment"]` was added and `Comment` regenerates as `*string`. `name` did
not distinguish the same way: an explicit-empty `<name></name>` is rejected outright (`400 Name must be
at least one character long`) rather than accepted as a clear, so it keeps the mechanical `string`
default, with the rejection recorded in a `CommandWarnings["modify_target"]` doc comment instead. `hosts`
and `exclude_hosts` have no legal standalone empty state a pointer could express at all: gvmd requires
both present together whenever either is touched (`400 HOSTS requires an EXCLUDE_HOSTS` / `400
EXCLUDE_HOSTS requires a HOSTS`), even when the omitted field's stored value is not changing, and rejects
both explicit-empty too (`400 HOSTS must be at least one character long`) — also recorded in the same
warning. `hosts_ordering` accepted every value tried but could not be verified via read-back:
`get_targets`'s response never echoes it, so the omit-vs-clear question is unanswerable from this client
alone, noted in the warning on the `modify_credential` precedent for untestable fields.

**`target` adopted:** `review_only` dropped from `internal/codegen/manifest.json` and `hack/generate.sh`
written into `commands/{create_target,get_targets,modify_target,target_shared}.go`, matching the golden
output reviewed above byte-for-byte. Downstream call sites updated for the breaking renames documented
above: `CreateTarget.Name` loses `omitempty` and several fields reorder; the shared credential/port-list
sub-structs narrow to family-local, per-command-scoped types (`TargetSSHCredential`,
`TargetSSHElevateCredential`, `TargetKrb5Credential`, `TargetSMBCredential`, `TargetESXICredential`,
`TargetSNMPCredential`, `TargetSSHLSCCredential`, `TargetSMBLSCCredential`, `TargetESXILSCCredential` in
`target_shared.go` for create/modify, plus `modify_target`'s own narrower `ModifyTargetSSHCredential`/
`ModifyTargetSSHLSCCredential`), each now just `{ID}` (some also `Port`); `ModifyTarget.Comment` becomes
`*string` per the `PointerOverrides` entry above. `GetTargetsResponse.Filters`/`Sort`/`Targets`/
`TargetCount` move from pointers to plain values (`Targets`, formerly `TargetTargets`, is the
`{Start,Max}` pagination wrapper); `TargetFilters` gains `Keywords TargetKeywords{Keyword
[]TargetKeyword{Column,Relation,Value}}`; `TargetSort` gains chardata `Value` and a nested `Field
TargetField{Order}`; each `Target`'s credential fields become distinct family-local response types
(`GetTargetsSSHCredential` and siblings, each carrying `ID`/`Name`/`Port` where applicable/`Trash
GMPBool`/`Permissions string`); `Writable`/`InUse`/`ReverseLookupOnly`/`ReverseLookupUnify`/
`AllowSimultaneousIPs` become `GMPBool`; `PortRange` becomes a plain string, dropping the nested
`TargetPortRange` struct; `Truncated` is dropped entirely; `TargetTasks` drops its `Count` attribute,
keeping only `Task []TargetTask{ID,Name,Permissions}`. Fixed in `internal/gmptest/cases_target.go`
(`Comment` wrapped with the shared `ptr()` helper), `commands/pagination/targets.go` (drops the nil-check
on the now-value `TargetCount`), `commands/pagination/iterator_mocks_test.go` and `client/client_test.go`
(drop the `&` on `TargetCount` literals), and `commands/pagination/iterator_factory_test.go`, which drops
`TestTargetsIterator_TotalNilCount` outright on the `port_list` precedent — a value-typed `TargetCount`
has no nil case left to test. `TestValidateTarget*` confirms the committed `commands/` package now
matches the pinned post-generation state; fidelity and conformance corpora regenerated accordingly
(`internal/gmptest/testdata/FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`, the `get_targets` live/upstream fixtures, the
`create_target` request goldens, `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md`) — `create_target` and
`modify_target` both move to fully conformant, `get_targets` closes to only the nine allowlisted
`SCHEMA-DEFECT` findings, and the two `get_targets` upstream fixtures move from `DECODE-ERROR` to `ok`
now that the hand-written type's cardinality mismatches no longer block decoding.

**`task` added in review-only mode:** the next family in un-adopted rank order after `target` (see the
`report_format` entry above: `user` 10, `config` 14, `target` 15, `task` 16, `scanner` 19) — `config` and
`scanner` remain, in that order, for the next round. Added to `manifest.json` as `review_only`, with
schema snapshots and golden output committed under
`internal/codegen/testdata/{schema,golden}/{create_task,get_tasks,modify_task,task_shared}.*` and
`commands/` left untouched.

`create_task` (CARDINALITY 1, EXTRA 15, MISSING 1, suppressed OMITEMPTY 4) and `modify_task` (EXTRA 12,
MISSING 1) both close entirely on generation. All three commands share one hand-written set of nested
types (`TaskConfig`, `TaskTarget`, `TaskScanner`, `TaskAlert`, `TaskSchedule`, `TaskObservers`,
`TaskPreferences`) shaped for `get_tasks`' rich response — reused verbatim on the request side, they carry
`Name`/`Trash`/`Type` fields the request-side `<pattern>`s never declare (bare `@id` only), which is most
of both commands' EXTRA counts. `create_task`'s CARDINALITY finding is `Alert`: singular `*TaskAlert` in
the hand-written struct against the schema's repeatable element (`[]TaskAlert` in the golden). Both
commands' MISSING findings trace to `<observers>`: the schema puts the observer list directly in the
element's character data, but the hand-written `TaskObservers.UserList` models it as a separate
`<user_list>` child instead (also counted among `create_task`'s EXTRA total); `modify_task` is separately
missing the schema's `<file>` element entirely — no hand-written equivalent of the golden's
`TaskFile{Value, Name, Action}` exists.

`get_tasks` carries the family's whole actionable load (CARDINALITY 7, EXTRA 14, MISSING 9, TYPE 8,
SCHEMA-DEFECT 10, plus 7 suppressed OMITEMPTY) and generation closes everything but the ten SCHEMA-DEFECT
findings: `Writable`/`InUse`/`Alterable` become `GMPBool` where the hand-written code used a bare `bool`
(TYPE, closing the same lexical-boolean loss `user`'s `in_use`/`writable` did); `SchedulePeriods` moves
from a hand-written `*TaskSchedulePeriods{Period []TaskSchedulePeriod}` container to the schema's plain
`*int` (TYPE); `ResultCount` moves from `int` to the schema's declared `text` (TYPE); `Scanner.Type` moves
from `string` to the schema's declared integer (TYPE); `ReportCount` gains the schema's `{Value, Finished}`
structure instead of a bare `int` (MISSING); `UserTags{Count, Tag[]}` is modeled for the first time
(MISSING); `Config`/`Target`/`Scanner`/`Alert` each gain the `permissions` element the four allowlisted
empty-pattern defects declare, empty of content (MISSING); and `CurrentReport`/`LastReport` are
differentiated instead of collapsed into one shared, under-detailed `TaskReport`: `CurrentReport.Report`
stays the lightweight `{ID, Timestamp}` the schema declares for it, while `LastReport.Report` gains the
`ScanEnd`/`ResultCount` (9 severity buckets)/`Severity`/`ComplianceCount` (4 buckets) breakdown the
hand-written type never carried for either report (MISSING/CARDINALITY). The hand-written `Reports
*TaskReports` field has no schema counterpart anywhere in `get_tasks`' pattern tree and is dropped outright
(EXTRA) — nothing in the corpus or fixtures suggests it was ever a real wire field.

The ten `SCHEMA-DEFECT` findings split into two already-established classes: six are `duplicate-ref` — the
same class `target` introduced this round (`task/last_report/report/result_count/{hole,info,warning}`,
`task/name`, `task/schedule`, `task/schedule_periods`, each a repeated `<e>` reference inside one
`<pattern>`) — and four are the recurring empty-`<pattern/>` class already allowlisted for eight other
families (`alert/permissions`, `config/permissions`, `scanner/permissions`, `target/permissions`, all
under `/get_tasks_response/task/`). All ten are allowlisted in `overrides.go`'s `DefectAllowlist`, matching
`internal/codegen/testdata/schema/get_tasks.json`'s own `Defects` array byte-for-byte. Confirmed via
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`'s `TestValidateTask*` gate pair.

Naming: `get_tasks`' response needs richer per-command types than the family's shared, request-side
minimal shapes (`TaskConfig{ID}` and siblings in `task_shared.go`), so the generator's naming-collision
convention renames the response-side types with the command prefix rather than colliding names:
`GetTasksConfig`, `GetTasksTarget`, `GetTasksScanner`, `GetTasksAlert`, `GetTasksObservers`, and
`GetTasksSchedule`, each carrying `Name`/`Trash`/`Permissions` (and, for `Scanner`, `Type int`) the shared
minimal types never have.

A live gvmd 26.36.1 experiment (created a throwaway container task and a throwaway second user via
`create_user`, probed `comment`/`name`/`observers` via `RawXML`, and read the result back through
`get_tasks`) resolved `modify_task`'s deferred presence-semantics question during review rather than
leaving it for adoption. `comment` behaves like `modify_alert`'s: omitting the element leaves the stored
value unchanged, `<comment></comment>` clears it to `""` — so `PointerOverrides["modify_task.comment"]`
was added and `Comment` regenerates as `*string`. `observers` behaves the same way once given a real
value — an arbitrary chardata string is rejected outright (`400 User name error`; the element's text must
be an existing username) — so `PointerOverrides["modify_task.observers"]` was added and `Observers`
regenerates as `*string` too. `name` did not distinguish: an explicit-empty `<name></name>` returns `200
OK` but is a no-op, indistinguishable from omission — the same behavior `user`'s `new_name` showed, not
`target`'s outright rejection — so it keeps the mechanical `string` default.

**`task` adopted:** `review_only` dropped from `internal/codegen/manifest.json` and `hack/generate.sh`
written into `commands/{create_task,get_tasks,modify_task,task_shared}.go`, matching the golden output
reviewed above byte-for-byte. Downstream call sites updated for the breaking renames documented above:
`CreateTask.Config`/`.Target`/`.Scanner` move from hand-written, pointer/optional `*TaskConfig`/
`*TaskTarget`/`*TaskScanner` (each carrying a `Name`/`Trash`/`Type` the request-side pattern never
declares) to the schema-required value types `TaskConfig`/`TaskTarget`/`TaskScanner{ID}` in the new
`task_shared.go`; `CreateTask.Alert` moves from a singular `*TaskAlert` to `[]TaskAlert`, matching the
schema's repeatable element; `ModifyTask.Comment`/`.Observers` become `*string` per the
`PointerOverrides` entries added above. `GetTasksResponseTask` renames to `Task` (no collision in
`commands/`), `GetTasksResponseTaskCount` renames to `TaskCount`, and `Task.Progress` moves from `int` to
`*int`; the response side's richer per-command types land as `GetTasksConfig`/`GetTasksTarget`/
`GetTasksScanner`/`GetTasksAlert`/`GetTasksObservers`/`GetTasksSchedule`/`GetTasksPreferences`,
disambiguated from the family-shared minimal request types by the generator's command-prefix convention.
Fixed in `commands/pagination/tasks.go` (`TaskIterator` now over `*commands.Task`),
`internal/gmptest/cases_task.go` (drops `&` on the now-value `Config`/`Target`/`Scanner` literals),
`examples/basic_usage/main.go` (`GetTask`'s return type, the `CreateTask` literal, and
`getTaskProgress` dereferencing the new `*int` `Progress`), `commands/pagination/iterator_mocks_test.go`
and `pagination_test.go` (mechanical `Task`/`TaskCount` rename), `client/client_task_test.go` and
`client/client_test.go` (`Comment` wrapped in a local variable and address-of, the mock's comparison
nil-checked to match `user`'s existing pattern), and `internal/gmptest/capture_sequence_test.go` — the
one fix `go build`/`go vet`/`go test` all missed, since this file sits behind `//go:build integration`
and only `golangci-lint run ./...` typechecks it (this repo's `.golangci.yml` already sets
`build-tags: integration`): the container-task lifecycle scenario's `Target` literal drops its `&`, and
its `modify_task` comment moves to a local variable plus `&`.

`TestValidateTask*` confirms the committed `commands/` package now matches the pinned post-generation
state; fidelity and conformance corpora regenerated accordingly (`FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`,
