  project's practice of not folding unrelated cross-cutting fixes into one batch.
- **`git diff --check` flagged `get_schedules/003_response.xml`'s CRLF.** The schedule fixture legitimately
  embeds an iCalendar block (`\r\n` line endings per RFC 5545), which a byte-faithful capture preserves.
  Added `.gitattributes` marking `internal/gmptest/testdata/live/**/*_response.xml -whitespace` rather
  than normalizing the captured bytes.
- **Comment bloat in `capture_sequence_test.go`.** Trimmed the multi-paragraph doc comments on
  `lifecycleReportConfig`, `captureContainerReport`, and `reportFormatIDByName` down to one sentence each,
  and removed `firstConfigurableReportFormatID`'s (it only restated the signature). The fuller reasoning
  for each lives in this section instead.

The alert and `delete_report` fixes needed three full recaptures in total before landing: the first two
verified the alert-lifecycle fix and ruled out the ordering hypothesis for `delete_report` (see above), and
the third is what's committed — a fully coherent alert lifecycle (create/get/modify/test/delete all from one
run), `create_task/001`+`002` correctly evidenced as a rejected/accepted pair, and `delete_report/001` landing
on `404` this time, consistent with the now-documented intermittent race rather than a regression. Full suite
(`go build`, `go vet`, `gofmt -l`, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`, `go test -tags integration ./...`) is
green against this recapture.

#### Selector/presence `delete_X` cohort (2026-08-17)

`delete_asset`, `delete_target`, `delete_task`, `delete_user` are adopted — one command added to each of
their existing family's manifest entry (`asset`, `target`, `task`, `user`), no new family.

`delete_target`/`delete_task` are a genuine **breaking API change**, not a no-op: the hand-written
`Ultimate *GMPBool` (pointer, `omitempty`) becomes generated `Ultimate GMPBool` (non-pointer, no
`omitempty`) — the same conversion the 16-command batch's `Ultimate` fix already made elsewhere, dropping
the unused unset state from the Go API. Every call site passing `commands.Bool(true/false)` for
`Ultimate` had to change to a bare `true`/`false` literal (`examples/basic_usage/main.go`,
`internal/gmptest/capture_sequence_test.go`). The wire encoding for a populated value is unchanged
either way (`ultimate="1"`/`"0"`), so this is an API break with no behavioral difference for callers that
were already setting the field.

**Review found a real regression in the first pass of this batch, since fixed.** `delete_asset`/
`delete_user` initially regenerated their `<or>`-selector fields (`@asset_id`/`@report_id`;
`@user_id`/`@name`/`@inheritor_id`/`@inheritor_name`) *without* `omitempty`, on the reasoning that the
schema's `Card` marks every choice branch required. Live recapture exposed why that reasoning was wrong:
`DeleteAsset{ReportID: "..."}` marshaled `asset_id=""` explicitly alongside the real `report_id`, and gvmd
treated the empty `asset_id` attribute as the selected branch — `delete_asset_response status="404"
status_text="Failed to find asset ''"` — not the intended report selector. `delete_user`'s equivalent
request happened to return `200` because gvmd's user-lookup evidently prefers `user_id` over the other
three empty branches, but that is not evidence the other three selectors (`name`, `inheritor_id`,
`inheritor_name`) work with their siblings sent empty — it's the same bug not yet triggered.

Root cause: `internal/schema.Node.InChoice` documents that a field reached through an `<or>` has
"advisory" cardinality — the schema marks each branch `Card: CardRequired` in isolation, but at most one
branch is ever present — and `internal/schema/conform.go`'s `checkOmitEmpty` already accounted for this
(suppressing the OMITEMPTY finding with `"inside <or>; only one branch is ever present"`). The generator
never had the matching half: `internal/codegen/generate.go`'s `fieldOf` decided `omitempty` from `n.Card`
alone, so a `CardRequired` node lost its `omitempty` regardless of `InChoice`. Fixed by folding `InChoice`
into the same branch as `CardOptional` in `fieldOf` — an `<or>`-reached field now gets `omitempty` (and a
pointer, where applicable) exactly like a genuinely optional one, matching what `checkOmitEmpty` already
assumed. Checked against every currently-adopted family's schema snapshot: `delete_asset`/`delete_user`
are the only two commands in scope with a `CardRequired`+`InChoice` node, so the fix has zero effect
elsewhere in `commands/` today (it will also apply correctly once `create_report`/`get_reports`, which
have the same `InChoice` shape in their hand-written code, are eventually adopted).

Post-fix, `delete_asset`/`delete_user` regenerate with `omitempty` on every `<or>`-selector field —
byte-identical to the hand-written struct tags — and `CONFORMANCE.md` again reports them via the
suppressed-OMITEMPTY verdict rather than a false "conformant". Recapture confirms the fix:
`delete_asset/001_request.xml` now reads `<delete_asset report_id="...">` (no `asset_id` at all) and
resolves `200`; `delete_user/001_request.xml` reads `<delete_user user_id="...">` and resolves `200`.

`internal/gmptest/cases_target.go`/`cases_task.go` keep a `true`/`false` `RegisterRequestCase` pair for
`Ultimate` (dropping only the now-invalid `_unset` case), rather than removing presence coverage
entirely — a deliberate departure from the already-adopted 16 delete_X commands' precedent of no dedicated
`Ultimate` case at all, kept here so the required-`GMPBool` encoding and the always-present `ultimate`
attribute have an explicit wire golden for these two commands.

Recapture also regenerated `REQUEST-FIDELITY.txt`, `FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`, and `CONFORMANCE.md`, and
surfaced one more instance of the by-now-familiar literal-value drift: a freshly recaptured credential
fixture's UUID changed `TestCredentialGeneratedResponseRoundTrips`'s hardcoded want string in
`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`. Full suite (`go build`, `go vet`, `gofmt -l`, `golangci-lint run`,
`go test ./...`, `go test -tags integration ./...`) is green against this recapture.

#### Sibling batch: `sync_config`/`test_alert`/`verify_report_format`/`verify_scanner` (2026-08-17)

The 4 remaining single-command siblings named above are adopted — one command added to each of their
existing family's manifest entry (`config`, `alert`, `report_format`, `scanner`), no new family.

Two new SCHEMA-DEFECT classes surfaced, both first occurrences of their class in the allowlist:
`sync_config`'s request is a bare `<pattern/>` (`empty-pattern`, zero fields at all), and
`test_alert_response`'s schema references `<e>status_details</e>` with no `<ele>`/`<elem>` resolving it in
scope (`dangling-e`) — an upstream schema scoping quirk, not an adoption risk. Both allowlisted with a
reason citing this review.

`sync_config` fails against this pinned gvmd build: a live capture returns `<gmp_response status="400"
status_text="Bogus command name"/>`. That response is only evidence that this specific runtime rejects the
request; it does not by itself establish why. A plausible, explicitly unconfirmed explanation is upstream
deprecation in favor of openvasd-based scanners (the `overrides.go` DefectAllowlist entry hedges the same
way, "likely reflecting"). The schema still declares the command, so per this project's practice of
prioritizing accurate wire behavior over papering over an inconvenient result, adoption proceeds on the
schema's declared (empty) shape and the observed failure is recorded here rather than hidden or
special-cased.

`verify_scanner`'s response `<pattern>` declares only `status`/`status_text`; the hand-written type also
carried `Version string \`xml:"version,omitempty"\`` (visible only in the schema's `<example>`, never its
`<pattern>`). An ad hoc live probe against two real scanners on the local stack confirmed this is real,
populated wire data (`OpenVAS 23.50.0`, `GVM/26.36.1`) — dropping it on adoption would be a behavior
regression, not a defect fix. This stack's committed `verify_scanner` fixture exercises a scanner with
nothing behind it (`503`, no `<version>`), so the field's authenticity rests on that separately-recorded
probe evidence rather than on the checked-in corpus.

Preserving `Version` needed a small generator change: `emitResponse` (`internal/codegen/generate.go`)
previously never consulted `FieldOverrides` — only `emitRequest` did, a scope limit the `FieldOverride` doc
comment already flagged as pending its own evidence. Widened `emitResponse` to consult `FieldOverrides`
symmetrically with `emitRequest`, and added the `verify_scanner` entry. The existing `gmpoverride` tag
mechanism (`generate.go`'s `writeField`, recovered by `schema/gomodel.go`, consumed by
`schema/conform.go`) suppresses the resulting EXTRA finding on both sides of the comparison —
`CONFORMANCE.md`'s totals move from 91/258 to 92 fully-conformant/257 actionable, exactly this one
suppression. `generate_test.go` gained focused coverage for the new path directly (not just via
`verify_scanner`'s golden): a response-envelope override is emitted with its XML and `gmpoverride` tags,
one colliding with a schema-declared response child is rejected, and a stale/mistyped response-keyed entry
fails closed via `checkFieldOverridesConsumed`.

`verify_report_format` is fully conformant, zero findings — the simplest of the 4.

No new live capture run was needed: `capture_sequence_test.go` already exercised all 4 commands under its
"expected failures" step before this adoption began (the harness captures across adopted and
not-yet-adopted commands alike), and the committed fixtures under
`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/{sync_config,test_alert,verify_report_format,verify_scanner}` already
decode correctly against the newly generated types — confirmed by a clean `go test ./internal/gmptest/...`
with no `-update` diff. Full suite (`go build`, `go vet`, `gofmt -l`, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`)
is green.

#### `task` lifecycle: `move_task`/`resume_task`/`start_task`/`stop_task` (2026-08-17)

The 4 remaining `task` lifecycle commands are adopted into the existing `task` family — no new
family. All 4 were already clean or only carried the generic, already-suppressed `OMITEMPTY`
finding class (request-side or response-side over-constraint), per `CONFORMANCE.md`: `move_task`
was fully conformant; `resume_task` tagged its response `report_id` `omitempty` despite the schema
marking it required; `start_task` and `stop_task` each tagged their request `task_id` `omitempty`
despite it being a required attribute. Regeneration corrected `start_task`'s and `stop_task`'s
request `task_id`: those really are always populated (the client always names a task), so dropping
`omitempty` is a pure tightening.

`resume_task`'s and `start_task`'s response `report_id` is different, and a follow-up review caught
the difference: regeneration's first pass dropped `omitempty` there too, on the same "match the
schema's required-ness" reasoning, but the two commands' own committed live fixtures
(`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/{resume_task,start_task}/001_response.xml`) are genuine `400`/`500`
error responses that omit `report_id` entirely. A non-`omitempty` `ReportID` fabricates
`<report_id></report_id>` on re-encode in exactly that case — a real behavior change, not the
"pure tightening" this section originally (and incorrectly) claimed for all three fields. This is
status-dependent presence (present on success, absent on failure), not unconditional requiredness.

Fixed with a new `RequiredOverride` mechanism (`internal/codegen/overrides.go`,
`internal/codegen/generate.go`'s `fieldOf`): keyed like `PointerOverride`
(`"<command>.<field>"`), it lets a specific schema-required field keep `,omitempty` when live
evidence contradicts the schema's declared requiredness — a gap neither `PointerOverride` (optional
fields only) nor `FieldOverride` (add-only, collides with a schema-declared field) could fill.
`resume_task.report_id` and `start_task.report_id` are its only two entries, each citing the
fixture that proves the omission. `commands/resume_task.go` and `commands/start_task.go` now tag
`ReportID` `xml:"report_id,omitempty"` again.

No new live capture run was needed, for the same reason as the sibling batch above:
`capture_sequence_test.go`'s `expectedTaskFailures` already exercises all 4 commands, and the
committed fixtures decode cleanly against the regenerated types. `start_task`'s per-fixture fidelity
file — which had shown `ADDED report_id` even before this adoption began, a pre-existing defect this
work surfaced rather than introduced — is clean again; `resume_task`'s never needed to change, since
its `ADDED report_id` was this adoption's own transient regression, not a committed state.
`FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`/`CONFORMANCE.md` were regenerated to match (`suppressed findings` 440→439:
`start_task`'s and `stop_task`'s request-side `task_id` resolve for good, while `resume_task`'s and
`start_task`'s response-side `report_id` each keep one deliberately-suppressed `OMITEMPTY` finding).
Full suite (`go build`, `go vet`, `gofmt -l`, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`,
`go test -tags integration ./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `RequiredOverride` fail-closed hardening (2026-08-17)

A second review round on the mechanism above found it did not match the project's fail-closed
override policy: `fieldOf` consulted `g.ro` with no shape validation and no consumption tracking,
so a typo'd key (e.g. `resume_task.reportid`), an entry aimed at an optional/nested/nonexistent
field, or a stale entry surviving a later schema correction would all silently do nothing while
generation still succeeded. Separately, the override's pointer-conversion branch
(`goType = "*" + base` for children or `pointerScalars` types) reshaped any required numeric,
boolean, or structured field into a pointer whenever named — a cardinality/API decision with no
evidence behind it; only the string `report_id` case has ever been justified.

Fixed both, following the same idiom already used for `FieldOverrides`/`DefectAllowlist`:
`validateRequiredOverrides` rejects a malformed `"<command>.<field>"` key or empty `Reason`
independent of which command is in scope; a new `roConsumed` map on `gen` is set when a
`RequiredOverrides` entry actually matches a required top-level field, and
`checkRequiredOverridesConsumed` fails the build if an entry whose command was in scope for this
`Generate()` call never matched — catching the typo/stale/wrong-cardinality cases the review named.
Separately, `fieldOf`'s `CardRequired` branch now rejects (rather than silently reshapes) any
override target that isn't a plain string leaf, since a non-string scalar or a structured field
would need a pointer to stay distinguishable from its zero value — a bigger, unevidenced change
this mechanism does not take on; the pointer-conversion code path was removed rather than
generalized.

Direct tests were added for both: `TestFieldOfRequiredOverride` (exact match keeps `omitempty` and
marks `roConsumed`; an unrelated required field and a different command's identically-named field
are both untouched) in `generate_test.go`; `TestValidateRequiredOverridesRejectsBadEntries`,
`TestValidateRequiredOverridesAcceptsCommittedTable`,
`TestCheckRequiredOverridesConsumedRejectsStaleEntry`,
`TestCheckRequiredOverridesConsumedRejectsWrongCardinality`,
`TestCheckRequiredOverridesConsumedIgnoresOutOfScopeEntries`, and
`TestRequiredOverrideRejectsUnsupportedTargets` in `overrides_test.go`. `commands/task_lifecycle_report_id_test.go`
adds direct XML round-trip tests proving `ResumeTaskResponse`/`StartTaskResponse` decode an
error response's absent `report_id` as empty and don't fabricate the element on re-encode, and
correctly round-trip a populated `report_id` on success. No generated output changes for the two
existing, valid entries (`resume_task.report_id`, `start_task.report_id`): both are plain string
fields, so the new rejection path never triggers for them. Full suite (`go build`, `go vet`
default + `integration` tags, `gofmt -l`, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`,
`go test -tags integration ./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `port_range`: `create_port_range`/`delete_port_range` (2026-08-17)

A new family, 2 commands. `delete_port_range` was already schema-conformant going in — a required
`port_range_id` uuid attribute, plain status/status_text response — and adopts as a clean drop-in
with zero findings.

`create_port_range` carried a live-confirmed defect: the schema declares `port_list` as a required
element with one child, a required uuid `id` attribute (`<port_list id="uuid"/>`), but the
hand-written `CreatePortRange.PortListID string \`xml:"port_list"\`` sent it as text content instead
(`<port_list>uuid</port_list>`). `CONFORMANCE.md` recorded this as `MISSING request
/create_port_range/port_list/*` before this adoption. It wasn't just a schema nuance —
`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/create_port_range/001_request.xml` showed gvmd rejecting the
hand-written shape outright (`400 "A START, END and PORT_LIST ID are required"`), and
`capture_sequence_test.go`'s `lifecyclePortList` had been carrying a `sendWithFallbacks` raw-XML
workaround — send the typed struct, let it fail, then retry with hand-built `<port_list id="…"/>`
XML — with a comment flagging the defect, ever since `port_list` was adopted.

Regeneration fixed it the same way this shape has resolved for every prior family it's shown up in
(`permission`, `target`, `ticket`, `port_list`, `filter`): the schema's declared child becomes a
nested struct, `PortList PortRangePortList \`xml:"port_list"\`` with `PortRangePortList{ID string
\`xml:"id,attr"\`}`, no override table entry needed. `lifecyclePortList` now sends the typed struct
directly and drops the fallback entirely; the recaptured `create_port_range/001_request.xml` is a
single clean, accepted request with no retry.

No new `SCHEMA-DEFECT` classes and no generator changes. Full live-fixture recapture (the whole
corpus, not just this family, per the harness's shared-lifecycle-state design) was run to capture
`delete_port_range` for the first time and to re-capture `create_port_range` cleanly; it surfaced one
unrelated collateral UUID change in a shared credential fixture, fixed by updating
`TestCredentialGeneratedResponseRoundTrips`'s hardcoded expected string in
`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`. `CONFORMANCE.md` reports both commands conformant.
`check_test.go`'s obsolete-file stand-in, previously `delete_port_range.go` (safe only while
`port_range` had no manifest family), moved to `delete_report.go`. Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build`,
`go vet` default + `integration` tags, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`,
`go test -tags integration ./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `report`: `create_report`/`delete_report`/`get_reports` (2026-08-17)

A new family, 3 commands, the largest and most structurally complex T-9 adoption so far:
`create_report`/`get_reports` both reach into the schema's `report` type — ~1683 lines of XML, the
biggest nested structure in the whole schema, recursively self-referential (`delta.report` nests
another `report`) and itself embedding `result`, the schema's other largest shared type (via
`results.result`). `delete_report` was already schema-conformant going in — a required `report_id`
uuid attribute, plain status/status_text response — and adopts as a clean drop-in.

The hand-written `commands/get_reports.go` (333 lines) had hand-collapsed most of this nesting into
plain scalar strings (NVT severities/refs, result QOD/delta/detection/notes/overrides/tickets, host
detail/port_count/result_count, TLS cert detail) and hand-declared two schema-invalid request
attributes, `Extension`/`ContentType` — not real `get_reports` attributes, alongside only 5 of the
schema's ~19 valid optional ones. Regeneration replaced all of it: `commands/report_shared.go`
(801 lines, ~35 types) is now the dedup home for the shared `report`/`result`/`nvt`/... tree,
`create_report.go` shrank to 31 lines and `get_reports.go` to 86. Per this project's alpha-API
policy, the shape changes are breaking: `GetReports.Extension`/`ContentType` are gone,
`GetReportsResponse.Reports []Report` is now `Report []ReportReport` (schema-derived plural),
`CreateReport.Report` is a value not a pointer, and `CreateReport.Task`'s type renamed
(`ReportTask` → `CreateReportTask`, since the response side needed the bare `ReportTask` name for a
different shape). `client/client_report_test.go` and `client/client_test.go` were updated to the
new shapes.

Three points of interest fell out of the shared-type dedup, none of them generator bugs:

- **A genuine schema self-inconsistency, not schema drift.** The schema declares the same element
  name `cvss_base` with two different types depending on nesting: `integer` at
  `results/result/nvt/cvss_base`, `severity` (float64) at `errors/error/nvt/cvss_base`. Real wire
  data sends decimals (`5.0`) for the first path —
  `internal/gmptest/testdata/live/create_report/001_request.xml` and
  `.../upstream/get_reports/002_response.xml` both do — which made `strconv.ParseInt` fail against
  the schema-faithful `int` the generator produced. None of the existing override mechanisms fit:
  `TypeOverrides` is keyed by the schema `<type>` name globally, too broad since `integer` is used
  correctly everywhere else; `FieldOverride` adds a field the schema doesn't declare, and reusing it
  on an existing schema-declared name trips `schemaChildCollision`'s hard failure;
  `FieldSuppression`/`RelocatedTo` is built strictly for moving a field between nesting levels, and
  its own paired-key discipline means using it to retype in place (same key on both halves) hits the
  same collision check. Added a new, minimal override table for exactly this shape:
  `FieldTypeOverride`/`FieldTypeOverrides` (`internal/codegen/overrides.go`) — keyed like
  `FieldOverrides`/`FieldSuppressions`, corrects an existing schema-declared field's Go type in
  place without touching presence, position or wire name. Threaded through
  `Generate`/`GenerateAll`/`CheckOutput`/`GeneratedGoRoots`/`Validate` and consulted in `fieldOf`
  right after the mechanical type mapping, so cardinality/pointer logic still runs on the corrected
  type; has its own consumed-tracking (`checkFieldTypeOverridesConsumed`) so a stale entry still
  fails the build. Two entries, one per command reaching `ReportNVT` through the shared `report`
  type. `ReportNVT.CVSSBase` is now `float64`; both fixtures now decode cleanly
  (`FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`'s `get_reports` rows: dropped 36/38 → 10/10, and the upstream side moved
  from `ok`/`ok` to `clean`/`ok`).
- **A `ReportFormat`/`ReportConfig` type-name collision**, not from this family's own schema shape
  but from `report`'s nested `<report_format>`/`<report_config>` id+name references colliding with
  the bare `ReportFormat`/`ReportConfig` entity names `report_format`/`report_config` (adopted
  earlier) already claim. Fixed with four `NameOverrides` entries
  (`ReportReportFormat`/`ReportReportFormatParams`/`ReportReportConfig`, one pair per command) —
  the generator's normal naming ladder never had a way to know about a name a sibling family
  claimed, so this is a targeted exception, not a generator defect.
- **Schema defects, all upstream, all allowlisted.** `empty-pattern` on `report/name` (no content),
  `no-pattern` on 7 `<count>` elements (`apps`, `closed_cves`, `cves`, `errors`, `hosts`, `os`,
  `ssl_certs`, `vulns` — neither `<pattern>` nor `<type>`), and `duplicate-ele` on `active` under
  both the plain and `delta`-nested `notes/note`/`overrides/override` paths — one `DefectAllowlist`
  entry per (command, class, path), same mechanism as every prior family with defects.

The 3 remaining actionable `EXTRA` findings each command shows in `CONFORMANCE.md`
(`ReportReport.Cves`, `ReportResultCount`'s chardata, `ReportResult.OCIImage`) are the same
vendored-XML-vs-live-schema version drift T-17 already documents for other families —
`get_notes`/`get_overrides` show the identical `+oci_image` drift on their own embedded `result`
type — not a new defect class, and not something an override should paper over per this project's
policy of recording drift rather than hiding it.

`get_reports`'s fidelity fixtures carry `added` counts well above every other adopted family's
(`FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`: live 001/002 `added=12`/`12`, upstream 001/002 `added=41`/`66`) — a scale
question worth confirming isn't a new defect class. Checked every cited path
(`filters/{term,phrase,notes,overrides,apply_overrides,result_hosts_only,min_qod}`,
`report_format/param/{name,value}`, `ports/@start`/`@max`, and upstream/002's `results/result/*`,
`host/*`, `nvt/*`, `task/target/*`) against `internal/codegen/testdata/schema/get_reports.json`:
all are `Card=CardRequired`. This is the same "inert on decode" OMITEMPTY category `role`/`group`/
`tag`'s adoptions also produced, just at `report`'s scale — the largest, most attribute-dense type
tree adopted so far, captured here against a synthetic container-task report and a
port/results-sparse live filter. `report/report/ports` itself, the one genuinely optional element
in this set (`Card=CardOptional`), is already a correctly `omitempty` `*ReportPorts` pointer; its
`start`/`max` attributes are schema-required only once `<ports>` is present, and the live captures
send a bare `<ports>` without them, so the `@start`/`@max` additions are the same
required-attribute-on-a-present-element shape, not a pointer-modeling gap. This review round's
`create_report` `added=56`/`70` observation is now moot: it was the pre-fix `cvss_base` decode
failure above producing a partially-decoded struct with inflated zero-valued required fields, not
an independent finding — the `FieldTypeOverride` fix makes `create_report` decode and round-trip
clean (`added=0`) on both fixtures, per the current `FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`.

Live-fixture recapture was local-only (`go test ./internal/gmptest/... -update` and
`go test ./internal/schema/... -update`, no live gvmd — see the T-16c note above on why network
recapture is currently blocked): both the `cvss_base` decode failures and the stale
`Extension`/`ContentType`/`Reports[]` shapes were exercised purely against already-committed raw
fixture XML, with no new wire capture needed. `check_test.go`'s obsolete-file stand-in, previously
`delete_report.go` (safe only while `report` had no manifest family), moved to `delete_agents.go`.
Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build`, `go vet` default + `integration` tags, `golangci-lint run`,
`go test ./...`) is green; the live-network integration run remains blocked per T-16c.

#### `trash`: `empty_trashcan`/`restore` (2026-08-18)

A new family, 2 commands, the simplest T-9 adoption so far. `restore` was already
schema-conformant going in — a required `id` uuid attribute, plain status/status_text response —
and adopts as a clean drop-in with zero findings. `empty_trashcan` takes no attributes or elements
at all and carries one upstream schema defect, `empty-pattern` on the request pattern (declares no
content) — the same generic class already carried by `note`/`override`/`create_group`, resolved
with one `DefectAllowlist` entry. No `FieldOverride`/`FieldTypeOverride`/`NameOverrides` entries
were needed; regeneration produced `commands/empty_trashcan.go` (22 lines) and `commands/restore.go`
(23 lines), byte-identical in wire shape to the hand-written originals modulo header/doc-comment
wording — zero client or test call-site changes required.

Both commands already had committed live/upstream fixtures from before this adoption
(`empty_trashcan`/`restore`, captured incidentally by the general lifecycle harness), and both
already showed `clean` in `FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`/`REQUEST-FIDELITY.txt` and zero findings in
`CONFORMANCE.md`. Since the regenerated types are wire-shape-identical to the hand-written
originals, no new capture was needed: `TestRequestRoundTrip`/`TestResponseFidelity`/
`TestConformanceReport` all pass unchanged against the existing fixtures with no `-update` required,
which is itself the evidence that this adoption didn't change wire behavior. (A full-corpus
recapture was run during this adoption to double-check, but it only touched unrelated
timestamps/UUIDs across the fixture tree — no new fidelity signal for `trash` — so it was reverted
rather than shipped; the local gvmd stack's admin credential has separately been confirmed rotated
away from the documented default, worth knowing for the next cohort that needs a live capture.)
`check_test.go`'s obsolete-file stand-in is unaffected (`delete_agents.go`, unrelated to `trash`).
Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build`, `go vet` default + `integration` tags, `golangci-lint run`,
`go test ./...`, `go test -tags integration ./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `agent`/`agent_group`: deferred, not adopted (2026-08-18)

The next candidate looked like `get_agents`/`modify_agents`/`delete_agents` — until live
introspection (`<help format="XML" type=""/>` against the pinned stack, cross-checked with
`schema.LoadServed`'s real XML parse) showed all 11 agent-controller commands
(`get_agents`/`delete_agent`/`modify_agent`/`sync_agents`/`modify_agent_control_scan_config`/
`get_agent_installer_instruction`/`get_agent_support_bundle`/`create_agent_group`/
`get_agent_groups`/`modify_agent_group`/`delete_agent_group`) genuinely absent from the served
schema, not merely renamed or restructured. A naive text search over the raw `<help>` response finds
them (full, accurate documentation exists), but they're wrapped in an XML comment
(`<!-- ... -->`); a real parser correctly treats them as not served. This is T-16c's live-only
guarantee working as designed, not a capture bug — confirmed by fully reverting the in-progress
hand-written scaffolding for both cohorts (`commands/{delete_agent,modify_agent,sync_agents,
modify_agent_control_scan_config,get_agent_installer_instruction,get_agent_support_bundle}.go` and
the `agent`/`agent_group` manifest entries) back to the pre-investigation baseline.

Root cause, confirmed against `gvmd` v26.36.1 source (the exact tag the pinned stack's image is
built from) rather than inferred from the binary alone:

- `CMakeLists.txt` gates the entire feature behind `-DENABLE_AGENTS`, defaulting to `0`, and that
  flag is literally what wraps the agent-controller docs in `<!-- -->` in the generated schema
  (`IF_ENABLE_AGENTS`/`IF_NOT_ENABLE_AGENTS` template variables). Binary symbol inspection
  independently confirmed zero `agent_controller` symbols in the pinned image's `gvmd` binary or any
  `libgvm_*.so` — the feature isn't compiled in, so the runtime `enable_agents` toggle in
  `gvmd.conf` is a no-op on this build (tested: flipped it, restarted, identical failure, reverted).
- Greenbone's own release pipeline (`.github/workflows/container.yml`, tag `v26.36.1`) builds two
  variants from every version tag: a `default` build (`ENABLE_AGENTS=0`) published to
  `registry.community.greenbone.net/community/gvmd` — the image this project mirrors and pins — and
  an `all features` build (`-DENABLE_AGENTS=1` among other toggles) published to a *different*
  repository, `registry.community.greenbone.net/openvas-scan-dev/gvmd`. So a stable, release-tagged
  build with agents enabled does exist; it's just not the one Greenbone publishes to the public
  registry.
- That second repository isn't reachable without Greenbone-internal credentials: `community/gvmd`
  goes through the normal Harbor bearer-token flow and allows anonymous pull (verified);
  `openvas-scan-dev/gvmd` returns a bare `403` from nginx before the request even reaches Harbor's
  auth layer, and a validly-issued token for it carries zero granted actions. Pulling the `edge` tag
  (`26.21.1~dev1`, an unstable dev branch) does have the feature compiled in, but adopting against an
  unstable, lower-versioned build conflicts with the project's "pin the officially mirrored stable
  image" practice (`hack/image-mirror.lock.json`, T-16c/T-22).

What the pinned `community/gvmd` image conclusively proves is narrower than "adoption is blocked
until Greenbone changes something": it proves the *normal integration stack's* runtime has
`ENABLE_AGENTS` compiled out, so *that* runtime's served schema cannot authorize this adoption, and
that the existing `get_agents`/`modify_agents`/`delete_agents` fixtures under
`internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/` were transcribed under an enabled-feature assumption this
runtime never satisfies. It does not prove agent-controller is unreachable in principle — T-16c
requires a genuinely served schema with reproducible runtime provenance, not specifically the
project's default Compose stack. A project-built gvmd image, compiled from the exact pinned upstream
revision with `-DENABLE_AGENTS=1` and mirrored under T-22's existing controls (digest-pinned,
provenance-recorded, alongside `hack/image-mirror.lock.json`), would be a legitimate agent-specific
extraction/capture environment while `hack/docker-compose.yml`'s default stack stays untouched —
i.e. Greenbone shipping this publicly is one valid unblock path, but not the only one.

**Decision: defer both cohorts, for now.** The compile-and-mirror-a-second-image path above is
viable but is new infrastructure scoped well beyond a single T-9 cohort — building and maintaining a
project-controlled feature-flagged runtime, deciding how it coexists with T-16c's "one pinned stack"
model, and getting it under T-22's provenance controls. That's a deliberate infra decision for the
user to make, not something to fold into this adoption. Absent that decision, `agent`/`agent_group`
stays deferred. Revisit either by re-running `TestSnapshotLive -update` after any pin bump — a clean
pass where `delete_agent`/`modify_agent`/etc. show up as real served commands is the signal Greenbone
enabled it publicly — or by taking up the project-built-image path as its own piece of work.

**Update (2026-08-22, T-32): removed, not merely deferred.** The stale hand-written
`get_agents`/`modify_agents`/`delete_agents` referenced throughout this closeout (and in T-24's finding
below) no longer exist in `commands/` — see T-32 for the removal rationale, inventory, and
reintroduction criteria. This section is left otherwise unedited as the historical record of why the
family was deferred in the first place; the decision it explains is a strict subset of T-32's.

#### `nvt`: `get_nvts`/`get_nvt_families`/`get_preferences`/`get_info` (2026-08-18)

A new family, 4 commands. **Headline, not cosmetic:** `get_info`'s `NVTCVE.Score` was wrong — the
schema field is `severity`, not `score`. Live fixture evidence
(`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_info/001_response.xml`) confirms this was active data loss:
real `type=cve` lookups return `<severity>5</severity>` on the wire, and the pre-adoption Go type
dropped it and fabricated a `score` that was never sent. Every consumer of `get_info type=cve` today
silently got no severity; the regenerated `NVTCVE.Severity` closes it.

`get_nvt_families`/`get_preferences` were already schema-conformant and adopted mechanically. The
one shape change worth calling out: `get_nvt_families`'s `Families` field is now a wrapper struct
(`NVTFamilies{Family []NVTFamily}`) rather than a bare slice — a genuine improvement (mirrors how the
schema actually nests `<families><family>...</family></families>`), not a defect fix, but it broke
every existing `resp.Families[i]` call site (`client/client_nvts_test.go`), fixed to
`resp.Families.Family[i]`.

`get_nvts` (`MISSING=6 EXTRA=1 CARDINALITY=7 OMITEMPTY=19 TYPE=4` going in) picked up structured
`Refs`/`Solution`/`QOD` (previously flattened to bare strings) and a previously-unmodeled
`EPSS{MaxSeverity, MaxEPSS}`; `NVTPreference.Alt` went from `string` to `[]string` (the same CARDINALITY bug
`get_preferences.go`'s own `Preference.Alt` had already fixed by hand); `Category`/`CvssBase`/
`DefaultTimeout`/`PreferenceCount`/`Timeout` became pointers per the project's usual schema-optional-int
convention; the unschemaed `Description` field was dropped. One schema/wire contradiction needed
`CardinalityOverride` rather than a mechanical fix: `command_get_nvts.xml`'s response `<pattern>`
declares exactly one `<e>nvt</e>`, contradicting its own `<summary>Get one or many NVTs</summary>` and
its own multi-`<nvt>` `<example>` — `internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/get_nvts/001_response.xml`
confirms three sibling `<nvt>` elements come back live for one unfiltered request, so the override
(`internal/codegen/overrides.go`) keeps the generated field a slice.

`get_info` needed the most structural work: added the missing `filt_id`/`filter` request attrs; split
the previously-shared `References` type into `NVTReference` (`{Value chardata, Href attr}`, the
CPE shape) and `NVTReferencesReference` (`{URL, Tags NVTTags{Tag []string}}`, the CVE shape) — their
shapes were never actually the same; added `NVTCVE.EPSS *NVTEPSS{Score, Percentile}`,
`NVTCert{Warning, CertRef []NVTCertRef{Type attr, Name, Title}}`, and the recursive
`NVTConfigurationNodes{Node []NVTNode{Operator, Negate, MatchString, Node []NVTNode}}`; added the
previously fully-missing `NVTDfnCertAdv` info type (schema-verified structurally identical to
`cert_bund_adv` — `{title, summary, severity, cve_refs, raw_data?}` — modeled as its own type rather
than assumed-shared, per the report family's cvss_base assumption-bug precedent); added `NVTCPE`'s
missing `cpe_name_id`/`severity` and removed its EXTRA legacy `Score`/`Status`; removed `NVTInfo`'s
EXTRA `Writable`/`InUse`. `configuration_nodes/node/node` is a genuine self-reference the schema can't name
(`DefectAllowlist`'s `undefined-type` class, `get_info:/info/cve/configuration_nodes/node/node`),
closed with a `FieldTypeOverride` retyping the mechanical string fallback back to the generated
`NVTNode` struct — a Go struct may reference itself through a slice field, so this is a closed-form
fix, not a hand patch.

One schema/wire contradiction surfaced only after generation, not during the hand-written pass:
`info/@id` is declared type `integer` by *both* the vendored docs and the live-captured schema, but
real `type=cve` wire data (`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_info/001_response.xml`) returns the CVE
name itself (`"CVE-1999-0001"`) as `@id`, not a number — the mechanically-correct `*int` couldn't even
decode the fixture (`strconv.ParseInt: parsing "CVE-1999-0001": invalid syntax`), a regression from the
pre-adoption hand-written `string` that generation would otherwise have introduced silently. Fixed the
same way as the report family's `cvss_base` contradiction: a `FieldTypeOverride`
(`get_info:/info`, `id`/`Attr: true` → `string`) with the wire evidence recorded in `Reason`, not a
generator or schema-capture bug. `CONFORMANCE.md` still shows this as a live `TYPE` finding
(`schema type integer, Go ID is string`) rather than suppressing it — consistent with how the
self-reference `SCHEMA-DEFECT` above is also left visible: both are deliberate, evidence-backed
departures from the schema, not defects to hide.

Two more findings surfaced post-generation that are neither codegen bugs nor schema defects: `get_info`
gained an EXTRA `web_application_vt` field and `get_nvts` an EXTRA `discovery` field, both present in
the live-captured schema but absent from the vendored static docs — T-17-style upstream drift (the same
class already documented for `get_notes`/`get_overrides`'s `+oci_image`). Recorded as expected drift,
not suppressed via an override, per this project's "record drift rather than hide it" policy.

`CONFORMANCE.md` after generation: `get_info` MISSING=12 EXTRA=7 OMITEMPTY=5 TYPE=3 → EXTRA=1 TYPE=1
SCHEMA-DEFECT=1 (the TYPE and SCHEMA-DEFECT lines are the two deliberate overrides above, not residual
defects); `get_nvts` MISSING=6 EXTRA=1 CARDINALITY=7 OMITEMPTY=19 TYPE=4 → EXTRA=1 (the `discovery`
drift) CARDINALITY=1 (suppressed, the override above) OMITEMPTY=2 (suppressed, familiar
request-side-over-constraint/inert-on-decode classes); `get_nvt_families`/`get_preferences` stay fully
conformant. `internal/gmptest`'s fidelity corpus confirms the headline fix independently: the
`severity`-DROPPED/`score`-ADDED pair that showed up on every `get_info type=cve` fixture before this
adoption is gone from `FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`, and both `get_info` live fixtures now show `decode: ok`
(one was a hard decode failure immediately after generation, before the `@id` override was added).

No new live capture was made. The existing `get_info`/`get_nvts` live and upstream fixtures already
round-trip cleanly against the regenerated types with no `-update` needed beyond the fidelity/goldens
that reflect this adoption's intended changes, which is itself the evidence the fix works; a full-corpus
`TestCaptureLive` run remains the wrong tool for a single-family adoption; it touches all ~350 fixtures
in one pass, unrelated timestamp/UUID churn included (same reasoning as `trash`'s adoption note above),
and the local gvmd stack's admin credential is still confirmed rotated away from the documented default.
Coverage the original plan wanted but didn't get — `get_info type=nvt`/`cert_bund_adv`/`dfn_cert_adv`
fixtures, and a `get_nvts details=1` capture against an NVT with populated refs/severities/epss/solution
— remains a gap for whoever next has the rotated credential to close.

`client/client_test.go`'s mock and `client/client_misc_test.go`/`client_nvts_test.go`'s assertions were
updated for every renamed type (`GetNvts`→`GetNVTs`, `Info`→`NVTInfo`, etc.) and shape change
(`Details bool`→`*GMPBool`, `Families`→`Families.Family`). A follow-up review round caught `GetNvts`
itself as miscased — `goName` matched initialisms only on whole-word tokens, missing the
initialism-plus-plural-s shape (`nvts`, `cves`, `cpes`, `epss`) that GMP schema tokens use throughout;
fixed generically in `goWord` (strip a trailing `s`, recheck the initialism table, reattach the `s`)
rather than with per-family overrides, which cascaded a mechanical rename across every already-adopted
family sharing those tokens (`get_notes`, `get_overrides`, `report_shared` included) and settled on the
final names used above (`GetNVTs`, `NVTCVEs`, `NVTMatchedCPEs`, `NVTWebApplicationVT`, `GetNVTsEPSS`).
Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build`,
`go vet` default + `integration` tags, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`, `go test -tags integration
./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `auth`: `authenticate`/`describe_auth`/`modify_auth` (2026-08-18)

A new family, 3 commands. **Headline finding:** `describe_auth`'s response `<pattern>` declares a
singular `<e>group</e>`, but live fixture evidence
(`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/describe_auth/001_response.xml`) shows 3 real sibling `<group>`
elements in one unfiltered response (`method:file`, `method:ldap_connect`,
`method:radius_connect`) — the pre-adoption hand-written `Groups []AuthGroup` already had this
right, so this adoption's job was recording the schema/wire contradiction, not fixing a bug. Same
class as `nvt`'s `get_nvts.nvt` cardinality contradiction above: a `CardinalityOverride`
(`describe_auth.group`) keeps the generated field a slice, citing the fixture.

`authenticate` needed no shape changes — `OMITEMPTY=2` on response fields `role`/`timezone` is
inert on decode (same suppressed class as elsewhere) and generation reproduces
`commands/authenticate.go`'s existing shape exactly.

`describe_auth` also picked up a genuinely-missing optional child: `auth_conf_setting`'s schema
`<pattern>` declares an optional `certificate_info` the hand-written `AuthSetting` never modeled.
Added `AuthCertificateInfo` (`TimeStatus`, `ActivationTime`, `ExpirationTime`, `Issuer`,
`MD5Fingerprint` required; `SHA256Fingerprint`, `Subject`, `Serial` optional) following the
project's family-local convention for this shared schema element (`AlertCertificateInfo`,
`ScannerCertificateInfo`, `CredentialCertificateInfo` are the precedents — never a shared type
across families). No live fixture has a populated `certificate_info` yet, so this is
schema-declared-but-unobserved, recorded as such rather than claimed as wire-confirmed. The
request side's `<pattern>` is genuinely empty (`describe_auth` takes no body beyond the envelope),
closed with the same `DefectAllowlist` `empty-pattern` class already used for `empty_trashcan`.

`modify_auth` needed its own request-local types rather than reusing `describe_auth`'s
`AuthGroup`/`AuthConfSetting`: the request-side `auth_conf_setting` only ever carries `key`/`value`,
never `certificate_info` — a real structural difference, not superficial duplication. The generator
produced `ModifyAuthGroup{Name string \`xml:"name,attr"\`, AuthConfSetting []AuthGroupAuthConfSetting}`
and `AuthGroupAuthConfSetting{Key, Value}` (the naming ladder disambiguates the request-local setting
type from `describe_auth`'s response-side `AuthConfSetting`). The schema's `<pattern>` for the request `group` element never
declares a `name` attribute at all, but the command's own `<example>` requires `<group
name="Foo">` to target a group — functionally required despite never being declared, the same
shape as `modify_report_config`'s `report_config_id` and `verify_scanner`'s `version`. Kept `Name`
on `ModifyAuthGroup` and closed the gap with a `FieldOverride`
(`modify_auth:/modify_auth/group`), citing the pattern/example inconsistency.

`CONFORMANCE.md` after generation: `authenticate` `OMITEMPTY=2` → fully conformant; `describe_auth`
`MISSING=1 CARDINALITY=1 SCHEMA-DEFECT=1` → `CARDINALITY=1 OMITEMPTY=1 SCHEMA-DEFECT=1`, all three
suppressed (the `MISSING` finding is eliminated outright by modeling `certificate_info`);
`modify_auth` `EXTRA=1` → `EXTRA=1`, suppressed via the `FieldOverride` above. Zero unexplained
findings remain across all three commands. `internal/gmptest`'s fidelity corpus confirms this
independently: the regenerated `authenticate`/`describe_auth`/`modify_auth` live fixtures all show
`decode: ok` with no DROPPED/CHANGED entries; the only `ADDED` is the expected
`role`/`timezone`-loses-`omitempty` pair on `authenticate`'s pre-auth-failure fixture.

`modify_auth` had zero live fixture coverage before this adoption. Since it mutates real gvmd auth
configuration (unlike `modify_setting`'s cosmetic-value precedent, which needs no restore), the new
`lifecycleAuth()` capture step reads `method:file`'s current settings via `describe_auth`, issues
`modify_auth` with the exact same key/value pairs back, then calls `describe_auth` a *second* time
and compares the returned key/value set against the first read — a review round caught that the
fidelity report alone (zero CHANGED/ADDED/DROPPED across the request/response XML) only proves this
library didn't lose data in transit, not that gvmd stored the write back unchanged; the second
`describe_auth` and explicit comparison is the actual no-op evidence, and `lifecycleAuth` now
`t.Fatalf`s the whole run (reporting only the differing keys, never their security-sensitive values)
if anything drifts. The comparison passed against the live stack, producing `describe_auth`'s `002`
(pre-write) and `003` (post-write) fixtures alongside `modify_auth/001`. Getting this first
`modify_auth` fixture required a full-corpus `TestCaptureLive` run (the harness has no way to scope
capture to one family); all non-`auth` fixture churn from that run, including timestamp-only
rewrites of the pre-existing `authenticate`/`describe_auth` fixture metadata, was inert (confirmed
via diff spot checks) and was reverted before this commit, per the same "full-corpus recapture is
the wrong tool for a single-family adoption" reasoning as `nvt`'s adoption note above.

`client/client_test.go`'s mocks were updated for the renamed types: the `describe_auth` mock's
`resp.Groups`/`AuthSetting` became `resp.Group`/`AuthConfSetting`, and the `modify_auth` mock's
`cmd.Group.Settings` condition became `cmd.Group.AuthConfSetting`. Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go
build`, `go vet` default + `integration` tags, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`, `go test -tags
integration ./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `get_aggregates` (2026-08-18)

A new family, the first singleton in `internal/codegen/manifest.json` — one command, no siblings.
Also this document's own worst-scoring command going in (`MISSING=5 EXTRA=5 CARDINALITY=2
OMITEMPTY=30`), so it got a real review rather than a mechanical pass.

**Request side.** The schema's `<pattern>` declares three repeatable child elements the
hand-written `GetAggregates` only ever exposed as single-value attributes: `<sort field="..."
order="..." stat="...">` (multi-criteria form, alongside the existing `sort_field`/`sort_order`/
`sort_stat` single-criterion attrs — both forms are schema-declared), and repeated `<data_column>`/
`<text_column>` elements additive to the existing `data_column` attribute. Added
`Sort []AggregateSort`, `DataColumn2 []string`, and `TextColumn []string` to carry these; no
existing field was removed, since the attribute and element forms are independent, both-real
mechanisms rather than a rename.

**Response side.** Four real gaps, closed with a mix of straight modeling and one genuine
correctness fix:

- `overall/text` (MISSING): the schema declares the same `<any><e>text</e></any>` under `<overall>`
  that `<group>` already carries: added `AggregateOverall.Text []AggregateText`, the identical shape.
- `filters` (MISSING, fidelity-confirmed — every one of the four live fixtures captured for this
  adoption shows gvmd always sending a real `<filters id="">...</filters>` block): added
  `GetAggregatesResponse.Filters AggregateFilters` plus a family-local `AggregateFilters`/
  `AggregateKeywords`/`AggregateKeyword` trio, following the same never-shared-across-families
  convention `get_tasks.go`'s `TaskFilters`/`TaskKeywords`/`TaskKeyword` already set.
- `aggregate/data_column` (CARDINALITY: schema zero-or-more, hand-written code had one string): this
  is a real correctness fix, not an override — the request side's new repeatable `<data_column>`
  (above) is genuine protocol support for aggregating multiple columns in one call, so the response
  plausibly echoes more than one back. Retyped to `DataColumn []string`, same treatment `TextColumn`
  already had.
- `aggregate` itself (CARDINALITY: schema zero-or-more, hand-written code had one `*Aggregate`):
  unlike `describe_auth.group` above, this one went the other way — every live capture across four
  distinct `type`/`group_column`/`data_column` combinations, plus the upstream schema-example
  fixture, returns exactly one `<aggregate>`, and no query shape plausibly requests more than one.
  Rather than force a `CardinalityOverride` to keep the pointer, generation was allowed to follow the
  schema's own `zero-or-more` here and produce `Aggregate []Aggregate`; every call site
  (`client/client.go`, `client/client_test.go`, `client/client_misc_test.go`) was updated to index
  `resp.Aggregate[0]` instead of dereferencing a pointer.
- `group/{min,max,mean,sum,c_sum}` (EXTRA): the schema's formal `<group>` pattern declares only
  `value`/`subgroup`/`count`/`c_count`/`stats`/`text`, wrapping per-column stats in `<stats
  column="...">`, but the schema's own baked `<example>` (and
  `testdata/upstream/get_aggregates/001_response.xml`, which reproduces it) shows a flat
  `<min>`/`<max>`/`<mean>`/`<sum>`/`<c_sum>` directly under `<group>` for the single-data_column
  case — the same pattern-vs-example disagreement as `verify_scanner`'s `version`. Kept the
  already-working flat fields and closed the gap with a 5-entry
  `FieldOverrides["get_aggregates:/aggregate/group"]` citing the upstream fixture.

A fifth, previously-unknown defect surfaced only once new live coverage was added (below):
`AggregateText`'s lone attribute is schema-declared as `name`, but a real gvmd 26.36.1 response
(`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_aggregates/004_response.xml`) sends `<text column="name">` — a
second, undeclared attribute the schema never mentions at all. The existing
`FieldSuppressions`/`FieldOverrides` relocation-pairing mechanism turned out to be structurally
incapable of expressing this: it requires the paired override's `Name` to equal the *original*
suppressed wire name, so it can only relocate an element to a different nesting level, never rename
an attribute in place. Absent proof that the schema's `name` declaration is simply wrong (rather
than an alternate or legacy form), the fail-closed choice was additive, not destructive: a plain
`Column string` `FieldOverride` was added at both `aggregate/group/text` and `aggregate/overall/text`
(byte-identical `Reason`, required because `AggregateText` is one structurally-deduplicated type
shared by both paths), leaving the schema-declared `Name` field in place.

`CONFORMANCE.md` after generation: `get_aggregates` moved from `MISSING=5 EXTRA=5 CARDINALITY=2
OMITEMPTY=30` to `EXTRA=7`, every one of the 7 findings suppressed via the overrides above (5 for the
flat stat fields, 2 for the new `text/@column` attribute). Zero unexplained findings remain. New live
captures added to `capture_sequence_test.go` exercising `<sort>`, multi-`<data_column>`, and
`<text_column>` (fixture `004`, the first coverage this family had for any of the three) show
`decode: ok` with no DROPPED/CHANGED/ADDED once the `Column` fix above landed; the pre-existing
`001`–`003` live fixtures and the `upstream/001` schema-example fixture were all re-verified clean.
The upstream fixture's two remaining `DROPPED` findings are gvmd's own `<truncated>...</truncated>`
documentation placeholder inside the schema's baked `<example>` (confirmed at
`vendor/XML/commands/command_get_aggregates.xml:4178,4208` — never observed on any real wire capture
across four live fixtures) — accepted as-is, the same treatment `get_roles` already established for
the identical pattern, rather than modeled for an artifact with zero real-wire evidence.

Getting fixture `004` required the same full-corpus `TestCaptureLive` run every prior family has
needed (the harness has no way to scope capture to one family); all non-`get_aggregates` churn from
that run (~354 files, none of it `get_aggregates`-related) was reverted before this commit, per the
same "full-corpus recapture is the wrong tool for a single-family adoption" reasoning as `nvt`'s and
`auth`'s adoption notes above. The same run also rewrote the existing `001`–`003` `get_aggregates`
fixtures themselves — not just unrelated families — with fresh runtime-dependent response data
(differing aggregate/group counts) and capture timestamps; a review pass caught that this discarded
existing evidence for no adoption-relevant reason, so `001`–`003`'s request/response/meta bytes were
reverted to their originally-committed content and only their derived `*_fidelity.txt` reports were
regenerated against that unchanged evidence. `004` is the only genuinely new fixture this adoption
adds.

`client/client_misc_test.go`'s `TestGetAggregates` was rewritten for the response's new slice
shapes: `resp.Aggregate` is indexed (`resp.Aggregate[0]`) instead of dereferenced, and the renamed
`agg.DataColumn`/`agg.Group` (from the pre-adoption singular/`Groups` names) are asserted directly.
Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build`, `go vet` default + `integration` tags, `golangci-lint run`, `go
test ./...`, `go test -tags integration ./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `license`: `get_license`/`modify_license` (2026-08-18)

The cleanest remaining ranked candidate — `modify_license` was already fully conformant before this
adoption, and `get_license` had exactly one real defect, so this family needed a mechanical pass
rather than a design review.

`get_license`'s only defect: `LicenseAppliance.Sensor` was hand-written as `bool`, but the schema
declares it GMP boolean text, not a Go bool: `strconv.ParseBool` (what `encoding/xml` uses for a
`bool` field) rejects a value like `"7"`, and re-encodes `"0"` as the string `"false"` rather than
preserving it — confirmed by the `upstream/001` fidelity fixture's `CHANGED: .../sensor/#text "0" ->
"false"` finding. The schema declares GMP boolean text; `GMPBool` correctly decodes and re-encodes
the observed `"0"` wire value, unlike Go `bool`. Retyped to the project's existing `GMPBool` (used
everywhere else this exact class of field appears); `TypeOverrides["boolean"]` means the generator
now produces `GMPBool` for this field automatically, with no per-field override needed. The retype
required zero call-site changes anywhere in the repository — an untyped `false` literal converts to
`GMPBool` implicitly, and no code branched on `Sensor`'s value.

The only other defect is the request side's `SCHEMA-DEFECT empty-pattern`: `get_license`'s
`<pattern>` declares no content at all, the same generic upstream-schema shape already carried by
`sync_config`/`empty_trashcan`/`describe_auth`. Allowlisted with the same precedent `Reason`.

Generation also flattened several response fields from pointer to value (`License`,
`LicenseContent.Meta`/`Appliance`/`Keys`/`Signatures` are all schema-required, not optional) and
renamed the two slice fields to the family's singular-element convention (`LicenseKeys.Keys` →
`Key`, `LicenseSignatures.Signatures` → `Signature`) — both mechanical, matching every other
generated family. The one production call site building a `LicenseAppliance`/`License` literal
(`client/client_test.go`'s `GetLicense` mock) and the one test asserting on it
(`client/client_misc_test.go`'s `TestGetLicense`) were updated for the new value types and field
names; no other call site in the repository touches any `License*` field.

`get_license`'s live command is disabled on the pinned gvmd stack (`503 Service unavailable: Command
disabled` — it requires the `libtheia` licensing library, not compiled into the public `gvmd:stable`
image, the same gating situation already on record for the deferred `agent`/`agent_group` surface).
The existing `live/get_license/001` fixture and its `DECODE-ERROR` fidelity report already document
this and needed no changes; `modify_license` already had working live and upstream fixtures. No new
`TestCaptureLive` run was needed for this adoption — every fixture the family needs already existed,
so there was no full-corpus-churn tradeoff to make this time.

`CONFORMANCE.md` after generation: `get_license` moved from `OMITEMPTY=3 TYPE=1 SCHEMA-DEFECT=1` to
`SCHEMA-DEFECT=1` (the one allowlisted empty-pattern defect; the three `OMITEMPTY` findings were
already the universally-suppressed decode-is-unaffected-by-encoding-tags class), and `modify_license`
does not appear in the report at all — fully conformant, unchanged. The `upstream/get_license/001`
fidelity report's `CHANGED "0" -> "false"` finding resolved to `(none)` once `GMPBool` took effect,
confirmed by regenerating fidelity reports and the summary. Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build`, `go
vet` default + `integration` tags, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`, `go test -tags integration
./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `settings`: `get_resource_names`/`get_settings`/`modify_setting` (2026-08-18)

Next ranked candidate per T-9's **Next** field. `modify_setting` was already fully conformant;
`get_resource_names` and `get_settings` each had one real schema-vs-wire mismatch, both the same
class of defect already on record elsewhere in the corpus.

`get_resource_names`'s `resource/@id` is declared `integer` in the schema, but its own baked
`<example>` and every live/upstream fixture (`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_resource_names/
{001,002}`, `testdata/upstream/get_resource_names/001`) show a UUID string
(`5b6b6aef-b320-42ca-899f-3161ee2a4195`) — real wire data the generated `*int` can't decode, the
same class as `get_info`'s `id` field. Added a `FieldTypeOverrides["get_resource_names:/resource"]`
entry retyping it to `string`, modeled directly on the `get_info` precedent.
`get_resource_names_response`'s own `<type>` element is `SCHEMA-DEFECT undefined-type` — it declares
its content as type `type`, a self-reference with no defined alias, the same class already carried
by `get_assets`'s `@distance` and `get_info`'s recursive `<node>`. Allowlisted with the matching
precedent `Reason`.

`get_settings`'s `<setting>` pattern declares only `name`/`comment`/`value`, no `id` attribute, but
all three live fixtures (`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_settings/{001,002,003}_response.xml`)
show every `<setting id="...">` carrying a real UUID, and the schema's own baked `<example>` agrees a
setting has an id — it's only the formal `<pattern>` that omits it. Added a
`FieldOverrides["get_settings:/setting"]` entry adding the `id` attribute back, citing the live
fixtures as wire evidence.

Generation also flattened `get_settings`'s response fields (`Filters`, `Settings`, `SettingCount`)
from pointer to value (all schema-required). The command's own baked `<example>` — reproduced
verbatim by `testdata/upstream/get_settings/001_response.xml` — nests `<setting id="...">` inside
`<settings>`, a second, undeclared wire shape distinct from the flat top-level `<setting>` siblings
every live gvmd 26.36.1 capture actually sends. An initial pass collapsed the response to the flat
shape alone, which decoded that upstream fixture with real data loss (`DROPPED
settings/setting x1 (+5 descendant paths)`) — the schema's own example is protocol evidence, not a
stale legacy form to discard, the same standard already applied to `verify_scanner`'s `version` and
`modify_report_config`'s `report_config_id`. Fixed with a second
`FieldOverrides["get_settings:/settings"]` entry adding `Setting []SettingsSetting` back to the
`Settings` struct (reusing the existing `SettingsSetting` type — no duplicate model), citing the
upstream fixture as evidence that no live capture ever produces this shape. `commands/pagination/
settings.go`'s iterator was restored to prefer the flat top-level `resp.Setting` and fall back to
`resp.Settings.Setting`, matching pre-adoption behavior. `commands/get_settings_test.go`'s new
`TestGetSettingsDualLayout` decodes both layouts directly (`encoding/xml`, no client mocks) and
asserts each populates its own field while leaving the other empty, so future generation cannot
silently drop either again. Call sites updated: `client/client_test.go`,
`client/client_misc_test.go`, `commands/pagination/iterator_factory_test.go` and
`iterator_mocks_test.go` — no other production call site in the repository touches any
`Setting*`/`Resource` field from this family.

`CONFORMANCE.md` after generation: `get_resource_names` moved from `OMITEMPTY=2 TYPE=1
SCHEMA-DEFECT=1` to `TYPE=1 SCHEMA-DEFECT=1` (both allowlisted/override-covered, shown unsuppressed
per the same convention as `get_info`'s identical-pattern findings); `get_settings` moved from
`EXTRA=2 OMITEMPTY=11` to `EXTRA=2` with both findings tagged `[suppressed: ...]` (the pre-existing
`setting/@id` override and the new nested `settings/setting` override); `modify_setting` is fully
conformant. The upstream fixture's `DROPPED settings/setting x1 (+5 descendant paths)` finding
cleared to `(none)`; the live fixtures' previously-failing `DROPPED filters/term` and `TRUNCATED
setting/value 20->18` findings cleared to `(none)` as a natural side effect of the corrected
value/non-omitempty typing, not via suppression. Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build`, `go vet`
default + `integration` tags, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`, `go test -tags integration
./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `version`: `get_version` (2026-08-18)

Next ranked candidate per T-9's **Next** field. A second singleton family (after `aggregate`),

with the matching precedent `Reason`) and response `status`/`status_text`/`version` (all required, no
`omitempty`). Generation produced no field or type deltas at all — the diff against
`commands/get_version.go` is the generated-file header and doc-comment convention only. Both live
fixtures and the upstream/schema-example fixture were already `DROPPED (none)` before and after.
`CONFORMANCE.md` is unchanged: `get_version` was and remains `SCHEMA-DEFECT=1`, shown unsuppressed

#### `feed`: `get_feeds` (2026-08-18)

Next ranked candidate per T-9's **Next** field. A third singleton family. Generation surfaced a
real correctness fix, not just convention churn: the three required boolean fields
(`feed_owner_set`/`feed_roles_set`/`feed_resources_access`) were hand-written as plain `bool`, which
decodes gvmd's lexical `"1"` correctly but re-emits it as `"true"` on encode — the live and upstream
fixtures both showed this as a `CHANGED "1" -> "true"` fidelity finding before adoption. Generation
retypes them to `GMPBool` (required, non-pointer, no `omitempty`, per the `get_tasks.Trash`
precedent), which round-trips losslessly; both fixtures are now clean and `CONFORMANCE.md`'s
`TYPE=3`/`OMITEMPTY=7` findings for `get_feeds` are gone entirely (not just suppressed). Generation
also renamed the repeated response field `Feeds []Feed` to `Feed []Feed`, the same
singular-element-name convention already seen in `settings`/other adoptions; fixed at the three call
sites that referenced the old name (`internal/gmptest/live.go`'s `feedsReady`,
`client/client_misc_test.go`'s `TestGetFeeds`, `client/client_test.go`'s mock response builder). One
`no-pattern` SCHEMA-DEFECT was allowlisted for `/get_feeds_response/feed/sync_not_available/error`
(its `<error>` `<ele>` declares neither `<pattern>` nor `<type>`), same class already carried by
`get_scanners`/`create_report`/`get_reports`/`get_info`; `CONFORMANCE.md` shows it unsuppressed per
the standing convention. `get_feeds` moves from `OMITEMPTY=7 TYPE=3 SCHEMA-DEFECT=1` to
`SCHEMA-DEFECT=1`. Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build`, `go vet` default + `integration` tags,
`golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`, `go test -tags integration ./internal/gmptest/...
-gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `feature`: `get_features` (2026-08-18)

Next ranked candidate per T-9's **Next** field. A fourth singleton family, same `empty-pattern`
request SCHEMA-DEFECT class as `describe_auth`/`get_license`/`get_version`. Generation surfaced two
findings, both expected rather than defects: the hand-written `Enabled bool` re-emitted gvmd's
lexical `"0"` as `"false"` on encode (the same `GMPBool` class already fixed for `feed`'s three
boolean fields); the live fixture confirms this cleared to clean. Second, the live-pinned gvmd's
served schema declares a `compiled_in` boolean attribute on `<feature>` that `vendor/XML/commands`'
older mirror does not — real wire data, present on every one of the 8 features in
`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_features/001_response.xml`, previously silently `DROPPED`.
Generation adds `CompiledIn GMPBool` mechanically from the live schema (no override needed to
generate it); `CONFORMANCE.md` shows it as an unsuppressed `EXTRA` finding since its vendor-mirror
comparison predates the field, the same T-17-style drift already recorded for `get_info`'s
`web_application_vt` and `get_nvts`' `discovery` — recorded as expected drift, not suppressed, per
this project's "record drift rather than hide it" policy. Fixed the `Features`→`Feature` rename at
its two call sites (`client/client_misc_test.go`'s `TestGetFeatures`, `client/client_test.go`'s mock
response builder, the latter also gaining an explicit `CompiledIn` field). `get_features` moves from
`TYPE=1 SCHEMA-DEFECT=1` to `EXTRA=1 SCHEMA-DEFECT=1`. Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build`, `go vet`
default + `integration` tags, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`, `go test -tags integration
./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `system_report`: `get_system_reports` (2026-08-18)

Next ranked candidate per T-9's **Next** field, chosen over the lower-scoring
`run_wizard` because `run_wizard`'s response is a hand-rolled polymorphic union
of ~35 optional pointer fields to nearly every other command's response type —
a shape the schema's own empty `<response>` pattern cannot drive the
deterministic generator to reproduce, and a poor next candidate until it gets
its own adoption approach. `get_system_reports` is a fifth singleton family
and, unusually, generated with zero fail-closed SCHEMA-DEFECT errors — the
first singleton this session not to need an `empty-pattern`/`no-pattern`
override. Generation surfaced three real, fixture-confirmed gaps between the
formal `<pattern>` and the live wire: `report/@start_time` and
`report/@end_time` are undeclared anywhere in the schema, but
`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_system_reports/001_response.xml` sends
both, previously silently `DROPPED`; restored via two `FieldOverrides`
entries. `testdata/upstream/get_system_reports/002_response.xml` proves the
same attributes are also genuinely absent on some reports, so — unlike this
codebase's usual non-pointer `GMPTime` convention — both are pointer-typed
(`*GMPTime`, `omitempty`) rather than plain `GMPTime`: a non-pointer struct's
`omitempty` tag is a no-op in `encoding/xml` (`isEmptyValue` never treats a
struct as empty), so the usual convention would have re-encoded an absent
report's zero `time.Time` as a fabricated
`start_time="0001-01-01T00:00:00Z"`. That fabrication is accepted elsewhere in
this codebase as a documented, non-blocking `ADDED` artifact (see
`FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`'s header), but caught here specifically because the
`upstream/002` fixture exercises the omitted case directly — filed as a
backlog item below since it likely affects other pre-existing optional
`GMPTime` fields project-wide, out of scope for this one family. Two focused
tests in `commands/get_system_reports_test.go`
(`TestGetSystemReportsReportTimestampsPresent`,
`TestGetSystemReportsReportTimestampsAbsent`) pin both shapes: timestamps
present (including the year-1 zero value) round-trip verbatim, and timestamps
absent stay absent rather than being invented on marshal. `report/@duration`
is declared a required integer, but that same live fixture sends `duration=""`
(empty string), which a plain `int` silently decodes as zero and re-emits as
`"0"` — a `CHANGED` finding pre-adoption; retyped to `string` via a
`FieldTypeOverrides` entry, the same schema/wire type-disagreement class as
`get_info`'s `id`. The top-level `<truncated>` element is never declared in
the formal `<pattern>` but appears in the schema's own baked `<example>`
(reproduced verbatim in
`testdata/upstream/get_system_reports/001_response.xml`) — the same
pattern-vs-example disagreement as `get_settings`' nested `setting`, though
weaker evidence since no live capture ever populates it (truncation only
triggers on long-running reports the pinned stack's fixtures never
exercised); kept via a `FieldOverrides` entry on the response envelope,
following the `get_settings` precedent of trusting the schema's own example.
Generation also renamed `SystemReports []SystemReport` to
`SystemReport []SystemReport`, fixed at its call sites in
`client/client_test.go` and `client/client_misc_test.go` (the former also
updated from `Duration: 86400` to `Duration: "86400"`). All three overrides
are recorded and suppressed in `CONFORMANCE.md` except the `TYPE` finding for
`duration`, which is shown unsuppressed per the same convention as
`get_info`'s `id`. Every fixture (live and both upstream) is now fully clean —
no `DROPPED`, `CHANGED`, or `ADDED` findings at all, including `upstream/002`,
which previously would have gained a fabricated-timestamp `ADDED`×2 under the
non-pointer convention. `get_system_reports` moves from `EXTRA=1` to
`EXTRA=3 TYPE=1` (all suppressed except the one `TYPE` finding).

**Backlog:** the non-pointer `GMPTime` + `omitempty` convention used
throughout the rest of this codebase (`report_shared.go`'s `Timestamp`/
`ScanStart`/`ScanEnd`, `get_tickets.go`'s `FixedTime`/`ClosedTime`/
`FixVerifiedTime`, `get_tasks.go`'s `ICalendar`, and every other
schema-optional `GMPTime` element) has the same latent fabrication risk this
review just fixed for `get_system_reports`' two attributes — `omitempty` is a
no-op on a non-pointer struct field, so any of those fields will silently
invent a zero timestamp on re-encode when the schema didn't send one. No
existing fixture happens to exercise the omitted case for those fields today,
which is why it hasn't surfaced as a fidelity finding elsewhere. Fixing it
project-wide means retyping ~15 pre-existing fields to pointers across
unrelated, already-adopted families — out of this family's narrow blast
radius; left as a backlog item for its own pass rather than folded into
`system_report`. Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build`, `go vet` default + `integration` tags,
`golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`, `go test -tags integration
./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `vuln`: `get_vulns` (2026-08-18)

Next ranked candidate per T-9's **Next** field, chosen over `run_wizard` for
the same reason as `system_report` above. A sixth singleton family. Generation
surfaced three real defects and confirmed one systemic pattern already
accepted elsewhere. `GetVulns.Trash *GMPBool` had zero schema or wire support
specific to this command — unlike `get_tasks`/`get_targets`/`get_schedules`/
`get_groups`/`get_roles`/`get_tickets`, all of which have a real, schema-declared
`trash` attribute, `command_get_vulns.xml`'s request `<pattern>` never
declares one, and no fixture (live or upstream) ever sends it — a
hand-authoring copy-paste mistake (vulnerabilities aren't a trashable resource
type), dropped entirely with no override, per CLAUDE.md's fail-closed
principle. The hand-written response's `Truncated string` field looked like a
restorable schema-omission at first (the same shape as `system_report`'s
`truncated` above), but the schema's baked `<example>` actually contains
`<truncate>...</truncate>` — literal ellipsis notation marking "more `<vuln>`
elements omitted from this illustrative example," not real wire data, and
even the wrong element name (`truncate`, not `truncated`) — not restored.
`vuln/type` is the opposite case: the `<vuln>` `<pattern>` list omits `<e>type</e>`,
but the command still declares a full `<ele><name>type</name></ele>` body for
it a few lines down (alts `nvt`/`cve`) — a dangling definition the pattern
forgot to reference, and every fixture sends a real `<type>nvt</type>` —
restored via a `FieldOverrides` entry, the same pattern-omits-a-fully-defined-
element class as `verify_scanner`'s `version`. Generation also reproduced two
already-accepted, pre-existing losses rather than fixing them uniquely here:
`sort`'s schema-declared chardata lands on `VulnSort.Value` because
`command_get_vulns.xml`'s `<pattern>` for `sort` literally declares `text` at
that level, even though every fixture's actual `<field>name<order>...</order>
</field>` nests the real field-name text one level deeper, inside `<field>` —
the identical schema-says-wrapper/wire-says-child disagreement already shipped
unfixed in `get_tasks`, `get_targets`, `get_reports`, `get_roles`, `get_groups`,
and `get_tags`, all of which show the same `DROPPED sort/field/#text` in their
own fidelity reports (T-18a later closed this across all of them, `get_vulns`
included — see T-18a below); and `vuln_count` has the same class of gap as
`role_count`/`group_count`/`tag_count` above — its `<pattern>` declares only
`<filtered>`/`<page>`, but the live fixtures and the schema's own example both
send a leading integer total ahead of those two children, with nowhere to
decode into. Per this project's established generate-only-what-the-
schema-declares principle (see `role_count` above), both stay as
schema-driven, unoverridden `DROPPED /.../#text` fidelity artifacts, accepted
and documented rather than patched — fixing the `sort` placement only for
`get_vulns` would leave it inconsistent with every sibling family sharing the
identical shape, so it belongs in one coordinated pass across all of them
rather than a one-off override here. Generation renamed the hand-written plural-prefixed types
(`VulnsFilters`, `VulnsSort`, `VulnsKeyword`, etc.) to the family-local
singular prefix (`VulnFilters`, `VulnSort`, `VulnKeyword`, ...) and
`Vuln.QoD` to `Vuln.QOD`; fixed at its only call site,
`internal/gmptest/cases_misc.go`, which also dropped its two `Trash`-using
request cases and their now-orphaned golden XML. `get_vulns` moves from
`MISSING=1 EXTRA=3 OMITEMPTY=8` to a fully-suppressed `EXTRA=1`. Full suite
(`gofmt -l`, `go build`, `go vet` default + `integration` tags, `golangci-lint
run`, `go test ./...`, `go test -tags integration ./internal/gmptest/...
-gmp.insecure`) is green.

#### `result`: `get_results` (2026-08-18)

The last of T-9's naturally family-shaped candidates — a singleton, read-only
command previously hand-written at 464 lines, scoring `MISSING=1 EXTRA=4
CARDINALITY=2 OMITEMPTY=33 SCHEMA-DEFECT=4`. Most of that score resolved
through mechanical regeneration alone: the shared `severities`/`solution`
elements (already correctly generated for `get_nvts`) filled in
`nvt/severities/severity` and the previously entirely-unmodelled `nvt/solution`;
`nvt/cvss_base` needed a `FieldTypeOverrides` entry retyping it to `float64` —
the served schema declares it `integer`, but every fixture sends a real
decimal (`"5.0"`), the identical disagreement already fixed for
`create_report`/`get_reports`. Four `duplicate-ele` `DefectAllowlist` entries
were needed for the upstream `active`-redefinition defect reachable through
`{delta,notes,overrides}/{note,override}/active`, matching the exact class
already allowlisted for `get_notes`/`get_overrides`/`get_reports`.

T-21's write-up (above) predicted the one non-mechanical shape correctly
ahead of generation: `get_results` is itself where `result` gets expanded, so
`note.result`/`override.result`/`delta.result` — one layer further down —
hit the shared-element guard while `result` is still expanding and legitimately
collapse to a bare `string` leaf rather than a full recursive struct. This is
not a T-21 regression (no `SCHEMA-DEFECT` is recorded for it, matching
`certificate_info`'s first-reference case), and the regenerated
`CONFORMANCE.md` confirms it: the old hand-written 8-field `Result` expansion
at that path is what was actually non-conformant, and the new leaf resolves
it cleanly with no EXTRA finding. Generation surfaced one genuinely new
finding not predicted during scoping: `result/oci_image` is `EXTRA` because
`vendor/XML/commands/command_get_results.xml` never declares it, even though
the live-served schema and every fixture do — the same
vendored-docs-lag-live-schema gap already accepted for `get_reports`'
`result/oci_image` (identical wording), left unpatched for the same reason.
`result_count`'s leading integer total and `sort`'s schema-says-wrapper/
wire-says-child chardata are the same already-accepted cross-family patterns
documented for `vuln`/`role`/`group`/`tag` above — not unique to this family,
not patched here. T-18a later closed the `sort` half across every evidenced
family including `get_results` (see T-18a below); `result_count`'s leading
total remains an accepted loss.

Generation renamed the hand-written plural-prefixed types
(`ResultsFilters`, `ResultsSort`, `ResultsMeta`, `ResultsCount`, ...) to the
family-local singular convention (`ResultFilters`, `ResultSort`, `Results`
for the `<results max start>` meta wrapper, `ResultCount`), and split the
hand-written single `Results []Result` list field from its meta wrapper into
`Result []Result` (the repeated element) and `Results Results` (the wrapper),
mirroring `Vuln []Vuln`/`Vulns Vulns`. Fixed at every call site this rippled
