into: `commands/pagination/results.go`, `commands/pagination/iterator_mocks_test.go`,
`commands/pagination/iterator_factory_test.go`, `client/client_test.go` (two
mock-response blocks), and `examples/basic_usage/main.go`. `get_results`
moves from `MISSING=1 EXTRA=4 CARDINALITY=2 OMITEMPTY=33 SCHEMA-DEFECT=4` to
`EXTRA=1 SCHEMA-DEFECT=4` (the four allowlisted `duplicate-ele` sites, plus
the one accepted `oci_image` gap). Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build`,
`go vet` default + `integration` tags, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`,
`go test -tags integration ./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.pass=... -gmp.insecure`)
is green.

**Addendum (2026-08-19): `nvt/solution` was never modelled, not "filled in."**
Reviewer follow-up on the entry above caught three defects before commit.
First, and most serious: the claim that `solution` was already filled in
was false — `internal/codegen/testdata/schema/get_results.json`'s
`result/nvt` node genuinely has no `solution` child, so `ResultNVT` had no
`Solution` field at all, and all 4 live fixtures' `<solution type=''>`
remained silently dropped in every fidelity report. Fixed with a new
mechanism, `internal/codegen/schema_patches.go`'s `SchemaPatches` table,
which splices an evidence-backed `*schema.Node` into the parsed tree before
`Generate` runs, so the ordinary `collect`/`walk`/`emitStruct` pipeline
produces a real family-local `ResultSolution` struct — same handling as any
organically-declared field, no parallel emission path. The spliced shape
(optional chardata element, optional `type`/`method` attributes) is not
invented: it's exactly what `get_nvts`' own live schema snapshot already
declares for the same `nvt/solution` reuse, already proven correct as
`commands.NVTSolution`. `ApplySchemaPatches` fails closed the moment gvmd's
served schema starts declaring `solution` itself, so this patch cannot
silently drift once upstream catches up.

Second, the `cvss_base` `FieldTypeOverride`'s reason overclaimed "every
fixture sends decimal": only upstream fixture 001 does (`"5.0"`); upstream
002 sends a whole number (`"9"`) and all 4 live fixtures send `"0.0"`.
Reworded to cite each fixture's actual value — one genuine decimal is
sufficient to rule out `int`, but the table shouldn't claim consensus that
isn't there.

Third, `get_results` had no command-level XML regression test; acceptance
rested solely on generated goldens and aggregate fidelity reports. Added
`commands/get_results_test.go` with 6 focused round-trip tests: repeated
`nvt/severities/severity`, `cvss_base`'s decimal-not-int round trip, the
live `nvt/solution` present-but-empty shape, `original_severity` present and
absent, and T-21's `note.result`/`override.result` leaf-collapse.

Regenerating surfaced one outcome the fix didn't anticipate: the
`nvt/solution` dropped-path in `internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_results/*_fidelity.txt`
didn't just disappear — it was replaced by a narrower
`nvt/solution/@type` drop. All 4 live fixtures send `<solution type=''>`:
present but explicitly empty, another instance of this document's
omit-vs-explicit-empty distinction, this time on the response-decode side.
`Type string \`xml:"type,attr,omitempty"\`` can't tell "explicit empty" from
"absent" on remarshal, so `omitempty` drops it either way. This is the same
class of accepted, unfixed lossy round trip as the `OMITEMPTY`-suppressed
fields documented elsewhere in this review (e.g. T-9's `create_report`/
`get_reports` entries), left unpatched for the same reason: fixing it would
mean dropping `omitempty` from every optional attribute in this family,
trading one cosmetic fidelity gap for universally noisier request/response
diffs. Net fidelity effect is still a strict improvement: live dropped-path
counts fall from 4 to 3 per fixture (the old full `solution` subtree drop
plus a `severities/severity` drop both clear; the narrower `@type` drop is
the only new one). `CONFORMANCE.md`'s `get_results` section moves from
`EXTRA=1 SCHEMA-DEFECT=4` to `EXTRA=2 SCHEMA-DEFECT=4` — the new `solution`
`EXTRA` is the same accepted "vendored docs lag the live schema" class
already carried for `result/oci_image` in the same section, not a
regression. Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build`, `go vet` default +
`integration` tags, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`) is green.

#### Backlog closeout: non-pointer `GMPTime` + `omitempty` fabrication (2026-08-19)

Closes the backlog item filed during `system_report`'s adoption above: `pointerScalars`
(`internal/codegen/generate.go`) — the table of Go scalar types whose zero value is a legal wire value,
so an optional field of that type must generate as a pointer for "absent" and "zero" to stay
distinguishable — listed `int`, `float64`, `GMPBool`, `GMPAtoiBool` but not `GMPTime`, the same class of
gap already fixed once for `GMPAtoiBool` during `config`'s adoption. Added `GMPTime`, then regenerated:
15 pre-existing optional `GMPTime` fields across six already-adopted families flip from plain `GMPTime`
to `*GMPTime` with `omitempty` retained — `get_nvts.go`'s `CreationTime`/`ModificationTime`,
`get_tags.go`'s `CreationTime`/`ModificationTime`, `get_tasks.go`'s `CreationTime`/`ModificationTime`/
`GetTasksSchedule.ICalendar`, `get_tickets.go`'s `FixedTime`/`ClosedTime`/`FixVerifiedTime`,
`report_shared.go`'s `Timestamp`/`ScanStart`/`ScanEnd`, and `get_system_reports.go`'s own request-side
`StartTime`/`EndTime` (distinct from `SystemReportReport.StartTime`/`EndTime` on the response, already
fixed via a `FieldOverrides` entry during that family's adoption). `get_agents.go`'s `CreationTime`/
`ModificationTime`/`LastUpdate` have the identical bug but are hand-written, not codegen-owned (the
`agent` family adoption remains blocked — `ENABLE_AGENTS` isn't compiled into the pinned `gvmd:stable`
image); hand-edited to pointers directly, which also silently fixed `delete_agents.go`/`modify_agents.go`
since both reuse the same `Agent` struct.

The fabrication was not purely latent. `internal/gmptest/testdata/requests/get_system_reports/*.xml` and
`requests/modify_agents/*.xml` — derived request goldens built from Go values with no field set — were
sending fabricated `start_time="0001-01-01T00:00:00Z"`/`end_time`/`creation_time`/`modification_time`/
`last_update` on every request that never set those fields; all now correctly omit them. The accepted
`ADDED` fidelity findings this bug produced elsewhere (`FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`'s documented "not loss"
category) also clear entirely rather than staying suppressed: `get_tasks` (`icalendar` ×5 live,
`creation_time`/`modification_time` upstream), `get_tickets` (`closed_time`/`fix_verified_time`/
`fixed_time` live and upstream), `get_nvts` (`creation_time`/`modification_time` upstream), and
`get_reports` (`timestamp`/`scan_start`/`scan_end` upstream) — each fixture's `added` count drops by
exactly the removed fields, nothing else moves. `internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_system_reports/
001_request.xml` — a captured, non-regenerable fixture — still shows the historical fabricated
attributes verbatim: `-update` never rewrites a captured `NNN_request.xml`/`NNN_response.xml`
(`internal/gmptest/update.go`'s `allowedUpdatePath` treats those as evidence, not derived goldens), and
decoding a real (if historically fabricated) wire value into the now-pointer field and re-marshaling it
faithfully reproduces it — correct round-trip behavior, not a sign the fix is incomplete. `CONFORMANCE.md`
only changes for `get_agents.go`'s three fields' printed type string (`GMPTime`→`*GMPTime` in the already
non-blocking `EXTRA` findings for `delete_agents`/`modify_agents`); structural OMITEMPTY-class conformance
findings are unaffected since that check doesn't distinguish pointer from non-pointer omitempty.

Call sites: ~24 `commands.GMPTime{...}` struct-literal sites in `client/client_test.go` building
`Tag`/`Task`/`GetNVTsNVT`-shaped mocks needed `&`-prefixing; a handful of superficially similar
`ICalendar:` string-literal sites (`client/client_test.go`, `client/client_schedule_test.go`,
`internal/gmptest/capture_sequence_test.go`) turned out to belong to an unrelated schedule-creation
request type, not `GetTasksSchedule.ICalendar` — confirmed by `go vet -tags integration` staying clean
with no changes there. Added `TestGetSystemReportsRequestTimestampsAbsent` in
`commands/get_system_reports_test.go`, mirroring the existing response-side
`TestGetSystemReportsReportTimestampsAbsent`, pinning that an empty `GetSystemReports{Name: "load"}`
marshals without fabricating `start_time`/`end_time`. Also added `TestFieldOfGMPTimePointerPolicy` in
`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`, a focused `fieldOf`-level test pinning the generic
`pointerScalars["GMPTime"]` policy directly (matching `TestFieldOfInChoiceCardinality`/
`TestFieldOfRequiredOverride`'s pattern) rather than relying only on family snapshots and the one
command-level marshal test above: optional `iso_time` generates `*GMPTime` with `omitempty`, required
`iso_time` stays plain `GMPTime` with no `omitempty`, and an optional field with an applicable
`PointerOverride` comes out the same way rather than double-applying the pointer. Full suite (`gofmt -l`, `go build` default +
`integration` tags, `go vet` default + `integration` tags, `golangci-lint run`, `go test ./...`,
`go run ./internal/cmd/gengmp -check`) is green. The live-server integration suite
(`go test -tags integration ./internal/gmptest/... -gmp.insecure`) could not be run this pass — the
pinned `gvmd` container is unhealthy in this environment, unrelated to this change.

#### `help` closeout: closed as permanently hand-written (2026-08-19)

`help` was on record above as excluded from the CRUD-family ranking because `HelpResponse` is a flat
chardata struct never routed through the generator — this entry closes that with firsthand generation
evidence rather than inference.

Added `help` to `manifest.json` as `review_only`, captured its live Stage-A snapshot
(`TestSnapshotLive -update`, no feed sync needed — `dialLive` only authenticates), and ran
`TestGenerateAllIsPerFamily`. `Generate` failed closed with 12 `recursion-cut` defects, all under
`help_response/schema/protocol/command/{pattern,response}`: `<help format="XML">`'s response recursively
re-embeds the RNC grammar's own shared building blocks (`g` = group, `o` = optional, `any` = wildcard) to
describe the protocol's pattern grammar, and each one reaches itself. `DefectAllowlist` exists for
one-off, genuinely-benign shared-shape reuse (e.g. `tag`'s `permissions` sub-list) — allowlisting all 12
sites here would force through a lossy Go type for a self-describing grammar that nothing in this
codebase consumes structurally; every caller wants the raw payload, which `HelpResponse.Text` provides
as an opaque blob rather than a typed model.

That opaque blob must actually be format-independent to be worth calling a closeout: the XML format's
response nests a `<schema><protocol>...</protocol></schema>` subtree
(`internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/help/002_response.xml`), and a first pass at `HelpResponse.Text`
used `xml:",chardata"`, which captures only direct character data and silently drops nested elements —
confirmed by that fixture's own `002_fidelity.txt` recording `/help_response/schema` and 28 descendant
paths as `DROPPED`. Fixed by switching `Text` to `xml:",innerxml"`, which captures the element's raw
undecoded inner XML regardless of whether it contains nested elements; the plain-text format has no
nested elements, so this is a no-op there (`commands/help_test.go`'s
`TestHelpResponseTextRoundTripsPlainText` pins that), while the XML format's `<schema>` subtree now
round-trips verbatim (`TestHelpResponseTextRoundTripsXMLSchema`).

Reverted the manifest/snapshot experiment (`help` was never adopted or left `review_only`) and instead
added `commands/help_test.go`, pinning: an unset `Help{}` marshals with neither `format` nor `type`
present (both are declared without a schema `<required>` flag, despite the summary prose calling
`format` "Required"); a set `Help{Format, Type}` round-trips both attributes (note: this doesn't cover
the live server's explicit-empty `type=""` introspection call — `Type`'s `omitempty` tag can't represent
that, and no caller in this codebase needs to construct it); and `HelpResponse.Text` preserves both the
plain-text and XML-schema formats' payloads verbatim via innerxml. `help` is now a closed decision, not
an open backlog item.

#### `run_wizard` closeout: closed as permanently hand-written (2026-08-19)

`run_wizard` was T-9's last open item, deferred throughout the cohort because `RunWizardResponse.Response`
(`WizardResponse`) is a hand-rolled union of ~35 typed pointer fields — one per command response it might
embed — rather than a schema-derived shape. Closing it here, same standard of evidence as `help`.

**Why it can't be generated.** `CONFORMANCE.md` records exactly one finding for this command:
`SCHEMA-DEFECT response /run_wizard_response/response` — an `empty-pattern` node, the schema's way of
saying "content varies, no fixed model." That alone is normally a mild, well-precedented defect
(`DefectAllowlist` already carries 15+ `empty-pattern` entries, e.g. `create_group`'s `specials/full`),
so unlike `help`'s 12-site `recursion-cut` this looked adoptable at first pass. Two things rule it out:

1. **There is no schema content to drive a deterministic enumeration.** The vendor XML's own worked
   example for this command embeds exactly one response shape (`start_task_response`,
   `vendor/XML/commands/command_run_wizard.xml:3667`) and declares nothing else — the ~35-field union in
   today's hand-written code was assembled from outside knowledge of gvmd's wizard scripts, not from
   anything the schema states. A generator can't reproduce an enumeration the schema never declares.
2. **Generating the empty-pattern node through the normal defaults would regress, not adopt.** Confirmed
   against precedent (`GroupSpecials.Full string`, `TagResourcesResource.Permissions string`): the
   generator's default Go shape for an `empty-pattern` node is a plain `string` on a named-element tag,
   which silently drops nested children — the exact bug class `help` was just fixed for. `<response>`
   always carries a nested child (the embedded command response), so allowlisting this defect and
   accepting the default would trade today's typed-decode-plus-lossless-fallback model for something
   strictly worse.

**Auditing the existing hand-written model.** `WizardResponse` pairs its 35 typed fields with an
`InnerXML string \`xml:",innerxml"\`` catch-all, previously commented "for any unknown or extra content."
That claim was untested, so — same lesson as `help`'s closeout — it was checked directly rather than
taken on faith. A decode-then-re-encode probe confirmed `InnerXML` actually captures the *entire* raw
child content, not just what the typed fields miss, so a populated `StartTaskResponse` field and
`InnerXML` both re-emit the same `<start_task_response>` element on marshal. This is not a fresh
discovery: `internal/gmptest/fidelity_test.go`'s `TestCompareDuplicated` already models exactly this
shape, names `RunWizardResponse` as "the one place this happens for real," and classifies duplication as
not loss — a deliberate, already-accepted trade-off in this codebase's fidelity harness, not a bug to
fix. It only matters if this type is ever re-marshaled after being decoded, which no production call site
does (`client.go`'s `RunWizard` only decodes; nothing here is ever sent back over the wire).

What was actually unverified was the fallback's value proposition — that `InnerXML` preserves a
wizard-embedded response type the 35-field enumeration doesn't cover. Added
`TestWizardResponseUnknownTypePreservedViaInnerXML` in `commands/run_wizard_test.go`, using
`CreateFilterResponse` (a real, undisputed gap in the enumeration) to prove an unenumerated embedded
response survives verbatim, alongside `TestWizardResponseKnownTypeDecodesTyped` pinning the typed-field
path for a shape the union does cover. Tightened `InnerXML`'s comment to state both guarantees plainly:
it always preserves everything, including embedded response types outside the hand-enumerated set, at
the cost of duplicating already-typed content if re-marshaled.

`run_wizard` is now a closed decision. T-9 has no remaining **Next** item — every one of gvmd's 155
commands is either schema-generated or closed as permanently hand-written with a verified contract.

#### The source of truth

`src/schema_formats/XML/GMP.xml.in` — 1,259,333 bytes, ~41.9k lines. A **bespoke XML DSL**, not
XSD/RELAX-NG. Root `<protocol>` containing 155 `<command>`, 30 `<type>`, 16 `<element>`,
92 `<change>`, 4 `<deprecation>`. *(All counts independently verified against `main`.)*

There is **no `.rnc` file in the repo** — `gmp.rnc` and the published HTML reference are *build
artifacts* produced by `xsltproc` over this file, and they are strictly lossy.

**Consume `GMP.xml.in` directly. Do not go via RNC/trang/XSD.** The RNC step discards the `<summary>`
docs, the `<example>` blocks, and the `required` provenance, and it injects defects that aren't in
the source (below).

`.in` is a CMake template: it carries `@GMP_VERSION@` and feature-flag pairs
(`@IF_ENABLE_AGENTS@`…`@ENDIF_ENABLE_AGENTS@`, plus `CONTAINER_SCANNING`,
`WEB_APPLICATION_SCANNING`, `JWT_AUTH`) that must be substituted before use.

> **Better option:** pull the schema from a live server via `<help format="XML" type=""/>`. gvmd serves
> the already-configured `GMP.xml`, so this sidesteps the CMake substitution entirely *and* gives you
> the schema for the exact server version you're talking to. It also avoids vendoring (see licensing).

#### The DSL maps cleanly onto Go

| tag | meaning | Go |
|---|---|---|
| `<e>x</e>` | required child | field |
| `<o><e>x</e></o>` | optional | pointer + `omitempty` |
| `<any><e>x</e></any>` | zero-or-more | slice |
| `<attrib>` | attribute | `,attr` (+ `omitempty` unless `<required>1</required>`) |
| `<alts><alt>` | enum | const block |
| `<g>` / `<or>` | interleave / choice | struct / variant |

Cardinality is exactly {1, 0..1, 0..n} — there is no `+` and no `minOccurs/maxOccurs`. Attrs-vs-children
is unambiguous. Pointer-vs-value and `omitempty` are directly derivable, which is precisely what fixes
T-5 mechanically.

Encouraging sanity check: the schema's `create_tag` maps 1:1 onto the existing hand-written
`commands/create_tag.go`, down to `Resources *CreateTagResources` and
`Filter string \`xml:"filter,attr,omitempty"\``. **The hand-written code is essentially what a generator
would emit** — which is the best possible signal that this is worth doing.

Bonus: **206 worked request/response examples covering 154 of 155 commands**, plus 4,271 `<summary>`
strings. The examples are free golden-test fixtures for T-8 (subject to licensing, below).

#### Known defects — the exception list

All verified against `main` today:

1. **~85 sites write `<pattern><type>text</type></pattern>` instead of `<t>text</t>`.** The XSL has no
   `type` branch, so the generated RNC emits a literal `ERROR`. Harmless if you consume the XML directly
   and treat `<type>` as a synonym for `<t>` — but fatal if you route through RNC.
2. **68 dangling `<e>` references** with no `<ele>` definition, concentrated in the newest commands:
   `get_scan_report` (18), `get_agent_groups` (15), `get_report_tls_certificates` (14),
   `get_integration_configs` (13).
3. **`get_filters` is structurally broken** — references a nonexistent `filters2` element and defines
   `<ele>filters</ele>` twice, producing invalid RELAX NG. Reported as
   [gvmd#541](https://github.com/greenbone/gvmd/issues/541) in 2019, closed 2021 *without the fix landing*.
4. **52 completely empty `<pattern/>`** — e.g. `task/config/permissions`. Pure hand-waving.
5. **8 undefined type names** needing an alias table: `UUID` (31×, wrong case), `real` (9×), `number` (3×).
6. **`boolean` is `text`, not a boolean.** Schema note: *"Zero is false, anything else is true. As a
   result, the empty string is considered true"* — and `boolean_atoi` is the **opposite**. Mapping
   either to Go `bool` needs a custom marshaler; a naive `bool` is wrong. Directly relevant to T-5.
7. **Upstream CMake bug:** `@ENDIF_ENABLE_CREDENTIAL_STORES@` appears twice with **no matching
   `@IF_ENABLE_CREDENTIAL_STORES@`** *(independently confirmed: 2 closers, 0 openers)*. With credential
   stores disabled, the generated XML is malformed.
8. **No shared type reuse.** The 16 top-level `<element>` definitions are never referenced by any
   command; everything is copy-pasted inline. 2,989 `<ele>` definitions but only 537 distinct names
   (`name` redefined 321×, `comment` 107×). Naive generation yields ~3,300 near-duplicate Go types —
   **structural hashing + dedup is where most of the generator's real work lives.**
9. **Requests are over-constrained.** `create_task` marks `config`/`target`/`scanner` required with no
   `<or>`, though the agent/OCI variants clearly replace `target`. Trust response-side optionality more
   than request-side.

#### Version drift — this is already biting

The schema tracks gvmd's branch, so output is version-pinned. Concrete: **`hosts_ordering` appears 8× in
`v23.9.0` and zero times in `main`** *(verified)* — yet `commands/create_task.go:15` has
`HostsOrdering`. **You are already targeting an older GMP than upstream `main`, without recording which
one.** Generate per-tag and diff; the 92 `<change>` and 4 `<deprecation>` entries are a machine-readable
migration log, and this is what finally lets you publish the compatibility matrix T-12 wants.

#### Prior art: none

- **python-gvm is fully hand-maintained** — no reference to `schema_formats` anywhere.
- **No Go project generates from this schema**, including the `filewalkwithme/go-gmp` this project
  is based on.
- The only recorded attempt (gvmd#541, Java, via RNC) failed on exactly the defects above.

You'd be first. That's an opportunity, but it also means no one has de-risked this for you — which is
why the conformance-report step below matters.

#### Recommended approach

1. **Do this after T-8/T-10.** Codegen without a fidelity oracle just changes *who* wrote the wrong tags.
2. **First deliverable is a conformance report, not generated code** — diff the schema-derived model
   against the existing 123 structs. Finds real bugs immediately, at a fraction of the cost, and proves
   the generator understands the DSL before anything depends on it.
3. Keep the generator out of the published module (`internal/cmd/gen` or the empty `scripts/`).
4. Only then migrate packages to generated output, one at a time, with golden tests as the gate.

#### Optional, time-boxed: `goxslt` as a normalization step

Worth a spike, not a commitment. The idea is to use [`speedata/goxslt`](https://github.com/speedata/goxslt)
*only* to normalize the configured `GMP.xml` into a compact IR, keeping graph analysis, structural
hashing, the exception policy, and Go emission in ordinary Go. Explicitly **not** for routing through
RNC/XSD (rejected above) and **not** for emitting Go directly from a stylesheet.

Facts below are **pinned to the revision inspected on 2026-08-07: `1cc6c0876f4a`, authored
2026-07-21** (repo created 2026-02-20, not archived). Re-check before acting on them; the volatile rows
are marked. Star/fork counts are deliberately omitted — they are not an engineering criterion.

| | |
|---|---|
| License | BSD-3-Clause — permissive, compatible with this MIT project |
| Implementation | Pure Go, no cgo/libxslt (a genuine advantage over `xsltproc`) |
| XSLT version | Targets 3.0 |
| Conformance *(volatile)* | ~5,000 of ~14,700 W3C XSLT 3.0 suite tests pass (~34%) — the suite revision this was measured against is unrecorded upstream and should be pinned by the spike |
| Maturity *(volatile)* | Self-described **beta**; no tagged releases, so consumption means a pseudo-version |
| Gaps | No streaming, no schema-aware processing, no packages; `xsl:iterate`/`merge`/`evaluate` absent |

If the spike proceeds, record in its write-up: the exact module pseudo-version used, the W3C suite
revision, **which XSLT features the prototype stylesheet actually depends on** (the only conformance
figure that matters is coverage of *those*), whether output is deterministic across runs, and the
quality of diagnostics on malformed input — `GMP.xml.in` is malformed in eight known ways (above), so
error reporting is a first-class requirement, not a nicety.

**Assessment: the XSLT layer has to earn its place, and currently the burden is high.** The strongest
argument for XSLT would be reusing gvmd's *existing* stylesheets — but those target RNC/HTML output that
T-9 explicitly rejects, so any stylesheet here would be newly written. At that point the comparison is
"a new stylesheet plus a 34%-conformance beta processor" versus "`encoding/xml` in the standard
library," and the standard library wins on every axis that matters for a build-time tool. Note also that
gvmd's own stylesheets are XSLT 1.0 (`xsltproc`/libxslt); a 3.0-targeting beta running 1.0 in
backwards-compatible mode is an additional untested dimension.

So: run it as an explicitly time-boxed prototype, A/B against direct `encoding/xml` parsing on the
known-problematic commands (`get_filters`, `get_scan_report`, the `<type>`-for-`<t>` sites), and **abandon
the XSLT layer the moment exceptions start accumulating in the stylesheet** — that accumulation is the
signal that the DSL's irregularities are better handled in Go, where they can be tested.

#### Licensing — resolve before writing code

**`GMP.xml.in` is `SPDX-License-Identifier: AGPL-3.0-or-later`, Copyright (C) 2010-2022 Greenbone AG**
*(verified in the file header)*. This project is **MIT**. Not legal advice, but:

- **Do not vendor the schema.** That puts an AGPL file inside an MIT distribution. Fetch it from a
  pinned tag at generate-time, or from a live server via `<help format="XML"/>`.
- **Generated struct/field names are the defensible part** — names of wire-protocol elements are
  interface facts, not creative expression.
- **The `<summary>`/`<description>` prose is not.** Emitting those 4,271 strings as Go doc comments —
  the single biggest appeal of generating from this schema — copies AGPL prose into MIT source. **This
  is the actual risk, and it is entirely avoidable.**
- **Low-risk default:** emit names + types only, with a pointer comment
  (`// See GMP <version> documentation for <command>.`). If you want the prose, paraphrase it or get the
  decision reviewed. The 206 examples raise the same question if committed as fixtures.

---

### T-15 · The generator does not fail closed on schema defects
**Status:** done (93ce663) · **Files:** `internal/codegen/generate.go`, `internal/schema/parse.go`

`internal/schema` parses eight defect classes into `Command.Defects`, the snapshot format carries them,
`CONFORMANCE.md` counts them — and `internal/codegen` never reads the field. Verified: outside
`internal/schema`, `.Defects` appears only inside comments in `validate_test.go`, never in generator
code. So an `empty-pattern`, `no-pattern`, `recursion-cut` or `dangling-e` node generates a silently
truncated struct instead of refusing to generate at all.

Nothing has gone wrong yet only because the family-selection method ranks candidates by SCHEMA-DEFECT
count and every family adopted so far was near-clean. That is a human reading a report standing in for
a machine guard, and it is exactly the safeguard the remaining families need: 44 `empty-pattern` sites
across 23 commands, 17 `no-pattern` across 4, 6 `recursion-cut` in one, plus the 68 dangling `<e>`
references upstream `main` concentrates in the newest commands (`get_scan_report`, `get_agent_groups`,
`get_report_tls_certificates`, `get_integration_configs`). The low-defect supply is exhausted; the next
families are the ones this catches.

This also contradicts the repository's own stated generator priority, "fail-closed handling of ambiguous
or unsupported schema shapes" (`AGENTS.md`).

Acceptance criteria:

- `Generate` rejects a command whose snapshot carries any defect not on an explicit allowlist;
- the allowlist is keyed `{command, class, path}` with a `Reason` citing evidence, in the same style and
  the same file as `TypeOverrides`/`FieldOverrides`/`CommandWarnings`;
- a stale allowlist entry — one whose defect no longer appears — fails generation, matching
  `checkFieldOverridesConsumed`/`checkCommandWarningsConsumed`; that check runs per family, so it
  structurally cannot see an entry naming a command in no family, and
  `TestDefectAllowlistHasNoOrphans` covers the corpus-wide case instead. The gap matters more here than
  for the other tables: an orphan is a standing pre-approval for a defect nothing generates through, so
  a command leaving the manifest or renamed upstream would keep its exemption silently;
- **every** defect record currently carried by an adopted-family snapshot is migrated into the
  allowlist individually, each with its own reason, rather than grandfathered by a blanket exemption or
  by a class-wide waiver — adopting a defect stays a decision on the record;
- generated output for the adopted families is byte-identical afterwards — this adds a gate, not a
  codegen change.

**What the adopted families actually carry**, derived from the committed snapshots rather than from this
document's per-family narrative (19 defect *records*, 4 *classes*, 12 of 42 *commands* — three counts
that differ and should not be used interchangeably):

| class | records | commands |
|---|---|---|
| `empty-pattern` | 11 | `create_group`, `get_alerts` (×2), `get_credentials` (×2), `get_filters`, `get_permissions` (×2), `get_port_lists`, `get_schedules`, `get_tags` |
| `duplicate-ele` | 6 | `get_notes` (×3), `get_overrides` (×3) |
| `undefined-type` | 1 | `get_assets` — `number` at `.../route/host/@distance`, aliased to `integer` |
| `unsupported-r` | 1 | `create_port_list` — `<r>` inside a pattern at the command root |

Two of those classes appear nowhere in the family narratives above. The `undefined-type` alias is
load-bearing — `internal/schema`'s `typeAliases` silently maps `number` onto `integer`, and the count
exists precisely to say how often the table is carrying the schema — and `unsupported-r` sits at
`create_port_list`'s own root. Neither was noticed during that family's review. That is the argument for
this entry in miniature: the narrative is not a reliable index of what was accepted, so the allowlist has
to be built from the snapshots, and the migration is the moment each of the 19 gets looked at once.

`get_notes`/`get_overrides` moved from ×2 to ×3 each after T-21 (below) fixed `expandShared`'s recursion
guard: the shared `note`/`override` elements' pre-existing `active`-redefinition defect became reachable
at each element's newly-resolved second reference too.

### T-16 · Provenance and pinning
**Status:** T-16a done for its content-addressed schema-provenance scope (runtime-provenance half
partial) · T-16b done · T-16c done (image pinning, digest wiring, and a working refresh procedure land;
re-capturing the 167 pre-existing live fixtures against the pinned stack is still pending, blocked on
rotating the live gvmd's admin credential, but no longer gates the corpus — see below) ·
**Split:** none of the three block T-9 family adoption any longer · **Files:**
`internal/codegen/snapshot.go`, `internal/codegen/snapshot_live_test.go`, `internal/codegen/vendor_parity_test.go`,
`internal/schema/parse.go`, `internal/dockerstack/`, `internal/gmptest/`, `internal/imageref/`,
`hack/docker-compose.yml`, `hack/pin-images.sh`, `internal/cmd/pinimages/`

Three independently reviewable changes, originally separated because only the first was a T-9 blocker
and bundling them would have made the blocker unmeasurable. T-16a's blocking scope is now done (see
below), so none of the three still gate family adoption:

- **T-16a — the provenance model, and failing closed on mixed provenance.** Done for its
  content-addressed schema-provenance scope, which is what unblocked further families; runtime
  provenance remains partial, see below.
- **T-16b — migrating Stage A to live schema extraction.** Done. `TestSnapshotLive` is now the *sole*
  route for writing `testdata/schema/*.json`; `TestSnapshot`'s file-based path proves the fallback
  extraction still works, no longer diffs against the committed snapshot, and — since a second-pass
  review found its `-update` flag could still overwrite an `ExtractionServed` snapshot with
  `ExtractionFile` provenance — no longer supports `-update` at all (see the T-16b entry below for why
  that comparison moved to its own opt-in test, `TestVendorParity`).
- **T-16c — pinning the integration stack.** Affects the runtime oracle, not Stage A. Image pinning,
  `Meta.ImageDigest` wiring, and the pin-refresh procedure are done; re-capturing the 167 pre-existing
  live fixtures against the pinned stack is still pending, but is no longer a corpus-wide gate — see
  below.

The shared problem statement follows; the three entries then take it in pieces.

`testdata/schema/*.json` records `Name` and `File`. It does not record which gvmd the schema came from,
the `@GMP_VERSION@` it declared, a hash of the source XML, or when it was extracted. `vendor/` is
gitignored — correctly, for the AGPL reason in T-9 — so **Stage A's input is not reproducible from the
repository at all.** The only version anchor anywhere in the tree is prose in this file mentioning gvmd
26.36.1, and that is the server the live behavioral experiments ran against, not necessarily the
revision the snapshots were cut from. Nobody, including the person who cut them, can currently answer
"which upstream is `commands/` in parity with" from the repository.

Cheap now, retroactive later: every family adopted before this lands is a snapshot that has to be
re-extracted once it does.

A separate gap exists on the runtime oracle. The live fixtures under `internal/gmptest/testdata/live`
record no server identity either, and `hack/docker-compose.yml` is unpinned. Adoption gates on those
fixtures, so that gate can shift without producing a diff.

**These are two identities, not one, and merging them is a modelling error.** A schema snapshot is
produced by an *extraction* — some method, against some source document, at some time. A live fixture is
produced by a *conversation with a running server*. Putting a gvmd image digest on `schema.Command` is
only coherent when Stage A reads from a live server; the moment it reads a file (which is what it does
today, and what T-16b may or may not change) the field is either empty or, worse, filled in with
whichever server happened to be running. The inverse failure is the one that actually costs evidence:
recording a server identity on schema snapshots and then still having no server identity on the live
captures, which are the artifacts that gate adoption.

So: **schema provenance** attaches to the snapshot, **runtime provenance** attaches to the capture, and
neither borrows a field from the other. They may report the same GMP version; that is a fact worth
cross-checking, not a reason to share a struct.

#### T-16a · The provenance model, and failing closed on mixed provenance
**Status:** done for schema provenance, scoped to content-addressed local extraction (unblocks further
T-9 families) · runtime provenance partial, see below

**Scope note:** "schema provenance done" means content-addressed local extraction provenance — SHA-256
of the exact document read, its declared version, a stable source identity, extraction time, and one
shared value enforced across a batch. That answers "what bytes were used, and do two extractions agree
on them," which is what `checkProvenance` gates on and what "done" refers to below. It deliberately does
not mean reacquirable revision provenance — "which upstream commit would reproduce these bytes" —
because `vendor/` carries no git identity today, so no `SourceRevision` value is available to record
honestly. That gap is real and will require a one-time migration of already-committed snapshots once
T-16b or a pinned vendoring process supplies a revision, but it does not compound per family the way an
absent detection mechanism did (see the "Current sprint" banner above), so it does not block adoption.

Acceptance criteria:

- **schema provenance**, carried by `schema.Command` and therefore by the committed snapshot:
  extraction method (file or served), source identity (path/URL plus upstream tag or commit where
  known), SHA-256 of the source document as read, the protocol/GMP version the document declares, and
  extraction time; **done** for the content-addressed portion — `schema.Provenance`, validated by
  `checkProvenance` (method whitelist, 64-lowercase-hex SHA-256, non-blank trimmed strings, UTC nonzero
  timestamp), all 42 snapshots re-extracted, `gengmp -check` byte-identical. `Source` records the stable
  upstream identity (`gvmd/src/schema_formats/XML/GMP.xml.in`), not the local vendor path;
  `SourceRevision` is an explicit parameter to `schema.Load`, left `""` — see the scope note above.
  Two digests, not one: `SourceSHA256` identifies `GMP.xml.in`, but `Load` parses `split.py`'s
  `command_*.xml` output and nothing binds that output back to the document, so an edited or stale split
  would ride along under an unchanged source hash. `ParsedSHA256` closes that — a digest over the base
  name and bytes of every file actually parsed, validated by `checkProvenance` alongside the other.
- **runtime provenance**, carried by the live-fixture corpus metadata rather than by any schema type:
  resolved container image digest, and the server-reported gvmd and GMP versions; **partial** —
  `Meta.GmpVersion` is populated at capture time from `get_version`'s response. The field was previously
  misnamed `GvmdVersion` despite always holding the GMP protocol version, not a gvmd build version, so
  the 167 pre-existing live fixtures needed a rename, not a backfill: each already carried the value its
  own capturing run's `get_version` call produced, via the same `detectVersion`/`persist` path used
  today. `Meta.GmpVersionSource` records that as `"captured"` for all 167. The `"inferred-from-corpus"`
  label exists but has no producer — it is there so that a future backfill has to declare itself rather
  than pass as captured. A genuine gvmd application/build version and the resolved container image
  digest (`Meta.ImageDigest`, unpopulated) are not obtainable from a GMP conversation at all — they
  depend on the running container, which is T-16c's territory. This portion stays open until T-16c lands.
- Stage A's `-update` path populates the first; the capture path populates the second; Stage B still
  never reads `vendor/`;
- provenance is metadata only — it must not reach `Generate`'s output, so generated bytes stay a pure
  function of shape, and the golden tests prove it;
- mixed schema provenance across a single generation run is an error, not a warning;
- all existing snapshots are re-extracted with provenance in one commit, generated output verified
  byte-identical;
- a snapshot with absent or partial provenance fails generation, so this cannot silently regress;
- runtime provenance has a fail-closed counterpart to `checkProvenance`, in `loadMeta`: a live fixture
  missing `gmp_version` or with an unrecognized `gmp_version_source` fails corpus loading, and an
  upstream fixture setting any of `gmp_version`/`gmp_version_source`/`image_digest` fails it too, since
  upstream fixtures are transcribed documentation and must not claim to have been captured from a live
  server (an upstream fixture setting `image_digest_source` at all, even without a digest, fails too).
  Every live fixture must also declare an `image_digest_source` of either `"resolved"` (a real digest
  from `internal/dockerstack.ImageDigest`, validated by `internal/imageref.ValidateDigest` against the
  full `repository@sha256:<64 lowercase hex>` shape — not just non-blank, so a garbage value like
  `"stable"` or a bare `"sha256:deadbeef"` with no repository fails closed too) or `"legacy-unpinned"`
  (predates T-16c's pinning, so no digest could ever have been recorded honestly; `image_digest` must be
  empty).
  The 167 pre-existing live fixtures are all `"legacy-unpinned"` — a permanent classification, not a
  stopgap, since a fixture captured before pinning existed can never legitimately claim a resolved
  digest. This is a per-fixture marker, not an all-or-nothing corpus gate: each fixture can flip to
  `"resolved"` independently whenever it is next re-captured (see T-16c below), rather than blocking the
  whole corpus until every one of the 167 is redone at once.

Also covered here, since the extraction side is what produces all of the above: `extractionProvenance`
ran only when `vendor/XML` was present, which is never on a clean checkout, so CI exercised the code
that *consumes* provenance and none of the code that *produces* it. `internal/schema/testdata/extraction`
is an original, minimal `GMP.xml.in` plus one invented command file — written for this repository, not
derived from upstream — so extraction, the `<protocol>`-scoped version read, and `ParsedSHA256`'s
sensitivity to an edited split are all covered without the AGPL input.

#### T-16b · Migrate Stage A to live schema extraction
**Status:** done

T-9 flags `<help format="XML" type=""/>` against a live server as the better Stage A source than the
`.in` CMake template: it sidesteps the `@IF_ENABLE_AGENTS@`/`@GMP_VERSION@` substitution problem, and it
is a cleaner AGPL position — a served protocol artifact rather than a vendored source file.

**It does not, on its own, establish gvmd build provenance.** The served document can establish the
*protocol* version it declares. It does not identify the gvmd build or the container digest that served
it; those have to be collected from the runtime environment in the same operation, and a protocol
version in the served XML must not be recorded as though it were a build identity.

Acceptance criteria, all met:

- the extraction records the exact command sent and the response received — `schema.LoadServed`,
  fed the raw `<help>` response bytes from `conn.RawXML`; its `sourceRevision` parameter is validated
  by `internal/imageref.ValidateDigest` against the full `repository@sha256:<64 lowercase hex>` shape
  rather than trusted as an opaque string, so a caller passing a bare tag or a malformed digest fails
  closed instead of silently recording bad provenance. An initial regex-based version of this check
  (`^[^\s@]+@sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$`) still accepted a syntactically invalid repository — an embedded tag
  (`example.com/gvmd:stable@sha256:...`) or an empty path component from a leading, trailing, or doubled
  slash — since it only anchored the digest half; `imageref.ValidateDigest` parses the repository
  component-by-component instead, and is shared with `internal/gmptest`'s and
  `internal/dockerstack`'s own reference handling so the three don't drift independently;
- the server-reported GMP version and, where the server exposes it, the gvmd version — the GMP version
  comes from the served document's own `<protocol><version>`; gvmd's build version is not obtainable
  over GMP at all (unchanged finding, see the "two identities" note above), so this criterion is
  satisfied by the image digest below instead;
- the resolved container image digest, read from the runtime rather than inferred from a tag —
  `internal/dockerstack.ImageDigest`, reading `RepoDigests` off the running `gvmd` container and
  matching them against the repository the Compose service is actually configured to run (via
  `docker compose config --images`), rather than taking `RepoDigests[0]`; fails closed both when no
  entry matches that repository and when more than one does, since either is ambiguous. The repository
  extraction itself (`repo@sha256:...` → `repo`, `repo:tag` → `repo`, a `host:port/repo` registry
  address left alone) now lives in `internal/imageref.Repository`, shared with `internal/cmd/pinimages`'s
  own parsing rather than a second, independently-drifting copy of the same rule;
- the SHA-256 of the returned schema document — `Provenance.SourceSHA256`; since there is no separate
  split step for served extraction, `ParsedSHA256` is identical to it by construction;
- the switch is proven by extracting the current families both ways and diffing the resulting snapshots
  modulo provenance — done; see the parity check below.

**Parity check found 10 of 63 families with a real shape diff, not zero — every one upstream drift, none
a bug in the new extraction path:**

| Families | Diff | Explanation |
|---|---|---|
| `create_task`, `get_tasks`, `modify_target` | `hosts_ordering` removed live | The exact failure this file's own T-9 write-up predicted: present in v23.9.0, gone from `main`, and `commands/create_task.go` still carried `HostsOrdering` until this cutover — see T-17 |
| `get_credentials` | `+private_key_info`, `+public_key_info` | New upstream fields (`private_key_info` was already seen live and flagged not-yet-modelled, above) |
| `get_notes`, `get_overrides` | `+oci_image` on the embedded result | New upstream field, independently on each family's own result type |
| `get_scanners` | `+disabled` (boolean), and `+usage_type` on the embedded task reference | New upstream fields |
| `create_target`, `get_targets` | `alive_tests` reshaped from an optional scalar to a repeated container | Upstream now allows multiple simultaneous alive-test methods |
| `create_override` | `new_severity`/`new_threat` moved position in the schema (and gained `InChoice`) | Real reordering; `InChoice` itself is advisory-only and not read by the generator |
| `get_tasks` | 3 fewer schema `Defects` (`duplicate-ref` on `result_count/hole`\|`info`\|`warning`) | Upstream fixed a known schema malformation — live schema is cleaner, not worse |

Chose full cutover over recording-only: this is an alpha library (`CLAUDE.md`'s policy — make breaking
changes now, update every call site, no compatibility shims), so `testdata/schema/*.json` and
`commands/*.go` were regenerated for all 10 families in the same change rather than carrying a second,
increasingly stale "what upstream actually looks like" fact alongside the adopted one. Call-site fallout
was one non-generated reference (`internal/gmptest/capture_sequence_test.go`'s `AliveTests` literal) plus
two request-golden fixtures whose field order shifted (`create_override`'s `severities_set`/
`severities_zero`); both fixed in the same change. `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md` now
legitimately shows these 10 families as diverging from the older vendored schema — that is the vendored
copy being stale relative to the live-derived commit, not a regression.

The cutover's own `TestSnapshot` initially still diffed byte-shape against the committed snapshot, which
made it fail permanently on exactly these 10 families — a false alarm baked into every future run, not a
regression check, since `vendor/XML/commands` and the live server are two independently-versioned
oracles that are now expected to disagree. `TestSnapshot` was narrowed to prove only that the file-based
fallback extraction still works (parses, covers every manifest command, marshals cleanly); the
byte-shape-vs-committed-snapshot comparison moved to its own opt-in test,
`internal/codegen/vendor_parity_test.go`'s `TestVendorParity` (run with `-vendor-parity`) — a manual,
T-17-style drift detector that is *expected* to report these 10 families as long as the local vendor
mirror and the live server disagree, and correctly does.

A second-pass review caught that `TestSnapshot` still accepted `-update`, and that path wrote
file-derived snapshots straight into `testdata/schema/*.json` — the same directory `TestSnapshotLive`
writes to — silently downgrading a snapshot's provenance from `ExtractionServed` back to
`ExtractionFile` and reintroducing exactly the mixed-identity state T-16a's fail-closed check exists to
catch. `TestSnapshot` no longer supports `-update` at all; `TestSnapshotLive` is now the only path that
writes those files, so a file-based run can no longer touch authoritative provenance by accident.

Also fixed in the same remediation pass: `get_notes`'s and `get_overrides`'s new `oci_image` element
generated as `OciImage`/`NoteOciImage`/`OverrideOciImage`, breaking the generator's own initialism
casing convention (`API`, `CVE`, `TLS`, ...). Added `"oci": "OCI"` to `generate.go`'s `initialisms`
table and a naming-stability test, `TestOCIImageNamingIsStable`, alongside the existing
`TestScheduleICalendarNamingIsStable`/`TestAlertFingerprintNamingIsStable`; `commands/get_notes.go` and
`commands/get_overrides.go` regenerated to `OCIImage`/`NoteOCIImage`/`OverrideOCIImage`.

#### T-16c · Pin the integration stack
**Status:** done · **Pinning, wiring, and the refresh procedure land; re-capturing the 167 pre-existing
live fixtures is still pending but no longer gates the corpus** · **Does not block T-9**

Pinning `:stable` is not enough, and the earlier framing of this as "pin `gvmd:stable` and the other
`:stable` images" understated it. Of the (corrected count) 17 image references in
`hack/docker-compose.yml`, **10 carried no tag at all** — implicit `:latest`, moving on every pull:
`vulnerability-tests`, `notus-data`, `scap-data`, `cert-bund-data`, `dfn-cert-data`, `data-objects`,
`report-formats`, `gpg-data`, `redis-server` and `gvm-tools`. The remaining 7 used `:stable` (`gvmd`,
`pg-gvm`, `gsa`, `openvas-scanner` ×3, `ospd-openvas`). The feed and data images are the ones that
determine what objects exist, which report formats are installed, and what a `get_*` response contains —
they change fixture content directly, and they were exactly the ones with no tag.

Acceptance criteria — chose full pinning over a smaller boundary, since the doc's own analysis above is
skeptical any boundary excluding the feed/data images can be honestly justified:

- every image in `hack/docker-compose.yml` is pinned to an immutable digest, done — each `image:` line
  is now `<repo>@sha256:<digest>` with the resolved-from tag kept as a trailing comment;
- documented, actually-working refresh procedure, done — `hack/pin-images.sh` is now a thin wrapper over
  `internal/cmd/pinimages`, which re-resolves *every* line including already-pinned ones (recovering
  what each was originally pinned from — a bare repo or `repo:tag` — from its own trailing comment) and
  rewrites the file only where a digest actually moved, so a no-op run is byte-identical. The original
  shell script only ever resolved unpinned lines, so after the first pinning commit every line was
  already pinned and every subsequent run was a permanent no-op — the documented "re-run to move a pin"
  procedure could never fire again. Table-driven tests in `internal/cmd/pinimages/pinimages_test.go`
  cover the state machine (unpinned, already-pinned unchanged, already-pinned moved, untagged, and a
  missing or unparseable recovery comment failing closed rather than silently freezing a pin) without
  needing Docker; the refresh itself is manual and reviewed via the PR diff it produces, not scheduled,
  so a feed bump is always a visible decision rather than unreviewed supply-chain drift. An initial
  version of `parseImageLine` split an unpinned reference at its first `:`, which misparsed a
  `host:port` registry address (`localhost:5000/community/gvmd:stable` came out as repository
  `localhost` instead of `localhost:5000/community/gvmd`); it now shares `internal/imageref.Repository`
  with `internal/dockerstack`'s digest resolution, the same "a `:` only introduces a tag when it occurs
  after the last `/`" rule, with tests covering both a tagged and an untagged reference behind a
  `host:port` registry.

The resolved digest of the images actually used is recorded in the runtime provenance of any capture
taken against them (T-16a): `captureRun` resolves it via `internal/dockerstack.ImageDigest` (the same
resolver T-16b uses) and `persist` writes it to `Meta.ImageDigest` and `Meta.ImageDigestSource:
"resolved"`. `loadMeta` requires every live fixture to declare an `image_digest_source`, but — unlike an
initial draft of this gate — that requirement does not depend on backfilling a digest that was never
recorded: the 167 pre-existing live fixtures were batch-marked `"legacy-unpinned"` (digest legitimately
absent, not missing-by-oversight), so the corpus loads and the fidelity/round-trip tests run today.
Re-capturing those 167 against the pinned stack so each carries a real `"resolved"` digest is still
pending, blocked on rotating the live gvmd's admin credential, but it is now an opportunistic per-fixture
upgrade rather than a gate on the whole corpus (tracked separately as task T-16c's re-capture step, not a
T-16c design question).

**Superseded:** `internal/cmd/pinimages` and `hack/pin-images.sh`, described above, were retired once
T-22 landed — mirroring subsumed digest pinning rather than duplicating it, see T-22 below.

#### T-22 · Mirror the pinned Greenbone stack into a controlled registry
**Status:** done, operationally verified (T-30) · **Depends on:** T-16c · **Does not block the T-16b/c cutover fixes

A digest pin makes an image immutable while the source registry retains it, but does not make the blob
reacquirable if Greenbone garbage-collects an untagged manifest, changes retention policy, removes a
repository, or is unavailable. Mirror every distinct image repository used by
`hack/docker-compose.yml` into GHCR; the three services using `openvas-scanner` are one matrix entry,
not three copies. Use Skopeo for the image transfer. ORAS is not a replacement for Skopeo here; it is
optional for attaching provenance or an SBOM after the image has been copied.

Acceptance criteria:

- a scheduled and manually dispatchable workflow covers every distinct Compose image and preserves all
  platforms with `skopeo copy --all`;
- each mutable upstream channel (`stable` or implicit `latest`) is resolved once, then inspected and
  copied exclusively through its immutable `repo@sha256:...` reference so a tag move cannot race label
  inspection or copying;
- destination tags derived from `org.opencontainers.image.version` and
  `org.opencontainers.image.revision` are validated as OCI tag names; missing or inconsistent labels
  across platforms fail closed, with a source-digest-derived immutable tag available as the fallback
  identity;
- version, revision, and source-digest tags are write-once: an existing tag pointing to different
  content fails rather than moving silently; only an explicitly documented convenience tag may move;
- post-copy verification records the source repository/channel/digest, destination repository/digest,
  version, revision, platforms, and capture time in a machine-readable lock or attestation; source and
  destination manifest digests are not assumed equal;
- Compose remains pinned to reviewed destination digests. Discovery/mirroring may be scheduled, but a
  separate PR updates the lock and `hack/docker-compose.yml`, so the integration stack never follows a
  moving mirror tag automatically;
- if OCI artifacts are added, ORAS attaches the provenance/SBOM to the destination digest; authenticity
  is handled separately (for example with Cosign), not inferred merely from mirroring.

Implemented as `internal/mirror` (the Skopeo-driving logic, unit-tested against an injected command
runner, no live registry needed) and `internal/cmd/mirrorimages` (the CLI: resolve, inspect, plan, copy,
write-once-tag, lock-write, and Compose-rewrite, one image at a time, wired through
`hack/mirror-images.sh`). `hack/image-mirror.lock.json` — not `hack/docker-compose.yml` — is the durable
seed of what to mirror from: each run reads the prior run's lock entries (`SourceRepository`,
`SourceChannel`), re-resolves them, and on success writes both an updated lock file and a rewritten
Compose file in one pass. This replaced an earlier two-tool design: `internal/cmd/pinimages` recovered a
Compose line's mutable channel from a trailing comment and re-resolved it with `docker buildx`, while
`internal/mirror` recovered the *same* channel from the *same* comment to re-resolve it with Skopeo —
once Compose points at the mirror instead of upstream Greenbone, that comment-recovery parsing would
recover the wrong registry entirely. `pinimages`, `hack/pin-images.sh`, and the comment-recovery grammar
in `internal/imageref` (`ParseComposeImageLine`'s pinned/comment branch, `OriginalRef`, `UntaggedComment`)
were deleted rather than adapted; `internal/imageref.ImageLineRepository` is the one piece
`RewriteCompose` still needs from that package. Destination namespace is
`ghcr.io/brennoo/gvm-mirror/<name>`; every tag except an explicitly configured movable one is write-once,
and version/revision tags fail closed per-image (not per-run) when
`org.opencontainers.image.version`/`.revision` are missing or disagree across platforms, falling back to
a `sha256-<digest>` tag that never depends on labels.

A follow-up review before this landed found two blocking gaps, both fixed: the fallback
digest-derived tag was written through a raw `skopeo copy` rather than `WriteOnceTag`, so a second run
against a since-moved fallback tag could silently overwrite it instead of failing closed; and
`skopeo copy --all --preserve-digests` was trusted to reproduce every source platform's manifest
byte-for-byte with no destination-side check. `mirrorOne` now writes the fallback tag through
`WriteOnceTag` like every other tag, then re-inspects the destination digest's platforms and calls
`mirror.VerifyPlatformsMatch` against the source platforms (order-independent, by OS/Arch/digest) before
tagging version/revision. Two lower-severity findings from the same review are also fixed: a
single-platform image previously recorded its platform as `"/"` (empty OS/Arch, since a single-manifest
`skopeo inspect --raw` carries no `manifests[]` list to read them from) — `InspectPlatforms` now makes a
second, non-raw `skopeo inspect` call for that case; and `.github/workflows/mirror-images.yml`'s
`actions/checkout`/`actions/setup-go`/`actions/upload-artifact` references are pinned to commit SHAs, not
just mutable version tags, since this is the one workflow in the repo holding `packages: write`.

`.github/workflows/mirror-images.yml` runs weekly (`workflow_dispatch` too) with `packages: write`,
performs real `skopeo copy` pushes to GHCR, and uploads the resulting lock file as a build artifact —
it never touches git. Repointing `hack/docker-compose.yml` at the mirror and committing a refreshed
`hack/image-mirror.lock.json` happen together in one `hack/mirror-images.sh` run, but committing that
diff stays a separate, human-reviewed step — the workflow itself never commits.

**Extracted to its own repository — see T-30.** Everything above describing `internal/mirror`,
`internal/cmd/mirrorimages`, `hack/mirror-images.sh`, and `.github/workflows/mirror-images.yml` is
historical: that code and workflow moved wholesale to
[brennoo/gvm-mirror](https://github.com/brennoo/gvm-mirror), which is a true leaf with no GMP/schema
coupling. `hack/image-mirror.lock.json` here is now a reviewed vendored copy, not the seed a local run
mirrors from; `internal/imageref.ImageLineRepository`/`ValidateTagName` moved with it, since their only
callers were the deleted files. The design and acceptance criteria above are unchanged and still hold,
with one exception T-30 surfaced operationally: "version, revision, and source-digest tags are
write-once" is not satisfiable for Greenbone's nightly images, whose rebuilds can reuse a revision label
for genuinely different content — see T-30 for specifics.

## P2 — Testing, tooling, docs

### T-10 · Integration harness and live capture workflow
**Status:** done · **CI policy:** integration workflow is manual-only; regular CI builds and vets integration-tag code

Implemented an integration-tag readiness ladder, `hack/gvmd-up.sh` / `hack/gvmd-down.sh`, live
capture/transcription support, and secret-safe persistence. The integration workflow is intentionally
`workflow_dispatch` only; ordinary CI still builds and vets with `-tags integration` so the harness
cannot silently rot. Live runs remain operator-triggered because they require the full Greenbone stack
and may exercise stateful commands.

### T-11 · No `staticcheck`/`gosimple` in the linter set
**Status:** done · **File:** `.golangci.yml`

Enabled. Fallout was 5 real issues, all fixed: a deprecated `tls.VersionSSL30` in a test listener
(swapped for `VersionTLS10`, equally unsupported by a TLS-1.3-minimum client, so the test's intent
survives), `io/ioutil` in `conn_unix_test.go`, a redundant type in `NewGMPError`, and an if/else-if
chain on one value that became a tagged switch.

The enabled set is good (`errorlint`, `contextcheck`, `nilerr`, `gosec`, `bodyclose`, …) and currently
reports 0 issues, but `staticcheck` is absent — its inclusion in `golangci-lint`'s `staticcheck` linter
already covers what used to be the separate `gosimple`/`stylecheck` checks in current versions, so this
is one gap, not two. It would have caught `for true {` in `conn.go:32` (should be `for {`), among other
things.

`contextcheck` is already enabled (verified in `.golangci.yml`) and reports 0 issues — it does not, and
structurally cannot, flag T-3: it checks that a context already in scope is propagated correctly to
callees that accept one, not that a public method fails to accept a `ctx` parameter in the first place.
T-3's `context.Background()` call sites are outside what any context-propagation linter checks; no
existing or added lint rule would have caught them.

### T-12 · Missing repository and package documentation
**Status:** done — `doc.go` for all six packages, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md`, a
stated GMP version compatibility note, and the restructured README error-handling section

- No `doc.go` in any package; nothing renders as an overview on pkg.go.dev beyond the README.
- No `CHANGELOG.md`, despite goreleaser publishing tagged releases and the README warning about
  breaking changes — users have no way to see what broke between tags.
- No `CONTRIBUTING.md` or `SECURITY.md`. For a security-tooling library, a disclosure policy is
  table stakes.
- No stated GMP version compatibility. The README links the GMP 22.6 docs; the compose stack pulls
  `stable` / feed release 24.10. Which protocol versions are actually supported is unspecified.
- Examples all pass `insecure = true` with no comment on why it is unsafe in production (see T-4).

### T-13 · No connection lifecycle management, and poisoning is broader than just cancellation
**Status:** in-progress — the boundary-known/unknown poisoning classification and the write-count
check are done (`connections/internal/implementation/conn.go`); reconnect, keepalive, backoff, and a
public `Client.Close()` remain

No reconnect, no keepalive, no retry/backoff, and no re-authentication after a dropped connection.

Context cancellation is the *obvious* poisoning case — `Execute` deliberately closes the socket on
`ctx.Done()`, permanently killing the `Connection` for every later call with no recovery path — but
it's not the only one, and framing this as only a cancellation problem understates the scope. The
distinguishing question for each failure is whether it leaves message-boundary position **known** or
**unknown** — only the latter requires poisoning the connection:

- **Any partial write** (deadline hit mid-write, transient network error) leaves the server having
  received a fragment of a command. The client doesn't know whether the server will act on it, ignore
  it, or hang waiting for the rest — boundary unknown, poison.
- **Any read timeout, EOF before the root closes, or malformed/unframeable XML** (see T-1) leaves the
  socket's read position at an unknown point relative to message boundaries. If the connection is
  reused, the *next* `Execute` call may read the tail of the previous, still-in-flight response and
  misinterpret it as its own — silent data corruption, not just an error — boundary unknown, poison.
- **A fully-framed document that fails typed `xml.Unmarshal`** is different, once T-1 lands: decoder-based
  framing means the root element's matching `EndElement` was already consumed cleanly, so the *stream*
  position is known — the reader is correctly positioned at the start of whatever comes next. Only the
  *decode into a concrete Go type* failed, which is a schema/version-drift signal (T-7a/T-9), not a
  transport-safety one. Poisoning the connection on every such mismatch would kill perfectly usable
  connections on ordinary version drift. Do not poison here; surface the typed decode error and leave
  the connection usable.

For the boundary-unknown cases, retrying on the same stream is unsafe because protocol synchronization
is no longer known. **Any transport/protocol failure that leaves message-boundary position ambiguous
must mark the connection permanently unusable** — but a clean decode failure into the wrong struct must
not. Reconnect/re-authentication, when added, must always happen on a fresh connection — never by
resuming reads/writes on one that hit an ambiguous failure.

**Related, currently unhandled: the write return count is discarded.** `performRequest` does
`_, err := conn.rawConn.Write(buffer)` (`conn.go:26`) and never checks `n`. `io.Writer`'s contract does
require implementations to return a non-nil error whenever `n < len(p)` — but that is an obligation on
the *implementation*, and checking `n` is the caller's obligation regardless. It is not merely
defensive: `io.WriteString`, wrapper types, and above all **this repo's own hand-written test mocks**
(every test runs against them — T-10) can and do exhibit short-write behavior that a real TCP socket
would not. Beyond that, the *real* defect the discard hides is that when `Write` fails after
transmitting some bytes, the current code returns the error and leaves the connection fully reusable —
precisely the ambiguous-partial-write case above. Acceptance criteria:

- check `n == len(buffer)`; treat a short write as ambiguous (`io.ErrShortWrite`) and poison;
- poison on *any* write error, since partial transmission cannot be ruled out;
- do not silently retry the remainder — resuming a half-sent command is only safe if retry semantics are
  deliberately designed, which they are not today.

**State handling lives in T-2b, not here.** A single unsynchronized `broken` bool is both racy (once
T-2 lands) and too coarse; the `open`/`broken`/`closed` model, its ownership rules, and how it interacts
with `Close` are specified in T-2b. T-13's contribution is the *classification* above — which failures
justify the `open → broken` transition and which do not.

`Client` also exposes no `Close()`, so callers must hold the `Connection` separately to shut down.

### T-14 · No observability hooks
**Status:** todo

No logging, tracing, or metrics hooks, and no supported way to dump wire XML when a response fails to
parse. For a protocol client whose failure mode is "the server sent XML we didn't model" (T-8), a
debug hook exposing raw request/response bytes is the single highest-value diagnostic affordance.

### T-17 · Upstream schema drift is undetectable
**Status:** done, dispatched (T-30) but blocked on infra · **Depends on:** T-16a (T-16b made it cheaper, not possible)

Generation is half the reason to generate; the other half is being told when upstream moves, and that
half did not exist. Nothing in CI or in the repository noticed a changed `GMP.xml`. The parity claim
rested entirely on someone deciding to look, which is the same failure mode hand-transcription had —
T-9's own "version drift is already biting" note found `hosts_ordering` present in `v23.9.0` and gone
from `main` while `commands/create_task.go` still carried `HostsOrdering`, and that was found by hand.

The diff engine, `internal/codegen.DetectDrift`/`DriftReport` (`internal/codegen/drift.go`), takes a
live `[]schema.Command` and diffs it against the committed `testdata/schema/*.json` snapshots plus
`manifest.json`, reporting per command so the output ranks work rather than saying "something changed":
commands gained/removed, and for adopted families the specific nodes added, removed or retyped.

The harder design question was *what feeds it*. Diffing against the currently-*pinned* gvmd
(`hack/docker-compose.yml`'s digest) would almost never show drift, because T-16c's recapture flow keeps
the pinned digest and `testdata/schema` in lockstep by construction — that comparison is a snapshot
compared to itself. `internal/cmd/schemadrift` instead runs in two modes: `-resolve-only` re-resolves
gvmd's upstream channel fresh every run (`hack/image-mirror.lock.json`'s `source_channel`, via the same
`mirror.ResolveDigest` `mirrorimages` uses) and prints the resulting digest, independent of whatever is
pinned; the default report mode dials an already-running gvmd, sends `<help format="XML" type=""/>`,
resolves the *served* container's own image digest, runs it through `schema.LoadServed` and
`DetectDrift`, and prints a pinned-vs-served digest header ahead of the report so a reader sees at a
glance whether upstream has moved at all before reading the node-level diff.

Verifying that end-to-end against a real stack (rather than trusting the fake-`Runner`/fake-`Connection`
unit tests alone) surfaced a real bug in `internal/dockerstack.ImageDigest`, used by this report mode and
predating T-17: `configuredImage` resolved a service's image via `compose config --images <service>`,
which — at least on Compose v2.30 — prints every transitively `depends_on`'d service's image too, not
just the one asked for. `gvmd` depends on six other services in `hack/docker-compose.yml`, so this
always failed with "expected exactly one configured image, got 7" the moment it ran against a real
stack, rather than the mocked one `internal/codegen/snapshot_live_test.go` and
`internal/gmptest/capture_test.go`'s integration-tagged tests exercise. Fixed by reading the *running
container's* own configured image directly (`docker inspect <container> --format '{{.Config.Image}}'`)
instead of re-deriving it from the Compose file — a container can't be ambiguous about what image it was
started from the way a multi-service `compose config` query can.

`hack/schema-drift.sh` wires the two modes together: it resolves the fresh candidate digest, boots a
throwaway stack from it via a Compose override (`hack/docker-compose.schema-drift-override.yml`,
layered onto the checked-in `hack/docker-compose.yml` through a `COMPOSE_OVERRIDE_FILE` hook added to
`hack/compose-lib.sh`'s `dc()`), and runs the report against that candidate — never touching the lock
file, the Compose pins, or the committed snapshots. `.github/workflows/schema-drift.yml` runs this on a
weekly `schedule` plus `workflow_dispatch`, writing the report to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`, and
deliberately stays off `pull_request`/`push` and out of the required check set — it fails for reasons
outside the tree (upstream moved, registry unreachable) and must not be able to block an unrelated PR.
Optionally opening or updating an issue from the report was considered and explicitly left out of this
first version — real additional scope (auth, dedup-by-reopening-vs-creating, noise control) that
deserves its own review.

**T-16b's cutover produced the first real instance of this failure mode, still found by hand.** A
one-off parity check (extract the same 63 adopted commands both from `vendor/XML/commands` and from a
live gvmd, diff modulo provenance) found 10 families had genuinely drifted — see T-16b above for the
full table. It confirms the `hosts_ordering` prediction two paragraphs up exactly: gone from
`create_task`, `get_tasks` and `modify_target` in the live schema. T-16b makes this check cheaper (no
AGPL vendoring step, no manual diffing script) but the mechanical, scheduled version described above
still does not exist — this was a manual `go test -update` run and a hand-written diff, not an alarm
that fires on its own.

That one-off check now has a reusable, repeatable form, `internal/codegen.TestVendorParity` (`-vendor-parity`,
see T-16b above), so re-running it no longer requires reconstructing the diff by hand — but it is still a
manually-invoked local test against whatever `vendor/XML/commands` happens to be checked out, not the
scheduled job against a pinned server this entry calls for. Re-running it today still correctly reports
the same 10 families.

A follow-up review before this landed found three gaps, all fixed: report mode resolved the running
container's served digest but never checked it against the digest `-resolve-only` produced, so a stale or
reused container, or a channel that moved between resolution and boot, could be reported as the intended
candidate with no indication — `schemadrift` now takes `-expect-digest` (wired through by
`hack/schema-drift.sh`) and fails closed unless the two match by SHA-256 content, before dialing or
diffing anything. `DetectDrift`'s `byName` map silently kept the last of two same-named live commands, so
a malformed served schema declaring a command twice could produce a misleadingly clean report — it now
rejects a duplicate command identity outright, per this project's fail-closed schema-handling rules. And
`.github/workflows/schema-drift.yml`'s summary step piped its whole body (report generation included) into
`$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` under `set -e`, so a failure partway through — upstream unreachable, stack failed to
boot, auth failed — dropped the closing code fence and any stderr diagnostics, defeating the point of an
informational check whose main artifact should survive expected external failures; the step now captures
combined stdout/stderr and the exit status explicitly, always writes a complete report/error block, then
exits with the original status so the run is still visibly red.

**Operationally dispatched, blocked on infra — see T-30.** Two `workflow_dispatch` runs on GitHub both
died with exit 137 (SIGKILL) inside the "Resolve candidate, boot, and report drift" step — 9 minutes into
the first, 3m21s into the second — before writing anything to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`, matching
`.github/workflows/integration.yml`'s own header comment that this stack's disk/memory footprint on a
GitHub-hosted runner has never been measured and "may simply not fit." The `if: always()`/exit-status
plumbing this section describes is real but cannot help here: the whole shell running
`output="$(hack/schema-drift.sh ...)"` was killed externally, so there was no exit status to capture and
no output to have captured. The teardown step (`hack/gvmd-down.sh --purge`) did complete on both runs
(`if: always()`), so at least that half of the design is confirmed. Getting a real report requires either
a larger runner or trimming this stack's footprint — out of scope for T-30, which was verification only.

### T-18 · No multi-version story, and no compatibility matrix
**Status:** todo · **Depends on:** T-16a, T-17

Three version axes are currently conflated, and the answer differs for each: the **GMP protocol version**
(`22.6`, what `<get_version>` returns and `@GMP_VERSION@` holds), the **gvmd release** (26.x/27.x,
Greenbone's calendar versioning), and this **module's semver**.

Matching the module version to the gvmd version — `v27.7.0` for gvmd 27.7 — is a valid semver tag and a
trap. The first breaking API change forces v28, the major version permanently decouples from gvmd at
exactly the moment it was supposed to signal compatibility, v2+ requires the `/v2` import path suffix,
and none of it can be walked back. The module keeps its own semver. Compatibility is expressed as data,
not as a version number: version the *snapshot*, publish a *matrix*.

The design question this entry exists to answer: when a protocol version adds a field, does the module
emit build-tagged variants, one superset struct with everything optional, or one module version per
protocol version? **Recommendation: the superset.** GMP is additive and `encoding/xml` ignores unknown
elements on decode, so a superset decodes older and newer servers alike — and the current single-tree
design already *is* the superset, implicitly. Declaring it is nearly free today and expensive after a
second version's snapshots exist.

Acceptance criteria:

- snapshots live under a per-protocol-version directory (`testdata/schema/22.6/…`) rather than one flat
  tree; `Generate` is already pure, so a second version's tree costs almost nothing to produce;
- the superset policy is stated in `doc.go`, not just here, including what it does *not* promise —
  a field present in the struct is not a field every supported server sends;
- `docs/COMPATIBILITY.md` is generated, not hand-written, from recorded provenance plus the schema's own
  92 `<change>` and 4 `<deprecation>` entries, which are a machine-readable migration log nothing
  currently reads;
- it is **committed and checked in CI** the way `commands/` already is — a read-only regenerate-and-diff
  step alongside `gengmp -check`, failing if the committed copy is stale. "Derived from provenance" with
  no check is just a second hand-maintained copy that happens to have been correct once;
- `README.md` **links** to it rather than restating any version. The current hand-written "developed and
  tested against GMP 22.6" line is deleted, not regenerated in place: a version number duplicated into
  prose is a drift source whether or not a generator originally put it there, and the check above cannot
  see it.

### T-18a · Relocate schema-declared sort chardata to its observed wire child
**Status:** done · **Files:** `internal/codegen/overrides.go`, `internal/codegen/generate.go`,
`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`, `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`, `commands/chardata_test.go`,
25 adopted `commands/*.go` families

Scoping this out found the affected set was larger than the doc text's original 7-command guess: grepping
every `*_fidelity.txt` fixture for `sort/field/#text` `DROPPED` findings surfaced **25** commands sharing
the identical schema/wire disagreement — the schema declares text on `<sort>` itself, while live and
upstream XML actually place the sort-field name one level deeper, inside `<sort><field>`. The 25:
`get_alerts`, `get_assets`, `get_configs`, `get_credentials`, `get_filters`, `get_groups`, `get_info`,
`get_notes`, `get_overrides`, `get_permissions`, `get_port_lists`, `get_report_configs`,
`get_report_formats`, `get_reports`, `get_results`, `get_roles`, `get_scanners`, `get_schedules`,
`get_tags`, `get_targets`, `get_tasks`, `get_tickets`, `get_tls_certificates`, `get_users`, `get_vulns`.
`get_agents` shares the same schema shape but is excluded: it is a deferred, unadopted family
(`ENABLE_AGENTS` isn't in the pinned `gvmd:stable` image), so its finding is `MISSING`, not
`SCHEMA-DEFECT`, and there is no adopted generated code to relocate anything in.

Implemented as a generic, fail-closed chardata relocation rather than a node-name heuristic:

- `FieldSuppression` gained a `Chardata` discriminator mirroring `FieldOverride.Chardata`, mutually
  exclusive with `Name`/`Attr`;
- `FieldSuppressions`/`FieldOverrides` gained 25 bidirectionally-linked pairs — each suppresses the
  schema-derived wrapper chardata at `<command>:/sort` and adds `FieldOverride{Chardata: true, GoType:
  "string"}` at `<command>:/sort/field`, tied together via `RelocatedTo`/`RelocatedFrom`;
