- `validateFieldRelocations`/`hasRelocationCounterpart`/`hasSuppressionCounterpart` now match relocation
  pairs on a `chardata bool` alongside `name`/`attr`, and reject mutual exclusivity violations, missing
  counterparts, and structural-dedup disagreement the same way the existing named/attribute relocation
  path already did;
- `fieldSuppressionsFor` returns `(map[fieldKey]bool, *FieldSuppression, error)`, the `*FieldSuppression`
  carrying the chardata relocation (nil when the struct has none); `emitStruct` skips
  `emitChardataOverridable` when it's set and folds it into the `chardata` bool used for collision-method
  generation. `fieldOf` (via the new `withChardataRelocationReason` helper, split out to keep its
  cyclomatic complexity under the `gocyclo` threshold) propagates that suppression's `Reason` onto the
  *wrapper field's* `gmpoverride` tag — the wrapper struct itself has nothing left to carry it on once its
  chardata is gone — so `internal/schema`'s reflection-based comparer can read it off `GoNode.OverrideReason`
  and classify both the source-side MISSING and the destination-side EXTRA as suppressed, rather than the
  relocation surfacing as an actionable regression;
- focused generator unit tests cover the successful relocation, `Chardata`+`Name` both set, a suppression
  with no override counterpart, and structural-dedup disagreement between two uses of the same struct;
  focused comparer tests in `internal/schema` cover the conformance side directly: a paired relocation
  suppresses both the source MISSING and the destination EXTRA, and an unpaired move (no counterpart
  `OverrideReason` on the other side) stays actionable.

Regeneration gave every one of the 25 families the desired shape: the wrapper's
`Value string \`xml:",chardata"\`` is gone, and the nested field type gains its own
`Value string \`xml:",chardata" gmpoverride:"..."\`` alongside its existing `Order`. `commands/chardata_test.go`'s
`TestChardataRoundTripSort` round-trips the new shape directly. Fidelity fixtures and
`internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md` were regenerated for all 25: every `DROPPED
/..._response/sort/field/#text` finding closes, `get_agents` untouched. The now-closed loss made ten
families' `Test<Family>SortFieldNameIsAnAcceptedLoss`/`Test<Family>SortValueIsWhitespaceOnly` tests in
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go` factually obsolete (they existed specifically to document this loss as
permanent); removed for group, tag, schedule, alert, filter, tls_certificate, permission, ticket,
credential, and port_list. The sibling `Test<Family>CountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` tests document an
unrelated accepted loss (`role_count`-style bare totals) and were left untouched.

### T-19 · `<alts>` enumerations are parsed and then dropped
**Status:** done · **Files:** `internal/codegen/overrides.go`, `internal/codegen/generate.go`,
`internal/codegen/check.go`, `internal/codegen/enum_test.go`, `internal/codegen/enum_inventory_test.go`,
`internal/codegen/check_test.go`, `internal/codegen/testdata/ENUM_INVENTORY.md`, 37 adopted
`commands/*.go` families plus one new shared file (`commands/config_shared.go`), `client/client_test.go`,
`internal/schema/testdata/OPTIONAL_SCALAR_INVENTORY.md`, `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md`

`Node.Alts` is populated by the parser and used in exactly one place: `writeHash`, the structural
dedup key. It never reached the emitted Go, so every enumerated field generated as a bare `string` —
T-9's own DSL mapping table says `<alts><alt>` should produce a const block.

Resolved by emitting an open named string type plus constants for every `Alts`-bearing field, e.g.:

```go
type CredentialType string

const (
	CredentialTypeCc    CredentialType = "cc"
	CredentialTypeKrb5  CredentialType = "krb5"
	...
)
```

Open, not closed: the underlying type is still `string`, so an unknown server value decodes unchanged —
no custom `MarshalXML`/`UnmarshalXML`, no validation, no rejection. This had to land before API
stabilization, since turning an already-stable `string` field into a named type later would be a breaking
change; alpha is the only point where it's free.

Corpus-wide, 94 `Alts`-bearing nodes collapse to **77** distinct generated enum types (family-local
dedup plus cross-occurrence agreement checks — every case that could collide across occurrences of the
same wire name was verified to agree on the same alt set within a family, so there was nothing to
reconcile). The full inventory, extracted directly from the generated `commands/*.go` bytes via
`go/parser`/`go/ast` rather than re-derived by a second collection pass, is committed at
`internal/codegen/testdata/ENUM_INVENTORY.md` and is `-update`-gated like every other golden here.

Two things the research surfaced that needed deliberate handling rather than reusing the existing struct
naming machinery outright:

- **Symbol-only alt values.** The `relation` field (GMP filter/keyword relation operator) appears across
  ~26 commands with alt values that are bare punctuation: `=`, `:`, `~`, `>`, `<`. Running the existing
  `goName`/`goWord` identifier machinery on these is a no-op (no word boundary, `ToUpper` on a
  non-letter does nothing), so it would either emit invalid Go or silently collapse every operator to the
  same degenerate fallback. Added one small, protocol-wide (not family-specific) override table,
  `EnumConstantNames` in `overrides.go`, keyed by literal wire value rather than by family — the meaning
  of `=` as "equals" is intrinsic to the value, so one entry replaces the same fix 26 times instead of
  reaching for a family-specific alias, which the task's constraint explicitly ruled out absent a real,
  reviewed need. Consumption is tracked once across the whole generation run and a never-used entry is a
  hard error, the same fail-closed policy every other override table in this generator already has.
- **Dual-role nodes.** Three nodes (`get_report_configs.type`, `get_report_formats.trust`,
  `get_report_formats.type`) need both a struct (for their attributes) and their own chardata enum. No
  special case was needed: the struct-naming pass already claims the bare name first, so the enum naming
  ladder's first candidate is already taken and falls through to the next qualified candidate — the exact
  mechanism struct-vs-struct collisions already used. `get_report_formats.trust` resolves to the
  command-qualified `GetReportFormatsTrust`, visible in the committed inventory.

Fail-closed checks added, all as hard generation errors rather than silent fallbacks: empty `Alts`
reaching the enum path, duplicate alt values within one field, two alt values colliding on the same Go
constant suffix, structurally-deduped fields disagreeing on `Alts` across occurrences, a naming collision
the ladder can't resolve, and any unconsumed `EnumConstantNames` entry. The unconsumed-entry check is
factored into `checkEnumConstantNamesConsumed` and called from both `GenerateAll` (the write path) and
`CheckOutput` (`gengmp -check`, the CI path) — an earlier version only ran it from `GenerateAll`, so
`-check` would silently accept a stale entry and only the write path would ever catch it. Covered by
`TestCheckOutputRejectsStaleEnumConstantNameEntry`.

Regenerating `commands/*.go` from the manifest changed exactly the 82 fields the inventory predicts
(scalar `string` → the field's enum type, `[]string` → `[]EnumType` where the field is a slice, e.g.
`CredentialFormats.Format`, `GetUsersSources.Source`) and nothing else — verified by diffing every
changed generated file's struct-field lines against the committed tree before and after. Call sites
assigning raw string literals to a now-enum scalar field needed no change (an untyped string constant
converts implicitly to a named string type), but slice literals do (`[]string{...}` isn't assignable to
`[]CredentialFormat`): `client/client_test.go` had four such literals, all fixed. Regenerating the two
schema-derived goldens (`OPTIONAL_SCALAR_INVENTORY.md`, `CONFORMANCE.md`) surfaced only the `GoType`
column flipping from `string` to the resolved enum type on the same rows T-24 had already flagged as
enum-like-string candidates — nothing else in either golden moved.

### T-20 · Comment bloat in the generator packages
**Status:** todo · **Policy:** `AGENTS.md` · **Files:** `internal/codegen/`, `internal/schema/`

`AGENTS.md` asks for lean comments and says explicitly that anything longer than two or three lines
belongs in this tracker instead. The generator packages have drifted well past that, and the drift
compounds per family:

| file | lines | comment lines | |
|---|---|---|---|
| `internal/codegen/overrides.go` | 340 | 173 | 50% |
| `internal/codegen/validate_test.go` | 3519 | 1307 | 37% |
| `internal/codegen/manifest.go` | 225 | 71 | 31% |
| `internal/schema/parse.go` | 613 | 144 | 23% |
| `internal/codegen/generate.go` | 1378 | 289 | 20% |

`overrides.go` is the clearest case: half the file is prose, and the largest blocks are experiment
write-ups — which live gvmd calls were made, what each returned, why four fields got no entry — that
duplicate what this document already records at greater length. `validate_test.go`'s 1,307 comment
lines are mostly the same per-family narrative repeated per test.

**This tracker is the home for that narrative; the code is not.** The rule to apply: an override entry's
`Reason` states the conclusion and cites the section here, one line, and the experiment record stays in
this file. Doc comments on exported types and on genuinely non-obvious invariants stay — `GenerateAll`'s
family-local rationale, the Stage A/B boundary, `pointerScalars`' default — those are load-bearing and
short. What goes is the evidence log.

Explicitly *not* in scope: the generated files under `commands/` (12–15% comment, which is fine), and
this document, which is allowed to be long because being long is its job.

### T-21 · `expandShared` leaks its recursion guard, silently dropping a shared `<element>`'s second reference
**Status:** done · **File:** `internal/schema/parse.go`

Found while reviewing `scanner`: `get_scanners`' `<scanner>` element references the shared, top-level
`<element name="certificate_info">` twice — once as `ca_pub_info`, once as `certificate_info` — both local
`<ele>`s spelled as a bare `<type>certificate_info</type>` reference to the same definition.
`ca_pub_info` expands correctly into the full eight-field struct (`ScannerCAPubInfo` in the golden);
`certificate_info` does not, and is left as an unresolved `Type: "certificate_info"` with no children,
generating as a bare `string` instead of a struct. No `SCHEMA-DEFECT` is recorded for the loss.

Root cause is `expandShared`'s own guard bookkeeping, not the DSL:

```go
func (e *env) expandShared(n *Node, path string) {
	def, ok := e.elements[n.Type]
	if !ok || e.expanding[n.Type] {
		return
	}
	e.expanding[n.Type] = true
	e.fill(n, def.Pattern, def.Defs, path)
	delete(e.expanding, n.Type)
	n.Type = ""
}
```

`e.fill(n, ...)` mutates `n.Type` as a side effect — for `certificate_info`'s own pattern (pure `<e>`
references, no leaf type of its own) `fill` sets `n.Type = ""`. The `delete(e.expanding, n.Type)` on the
next line then reads that already-mutated field and deletes `e.expanding[""]` instead of
`e.expanding["certificate_info"]`, so the guard is never cleared. The first reference (`ca_pub_info`)
still expands correctly — it sets the guard, expands, and the leak only matters for whoever comes next —
but every subsequent reference to the same shared element within the same command permanently reads as
"still expanding" and returns early, silently, with no defect. `child`'s own copy of this same guard
pattern (used when a name resolves via the document's top-level `<element>` table rather than local
scope) does not have this bug: it holds the type name in a local variable throughout, rather than
re-reading a struct field `fill` might have changed underneath it.

Confirmed with a standalone repro (two `<ele>`s in one response pattern, both typed to the same shared
`<element>` with a pure-`<e>`-references pattern): the second reference comes back with `Type` still set
and zero children, matching `certificate_info` exactly.

The fix is a one-line capture (`key := n.Type` before the `e.fill` call, used in both the set and the
delete), mirroring `child`'s already-correct guard. See `internal/schema/parse_test.go`'s
`TestSharedElementSecondReferenceIsExpanded` for the regression test (fails on the unpatched parser,
verified via `git stash`).

Applied and regenerated: the blast radius reached six commands beyond `get_scanners` itself.

- **`get_notes`/`get_overrides`** are the only two of these in `manifest.json` and adopted. Fixing T-21
  makes the shared `note`/`override` elements' pre-existing upstream `active`-redefinition defect
  reachable at their newly-resolved second reference too, so `Generate`'s fail-closed defect gate needed
  two new `DefectAllowlist` entries (`duplicate-ele` at
  `.../note/result/overrides/override/active` and `.../override/result/notes/note/active`). Each
  family's SCHEMA-DEFECT count moves 2 → 3 (see T-15's table above), and the generator's type-dedup pass
  unifies the previously-separate `NoteResultOverrides`/`OverrideResultNotes` wrapper types into the
  correct, already-existing `NoteOverrides`/`OverrideNotes` sibling types — a real `commands/` diff,
  adopted as part of this fix.
- **`get_reports`/`create_report`/`get_results`** have no manifest family at the time of this fix, so
  nothing regenerates their Go code — the fix only changes their `CONFORMANCE.md` entries. (`get_results`
  gained a `result` family and was adopted on 2026-08-18 — see its closeout above, which confirms this
  entry's prediction: the nested `result` back-reference collapse is a legitimate recursion cut, not a
  defect, and generation records no `SCHEMA-DEFECT` for it.) `get_reports`/`create_report` each gain
  two more `duplicate-ele` SCHEMA-DEFECT sites, same shape as `get_notes`/`get_overrides`. `get_results`
  is not a simple cleanup: its previously-collapsed `notes`/`overrides` embedded results now resolve into
  their own eight-field subtree, but that subtree's own *nested* `result` back-reference — one layer
  deeper — still collapses to a bare leaf (a legitimate recursion cut: `fill`'s own guard check returns
  silently with no recorded defect when a name is genuinely still expanding, unlike `child`'s explicit
  `DefectRecursion`, and a single per-name guard bit cannot represent a doubly-nested back-reference; this
  is not a second T-21 instance). Net effect: `get_results`' finding profile *grows*
  (CARDINALITY 1 → 2, OMITEMPTY 20 → 33, SCHEMA-DEFECT 2 → 4) rather than closing — real information
  surfacing for the first time, not a regression.
- **`get_scanners`** (adopted after this fix): `certificate_info` now resolves to the real eight-field
  shape in both the schema snapshot and the golden fixture (see the `scanner` section above).
- **`help`** carries a 7th confirmed instance (`response`'s own reference to the shared recursive
  `pattern` grammar), confirmed but inert for codegen: `CONFORMANCE.md`'s recursion-cut count for `help`
  doubles from 6 to 12 sites, but `HelpResponse` is a flat chardata struct never routed through the
  generator or `schema.Comparer`.

### T-23 · Add schema-undeclared count-wrapper chardata totals
**Status:** done · **Files:** `internal/codegen/overrides.go`, `internal/codegen/generate_test.go`,
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`, `commands/chardata_test.go`, `internal/schema/conform_test.go`,
25 adopted `commands/*.go` families

T-18a's closest analogue: every adopted `get_X` family that returns a list has a top-level `<X_count>`
summary element whose schema `<pattern>` declares only `<filtered>`/`<page>` children — no chardata. Live
and upstream wire data disagree: every real response puts a bare leading total in front of those children
(`<role_count>6<filtered>6</filtered><page>6</page></role_count>`), including confirmed `0` totals
(`<alert_count>0<filtered>0</filtered><page>0</page></alert_count>`). Decoding a real response through the
schema-faithful struct permanently dropped that total.

Grepping every `*_fidelity.txt` for `_count/#text` `DROPPED` findings surfaced the identical 25-command
set T-18a already covers for `<sort>`: `get_alerts`, `get_assets`, `get_configs`, `get_credentials`,
`get_filters`, `get_groups`, `get_info`, `get_notes`, `get_overrides`, `get_permissions`,
`get_port_lists`, `get_report_configs`, `get_report_formats`, `get_reports`, `get_results`, `get_roles`,
`get_scanners`, `get_schedules`, `get_tags`, `get_targets`, `get_tasks`, `get_tickets`,
`get_tls_certificates`, `get_users`, `get_vulns`. Of these, 11 already carried an explicit, permanent
`Test<Family>CountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` test pinning the loss as deliberate: `role`, `group`, `tag`,
`schedule`, `alert`, `filter`, `tls_certificate`, `permission`, `ticket`, `credential`, `port_list`. The
other 14 (`get_assets`, `get_configs`, `get_info`, `get_notes`, `get_overrides`, `get_report_configs`,
`get_report_formats`, `get_reports`, `get_results`, `get_scanners`, `get_targets`, `get_tasks`,
`get_users`, `get_vulns`) had the same evidenced gap but no test at all — invisible, since neither side
declared chardata and `schema.Comparer` had nothing to flag. `get_agents` shares the same wire shape but
stays excluded: it's a hand-written legacy struct that predates the schema-driven family system and was
never brought under `manifest.json`, so there is no adopted generated code to add a field to. Explicitly
out of scope and untouched: `get_configs`' per-family `known_nvt_count`/`max_nvt_count` and the nested
`<nvt_count>N<growing>G</growing></nvt_count>` shape under `<config>` (a different element, one level
deeper than the top-level `config_count` wrapper this task targets), and the per-item nested count types
`ConfigFamilyCount`/`ConfigNVTCount`, `PortListPortCount`, `TaskReportCount`/`TaskResultCount`/
`TaskComplianceCount`.

Unlike T-18a, this is pure addition, not relocation: the schema declares no chardata at all on
`<X_count>`, so there is nothing to suppress on the source side. No generator code changed —
`overrides.go`'s `FieldOverride{Chardata: true, GoType: ...}` already does exactly this (it's the existing
mechanism behind `get_tls_certificates:/tls_certificate/certificate`), and
`internal/schema.checkChardata`'s `!s.Chardata && g.Chardata` branch already suppresses the resulting
EXTRA finding via `g.ChardataOverrideReason`. Added 25 `FieldOverrides` entries (`"get_roles:/role_count"`
carries the fully evidenced reason citing the schema pattern, the command's own baked example, and the
zero-total fixture; the other 24 share `t23CountTotalReason`), then removed the 11 now-factually-obsolete
`Test<Family>CountBareTotalIsAnAcceptedLoss` tests and bumped every affected family's `wantAfter`/`want`
suppressed-`CatExtra` counts in both `TestValidate<Family>GeneratedMatchesOrImproves` and the independent
`TestValidate<Family>BeforeMatchesKnownConformance` functions (the latter assert against the *committed*
`commands/*.go` baseline, which also picked up the new field once regenerated).

Confirmed with a standalone `encoding/xml` experiment that an int chardata field with no `omitempty`
round-trips `0` correctly, while adding `omitempty` to a chardata field drops the whole element's text on
marshal regardless of value — this is why every new field is declared as plain `xml:",chardata"` with no
`omitempty`, and why `commands/chardata_test.go` gained `TestChardataRoundTripCountZero`/
`TestChardataRoundTripCountNonZero` as the concrete regression test for that trap.
`internal/codegen/generate_test.go` gained a generic (non-relocation) count-wrapper generation test and a
structural-dedup cross-use mismatch test, mirroring `TestChardataRelocationCrossUseMismatch`'s pattern for
the plain-override path. `internal/schema/conform_test.go` gained
`TestChardataWithOverrideReasonDoesNotHideSiblingMissing`, proving the new suppression doesn't mask an
unrelated, genuinely missing sibling field on the same node.

Regeneration gave every one of the 25 families the desired shape: each top-level `*Count` struct gained
exactly one new leading `Value int` field, nothing else. Fidelity fixtures, `FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`, and
`internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md` were regenerated for all 25: every `DROPPED
/..._response/*_count/#text` finding closes; `get_configs`' excluded nested-count `DROPPED` lines remain
untouched.

### T-23b · Pointer-ify count-wrapper fields so schema-required-but-sometimes-absent wrappers round-trip absence correctly
**Status:** done · **Files:** `internal/codegen/generate.go`, `internal/codegen/overrides.go`,
`internal/codegen/generate_test.go`, `internal/codegen/overrides_test.go`,
`internal/codegen/validate_test.go`, `commands/chardata_test.go`, 23 adopted `commands/*.go` families,
`commands/pagination/{assets,portlists,targets,tasks,tickets}.go`

Review flagged a defect T-23 didn't fix: every `*Count` wrapper field (e.g. `FilterCount FilterCount`)
was a plain struct, not a pointer, even though the schema marks the parent element required and the
wrapper's own zero value (`Value`/`Filtered`/`Page` all `0`) is indistinguishable from "genuinely absent."
`get_filters`'s single-filter-lookup `<example>` (filter_id lookup) omits `<filter_count>` entirely, and
`internal/gmptest/testdata/upstream/get_filters/002_response.xml` confirms it live — decoding that
response and re-encoding it fabricated `<filter_count>0<filtered>0</filtered><page>0</page></filter_count>`
where the wire sent nothing. The same class of defect T-18a's `<sort>`/`<filters>` siblings still carry,
but this pass is scoped to `*_count` only, per the decision recorded when the fix was chosen.

Fixing this required relaxing `internal/codegen/generate.go`'s `fieldOf`, whose `CardRequired` branch
previously let `RequiredOverrides` add `,omitempty` only to plain string leaves (erroring on anything
else). It now also accepts structured fields (`len(n.Children) > 0`): the field becomes a pointer
(`*FilterCount`) with `,omitempty` instead of a bare value type, while non-string scalars are still
rejected as before (no live evidence yet justifies that case). Added 23 new `RequiredOverrides` entries —
`get_filters.filter_count` carries the full evidence chain (schema `<pattern>`, the bundled example, the
live fixture); the other 22 share `t23CountWrapperReason`. `get_reports`/`get_results` are excluded on
purpose: their schema already wraps `report_count`/`result_count` in an `<o>`, so `fieldOf`'s existing
`CardOptional` auto-pointer logic already makes them pointers.

Regenerating surfaced a real, unrelated bug the pointer conversion made visible: several
`commands/pagination/*.go` iterator constructors (`assets.go`, `portlists.go`, `targets.go`, `tasks.go`,
`tickets.go`) built their page-size-from-response closures as a bare `resp.XCount.Filtered` with no nil
check — harmless while `XCount` was a value type, a nil-pointer panic once it wasn't.
`commands/pagination/results.go`'s `ResultCount` was already pointer-typed for the unrelated `<o>` reason
above and already nil-checked; the other five now match that pattern. Every hand-written test file
constructing a `*Count` as a struct literal (`commands/pagination/iterator_mocks_test.go`,
`commands/pagination/pagination_test.go`, `client/client_test.go` — 14 call sites across the three) needed
`&` added to keep building.

Added `TestRequiredOverridePointerizesStructuredField` (positive coverage for the newly-allowed path) and
narrowed `TestRequiredOverrideRejectsUnsupportedTargets` to only the still-rejected non-string-scalar case.
Added `commands/chardata_test.go`'s `TestChardataRoundTripCountAbsent` — the review's explicit regression
request: a response omitting `<role_count>` entirely must stay `nil` through decode and marshal, not
fabricate the element back.

Every pointer+omitempty count-wrapper field trips `internal/schema/conform.go`'s `checkOmitEmpty` once
(response-side omitempty is always auto-suppressed as inert — decoding, not encoding, is what matters for
a response). Bumped `schema.CatOmitEmpty: 1` into the suppressed-count maps of both
`TestValidate<Family>BeforeMatchesKnownConformance` and `TestValidate<Family>GeneratedMatchesOrImproves`
for all 21 affected family test pairs (`get_info`/`get_vulns` have no such test pair to bump).

Regenerated fidelity fixtures, `FIDELITY-SUMMARY.txt`, and `internal/schema/testdata/CONFORMANCE.md`:
every fabricated `ADDED /..._response/*_count/*` finding for a genuinely-absent wrapper closes (e.g.
`get_filters/upstream/002`, `get_info/live/003`, `get_tasks/live/005`).

### T-24 · Optional scalar presence audit
**Status:** done · **Files:** `internal/schema/gomodel.go`, `internal/schema/conform.go`,
`internal/schema/inventory.go`, `internal/schema/inventory_test.go`,
`internal/schema/testdata/OPTIONAL_SCALAR_INVENTORY.md`, `internal/codegen/generate.go`,
`internal/codegen/overrides_test.go`, `internal/codegen/generate_test.go`

Requested as an audit of every optional schema scalar (`int`, `decimal`, `GMPBool`/`GMPAtoiBool`,
enum-like string) to determine whether the API needs to distinguish "omitted" from
"present with zero/false/empty-string" — framed as a task that could change exported field types across
many generated families, and that should precede T-19 (`<alts>` enumerations are parsed and dropped).

The audit found the numeric/boolean half of this question already fully and correctly settled:
`internal/codegen/generate.go`'s `pointerScalars` map makes every optional `int`/`float64`/`GMPBool`/
`GMPAtoiBool`/`GMPTime` field a pointer with `omitempty`, unconditionally, since each of those Go types'
zero value is itself a legal, meaningful wire value. Grepping all of `commands/*.go` for a non-pointer
optional occurrence of any of these types found zero exceptions in the adopted, generated corpus. The one
confirmed violation, `commands/get_agents.go`'s `Writable`/`InUse` (plain `bool`, not `GMPBool`) and
`Authorized`/`MinInterval`/`HeartbeatInterval` (`int` with `omitempty`, no pointer), is a hand-written
legacy struct outside `manifest.json` — the `agent` family's adoption stays blocked because `ENABLE_AGENTS`
isn't compiled into the pinned `gvmd:stable` image. Left untouched: fixing it blind, with no schema or live
evidence and no way to test against a running `agent` endpoint, would be a worse outcome than documenting
the gap and leaving it with the same deferred status as the rest of that family.

With no live defect to fix and no exported field type to change, T-24 was rescoped as audit +
test-infrastructure hardening, closing four real gaps the research surfaced rather than doing unnecessary
or unverifiable rework:

1. No inventory or regression test pinned "every optional scalar leaf generates as declared" — a future
   generator change could silently regress `pointerScalars` and nothing would fail. Added
   `internal/schema/conform.go`'s `Comparer.OnOptionalScalar` callback, invoked from `compareNode` at the
   existing scalar-leaf match point (nil callback is a no-op — zero behavior change for `buildReport` and
   `internal/codegen`'s validate tests), and a new `internal/schema/inventory.go`/`inventory_test.go` pair
   that walks the full adopted corpus (sourced from `internal/codegen`'s committed, MIT-licensed
   `testdata/schema` snapshots, not the gitignored AGPL `vendor/XML` tree, so it runs unconditionally in
   CI) and renders a deterministic, `-update`-gated golden,
   `internal/schema/testdata/OPTIONAL_SCALAR_INVENTORY.md` (697 rows). `inventory_test.go` is an external
   test package (`schema_test`): an internal `package schema` test file importing `internal/codegen` would
   be a genuine import cycle, since `codegen` itself imports `schema`'s production code. Every optional
   string leaf with non-empty `Alts` shows up in the golden with `Pointer: false` — the concrete
   enum-like-string field list T-19 can consume, without this task deciding anything about enum type
   design. Spot-checking the golden's only `pointer=false` rows among schema-numeric-typed fields found
   exactly two (`get_info_response/info/@id`, `get_resource_names_response/resource/@id`), both already
   tracked as pre-existing `TYPE`-category findings in `CONFORMANCE.md` (the schema mislabels a UUID-shaped
   `@id` as `integer`, but the Go field is genuinely `string`) — unrelated to pointer policy.
2. `internal/schema/gomodel.go`'s `ZeroIsMeaningful()` matched only the built-in Go type names (`bool`,
   `int`, `float64`, ...), not the named types `GMPBool`/`GMPAtoiBool` — the conformance-side counterpart
   to `pointerScalars` wasn't watching the same set of types the generator-side rule already protects. A
   non-pointer optional `GMPBool` would not have tripped `checkCardinality`'s `CatCardinality` finding
   today; it's only caught because `pointerScalars` never lets one exist. Widened the type-name switch to
   include both — and review caught that the first version of this fix was itself ineffective: `GoType`
   comes from `f.Type.String()`, which for a real command field is package-qualified
   (`commands.GMPBool`, `*commands.GMPBool`), not the bare `GMPBool` the switch checked for, so a
   non-pointer `commands.GMPBool` still fell through to `false`. Fixed generically by stripping both the
   pointer prefix and any package qualifier before the switch, and added
   `TestZeroIsMeaningfulRecognizesQualifiedGMPBool`/`...GMPAtoiBool` plus their pointer counterparts in
   `internal/schema/gomodel_test.go`, built from the real `commands.GMPBool`/`commands.GMPAtoiBool` types
   via reflection rather than a hand-constructed `GoNode{GoType: "GMPBool"}`, so this class of regression
   is now actually caught.
3. `PointerOverrides` (`internal/codegen/overrides.go`) had no well-formedness or staleness test, unlike
   every sibling table (`RequiredOverrides`, `FieldOverrides`, `NameOverrides`, `FieldTypeOverrides`).
   Mirrored `RequiredOverrides`' pattern exactly: `internal/codegen/generate.go` gained
   `validatePointerOverrides` (key shape, non-empty `Reason`) and `checkPointerOverridesConsumed` (a `pf`
   entry whose command is in scope but never matched a generated optional field is stale), both wired into
   `validateOverrideTables`/`checkTablesConsumed`, plus a `pfConsumed` tracking map set in `fieldOf`'s
   existing `forced` lookup. No changes to the 21 existing table entries — running the full committed
   corpus through `checkPointerOverridesConsumed` for the first time confirmed every one is still live.
4. No test pinned the plain `CardOptional` `int`/`decimal`(`float64`)/`boolean`(`GMPBool`)/
   `boolean_atoi`(`GMPAtoiBool`) → pointer decision directly at the `fieldOf` level, the way
   `TestFieldOfGMPTimePointerPolicy` already did for `GMPTime` — previously only exercised indirectly
   through full-family golden tests. Added `TestFieldOfScalarPointerPolicy` alongside it.

No exported field types changed anywhere in this task, and `commands/get_agents.go` is untouched.
(`commands/get_agents.go` was later removed entirely by T-32; this section's finding is preserved as
the historical record of the bug, not as a description of current code.)

### T-25 · Explicit-empty audit: closing 5 evidence gaps
**Status:** done · **Files:** `internal/codegen/overrides.go`, `docs/review-2026-08.md`

The typed-request explicit-empty semantics audit (T-24 baseline: classify every optional request-side string/enum field into omit/explicit-empty proven equivalent, explicit-empty meaningful, explicit-empty rejected/unsupported, or unknown/evidence-blocked) had 5 fields/families remaining at the unknown/evidence-blocked category. Live probes against the local gvmd development stack produced conclusive evidence for all 5, closing the remaining gaps.

**Findings:**

1. **`modify_credential.auth_algorithm`**: Omission and explicit-empty both leave the stored value unchanged — ordinary leave-unchanged behavior with no distinction between the two wire forms. Category: proven equivalent, no hazard to document.

2. **`modify_user.sources.source`**: Omission leaves the stored source unchanged; explicit-empty (`<sources><source></source></sources>`) reproducibly **segfaults gvmd** (crash backtrace inside glib's `g_markup_parse_context_parse`). This is the second confirmed gvmd crash bug, alongside the already-documented `modify_permission` Name/Comment crash. The typed API already cannot construct this payload: `UserSources.Source` is `UserSource` (a T-19 named string type) with `xml:"source,omitempty"`, so an empty `UserSource("")` is dropped by `omitempty`, not encoded as `<source></source>`. No code change needed — only a `CommandWarning` entry added so the hazard is on record for anyone tempted to hand-build this XML (e.g. via `RawXML`) or to change `Sources`' pointer/omitempty policy later.

3. **`modify_note.Hosts`/`.Port`**: Both fields are cleared to empty on omission (full-replace mechanism, not leave-unchanged), and explicit-empty produces the identical cleared result. Category: proven equivalent (omit and empty have same effect), but the full-replace mechanism is a genuine hazard worth a `CommandWarning` — same shape as `modify_role`/`modify_group`/`modify_port_list`/`modify_asset`'s existing entries for the same "omit clears, not preserves" pattern. A caller modifying one field alone will silently blank the other.

4. **`modify_override.Hosts`/`.Port`/`.Threat`**: All three fields clear to empty on omission (full-replace); explicit-empty is identical. **`modify_override.NewThreat`** behaves oppositely: omission and explicit-empty both leave it unchanged. Both halves are individually in the "proven equivalent" category, but the **asymmetry within one command** is the hazard worth documenting — a caller who assumes uniform full-replace across `modify_override`'s fields would wrongly expect `NewThreat` to clear too. One `CommandWarning` entry documents this asymmetry explicitly.

5. **`modify_report_format.Name`/`.Summary`**: All 5 report formats on the development stack (all predefined, all reporting `writable=true`) reject *any* modification with `Permission denied`, before omit-vs-explicit-empty semantics can even be tested. Category: still evidence-blocked — this environment has no non-predefined report format to test against. Documented as blocked with the same precedent as the `agent`-family/`ENABLE_AGENTS` and `Help.Type` entries, not as a `CommandWarning` (no confirmed wire hazard to warn callers about, just an open question).

**Key insight: full-replace vs. leave-unchanged asymmetry.** The audit surfaced a reusable finding for future families: some `modify_*` commands exhibit full-replace behavior (omitting a field clears it rather than leaving it unchanged), and that behavior can be *inconsistent within one command* (`modify_override.NewThreat` leaves unchanged while `Hosts`/`Port`/`Threat` clear). This is distinct from the previously documented DISTINCT pattern (omit=unchanged, empty=clear, requires pointer) — here, omit and empty are equivalent (both clear or both leave unchanged), so no pointer is needed, but the caller still faces a hazard: sending only the field they want to change may silently blank others.

**Implementation:** Three new `CommandWarning` entries added to `internal/codegen/overrides.go`: `modify_user` (segfault hazard), `modify_note` (full-replace hazard), and `modify_override` (asymmetric full-replace hazard). No exported field types or `PointerOverrides` entries changed: every field above already round-trips correctly through the existing non-pointer-with-omitempty policy (proven equivalent) or already fails closed at the type level (`UserSource`'s `omitempty` prevents the segfault payload). Regenerated `commands/modify_user.go`, `commands/modify_note.go`, and `commands/modify_override.go` to emit the new doc-comment warnings.

### T-26 · Classify and fix raw-plus-typed duplication contracts for get_info/get_results
**Status:** done · **Files:** `internal/codegen/generate.go`, `internal/codegen/overrides.go`,
`internal/codegen/schema_patches.go`, `internal/codegen/overrides_test.go`,
`internal/codegen/schema_patches_test.go`, `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`,
`internal/schema/parse.go`, `commands/get_info.go`, `commands/get_info_test.go`,
`commands/get_results.go`, `commands/get_results_test.go`, `commands/get_notes.go`,
`commands/get_overrides.go`, `commands/report_shared.go`, `commands/get_filters.go` (mechanical,
see below)

Investigated the DUPLICATED findings in the fidelity corpus
(`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/{get_info,get_results}/001_fidelity.txt`) to classify the underlying
wire-model defects before API stabilization. Both turned out to be real defects, not accepted
remarshal contracts — fixed rather than merely classified.

**get_results (and family): nvt/refs presence bug, not a duplication contract.** `encoding/xml`
decodes each `<nvt>` element independently, so one wire `<refs>` populating all 5 `ResultNVT` instances
(the review's originally-considered hypothesis) is mechanically impossible. Root cause confirmed by a
decode-then-remarshal probe: `Refs` was a non-pointer required field on the shared NVT struct, so
remarshal fabricated an empty `<refs></refs>` on every sibling NVT that never had one —
`internal/gmptest/testdata/live/get_results/001_response.xml` has 5 `<nvt>` elements, only 1 carries
`<refs>`, and remarshaling produced 4 fabricated empties. Same fabrication class T-23b's count-wrapper
fix already closed, just reached through a nested, structurally-deduplicated shape (get_results'
`ResultNVT`, get_notes'/get_overrides' `NoteResultNVT`/`OverrideResultNVT`, and the `report` family's
`ReportNVT`, shared by `create_report`/`get_reports`) rather than a command's own top-level field, so
`RequiredOverrides` (keyed `"<command>.<field>"`, top-level only) couldn't reach it.

Fixed by extending `FieldTypeOverrides` (`internal/codegen/overrides.go`) with a new `Pointer bool`:
when set, `requiredFieldType` (`internal/codegen/generate.go`) makes the retyped field a pointer with
`,omitempty`, mirroring `RequiredOverrides`' existing structured-field pointer exception but reachable
from a nested shape's `"<command>:/<path>"` key. Added five entries sharing `t26NVTRefsReason`:
`get_results:/result/nvt`, `get_reports:/report/report/results/result/nvt`,
`create_report:/report/report/results/result/nvt` (all three already had a `cvss_base` entry at this key
from the T-9 report review, so `refs` was added alongside it), plus two new keys `get_notes:/note/result/nvt`
and `get_overrides:/override/result/nvt`. The shallow `NoteNVT`/`OverrideNVT`/`ResultNoteNVT` types
(the ones with only OID/Name/Type, reused at `note.nvt`/`override.nvt` rather than `note.result.nvt`/
`override.result.nvt`) have no `refs` field at all and needed no change.
`Refs` changed from a value type to `*XRefs` with `,omitempty` in `commands/get_results.go`,
`commands/get_notes.go`, `commands/get_overrides.go`, and `commands/report_shared.go` — the only
generated-API breaking change in this task, and an uncontroversial one (nothing dereferenced `.Refs`
directly; grepped the whole repo to confirm).

Regenerated fidelity fixtures and `CONFORMANCE.md`: all `nvt/refs 1 -> N` DUPLICATED lines close across
all four `get_results` live fixtures, and the two upstream `get_notes`/`get_overrides` fixtures' `ADDED
nvt/refs` findings close too (same fabrication, visible as ADDED rather than DUPLICATED there because
those fixtures' single `<nvt>` never had `<refs>` on the wire at all). Every new `OMITEMPTY` finding this
produces is suppressed under the pre-existing categories (`inert: responses are decoded...` for the four
response-side families, `request-side over-constraint` for `create_report`'s request-side use) —
actionable-finding count is unchanged. Bumped `get_notes`/`get_overrides`'s `TestValidate*` suppressed
`CatOmitEmpty` from 1 to 2 in `internal/codegen/validate_test.go`, the same lockstep bump T-23b's own
closeout made for its 21 affected families. Added `TestGetResultsNVTRefsAbsentStaysAbsent`/
`...PresentRoundTrips`/`...EmptyPresentSurvives` to `commands/get_results_test.go`.

**get_info: wrapper/item name collision — genuine schema gap, fixed via `SchemaPatches`.** Wire XML
contains 11 data items (`<info id="CVE-xxx">...</info>`) plus 1 pagination wrapper
(`<info start="1" max="1000"/>`) sharing the same element name. Checked what
`internal/codegen/testdata/schema/get_info.json` (and `vendor/XML/commands/command_get_info.xml`
directly) declares: exactly one `<info>` shape, the data item — the wrapper is not declared anywhere,
not even as the served schema's `<pattern>`/`<example>` disagreement class T-9's other findings are.
The vendor XML's own worked example (`command_get_info.xml`, "Get a info about cpe:/a:gnu:gzip:1.3.3")
independently confirms gvmd really does send `<info max="-1" start="1"/>` after the data items, so this
is gvmd's schema failing to declare a real wire shape, not a one-off capture quirk — `get_filters`
already has the identical two-same-name-elements shape (`<filters>` id/term/keywords/name vs.
`<filters>` start/max), just correctly declared via a second `<ele><id>filters2</id>...</ele>`
definition that `command_get_info.xml` never added for its own wrapper.

Modelled as a schema-patch, not a codec-only change: extended `SchemaPatch`
(`internal/codegen/schema_patches.go`) with `AllowCollision bool`, letting a patch add a same-name,
same-`Kind` sibling of an already-declared child instead of failing closed on the pre-existing
"schema has caught up" guard — the DSL's own `<id>` escape hatch, reproduced through a patch for a
command whose served schema never added the second definition itself. Also extended
`descendSchemaPath` to let a `SchemaPatches` key target a command's own request/response root directly
(`"get_info:/get_info_response"`), which no existing key needed since every prior patch targeted a
child reached by descending from the root. Added one `SchemaPatches` entry: a second, optional `<info>`
node with required `@start`/`@max` integer attributes, `AllowCollision: true`, inserted immediately after
the existing `<info>` definition (inserting before it, tried first, made the *wrapper* claim the
pre-existing `NVTInfo` name and renamed the real data item to `GetInfoInfo` — backwards from the
minimal-diff intent; insertion order controls which occurrence `pick`'s naming ladder sees first).

The existing duplicate-wire collision codec (`collisionDiscriminators`/`emitCollisionMethods`) already
dispatches by disjoint attribute signature (`@id` vs `@start`/`@max`, exactly as `get_filters`' two
`<filters>` do) and fails closed on an `<info>` matching neither or both signatures — no new dispatch
logic needed. One real bug surfaced and fixed in that shared codec while adopting it here:
`emitCollisionUnmarshal`'s "unexpected extra element" duplicate guard assumed every colliding member is
singular, true for every prior use (`get_filters`' two `<filters>` are each exactly-one) but wrong for
`get_info`'s data-item variant, which is legitimately repeated (`Info []NVTInfo`) alongside its singular
wrapper sibling (`Info2 *GetInfoInfo`). Without the fix, a second `<info id="...">` tripped the same
"seen" guard as a second wrapper, and every live fixture with more than one data item failed decode
outright (`unexpected extra <info> element`) — caught immediately by the regenerated fidelity tests, not
shipped. Fixed by skipping the duplicate guard for a slice-typed member; `decodeInto`/`encodeStmt`
already handled the append-vs-replace distinction correctly per field, so only the guard was wrong.
`get_filters`' own generated collision code changes shape slightly (the guard moves inside the per-member
`switch` instead of before it) but is behaviorally identical — neither of its two members is a slice.

Result: `GetInfoResponse.Info []NVTInfo` keeps its existing name and shape (the 11 real data items,
unchanged), and a new `Info2 *GetInfoInfo` (`GetInfoInfo{Start, Max int}`) carries the wrapper — additive,
not breaking; nothing in the repo dereferenced `NVTInfo`/`GetInfoResponse.Info` in a way this touches.
Regenerated fidelity fixtures: both live 001/002 fixtures' `@start`/`@max` DROPPED lines and all 11
DUPLICATED lines (`comment`, `creation_time`, `name`, `owner`, `permissions`, `update_time`, etc. — every
field the fabricated 12th "item" spuriously carried) close completely. Added
`TestGetInfoResponseItemsOnlyRoundTrips`/`...WrapperOnlyRoundTrips`/`...MixedRoundTrips`/
`...AmbiguousInfoFailsClosed`/`...ExtraWrapperFailsClosed` to a new `commands/get_info_test.go`.

**Conformance regression (review finding, fixed): `Info2` surfaced as an unsuppressed actionable `EXTRA`,
not the accepted `solution`-patch shape.** First submission compared `Info2`'s finding to
`get_results:/result/nvt/solution` (also `SchemaPatch`-added) and reasoned the actionable-count bump from
73→74 was the same already-accepted cost that patch pays. Review caught that this was wrong on inspection:
`solution`'s own `EXTRA` finding is *also* unsuppressed in the committed report — a pre-existing, unnoticed
gap, not a precedent to lean on — so citing it as justification just meant two unexplained findings
instead of one. Root cause: `SchemaPatch.Reason` never reached the generated field's `gmpoverride` tag at
all (only `FieldOverride`/`CardinalityOverride`/chardata-relocation reasons did), and separately,
`writeField` (`internal/codegen/generate.go`) only ever emitted one of `gmpwire`/`gmpoverride` on a field —
exclusive, not additive — so even a field carrying both a collision identity and a suppression reason
(`Info2`'s exact shape) could never surface the second one.

Fixed both gaps. Added `schema.Node.PatchReason` (`internal/schema/parse.go`, `json:",omitempty"` so every
committed Stage-A snapshot stays byte-identical — the field is only ever set in-memory by
`ApplySchemaPatches`, never present in a loaded, unpatched tree), threaded from `SchemaPatch.Reason` via
`schemaPatchNode` (`internal/codegen/schema_patches.go`). `fieldOf` (`internal/codegen/generate.go`) now
folds `n.PatchReason` into the field's `overrideReason` (erroring if one already exists from another
source — ambiguous which reason a single `gmpoverride` tag should carry). `writeField` gained a case for
`wireTag != "" && overrideReason != ""` that emits both tags on one field; `internal/schema/gomodel.go`'s
`leafNode` already reads `gmpoverride` unconditionally regardless of which path (`xml` or `gmpwire`)
supplied the field's identity, so no reflection-side change was needed. The fix is generic, not
`get_info`-specific: it also closed the pre-existing `solution` gap for free, since that patch already had
a `Reason` sitting unused. Regenerated: `Info2`'s `EXTRA` finding and `solution`'s now both show
`[suppressed: ...]` with their own evidence; actionable count moves 73→**72** (net improvement, not merely
a wash — `Info2`'s +1 and `solution`'s -1 combine with T-26's other bookkeeping). Added
`TestFieldTypeOverridePointerizesStructuredField`/`...RejectsPointerOnScalar` (mirroring
`TestRequiredOverride...`'s pair for the new `FieldTypeOverride.Pointer` path) and
`TestApplySchemaPatchesCollisionSiblingIsSuppressed` (a synthetic `AllowCollision` patch, asserting the
generated field carries both `gmpwire` and `gmpoverride`) to `internal/codegen/overrides_test.go` and
`schema_patches_test.go` respectively; also added an assertion to the existing
`TestApplySchemaPatchesSplicesIntoGenerate` that `solution`'s own patch now produces a `gmpoverride` tag.

Also fixed, per review: `FieldTypeOverride.Pointer`'s doc comment always said "only meaningful when
GoType names a struct," but nothing enforced it — `requiredFieldType` now rejects `Pointer: true` on a
scalar leaf (`len(n.Children) == 0`) the same way `RequiredOverrides` already rejects a non-string scalar
target, with a matching regression test.

Also trimmed, per review nit: the `get_info` `SchemaPatch`'s inline `Reason` (and its two attribute
children's copies of it) ran ~1,200 characters, generating a proportionally bloated `gmpoverride` tag on
`Info2`. Replaced all three with `t26InfoWrapperReason`, a single short shared constant pointing at this
doc section — the same `t18aSortFieldReason`/`t23CountWrapperReason`/`t26NVTRefsReason` pattern already
used elsewhere in this file, rather than the `get_vulns.Type`-style full-detail-inline precedent.

**Cross-family sweep (both defects):** grepped every family's committed schema snapshot for any
already-declared same-name-sibling collision (found only `get_filters`' `filters`/`filters2` and
`get_aggregates`' attribute/element `data_column`, both pre-existing and already handled) and for every
`nvt/refs` occurrence (`get_notes`, `get_overrides`, `get_reports`, `create_report`, `get_results`, plus
`get_nvts`/`get_info`'s own unrelated, already-pointer-typed `refs` shapes) — all five affected keys are
listed above; nothing else in the corpus shares either defect. Checked `get_resource_names` specifically,
per the review's suggestion: no fixture (live or upstream) ever sends a second `<resource>` shape lacking
`@id`, so there is no live evidence of the get_info defect recurring there.

### T-27 · Committed public API snapshot with CI enforcement
**Status:** done · **Files:** `internal/apisnapshot/apisnapshot_test.go`,
`internal/apisnapshot/testdata/PUBLIC_API.txt`

T-19/T-24/T-26 each deliberately reshaped part of the exported surface of `commands/*.go` — named enum
types, pointer-presence fixes, `Info2`'s collision field — every one reviewed by hand as it happened. But
nothing stopped a *future* regeneration, override-table edit, or hand-written change in `client/`/
`connections/` from silently altering the public API as a side effect: `internal/schema/testdata/
OPTIONAL_SCALAR_INVENTORY.md` and `internal/codegen/testdata/ENUM_INVENTORY.md` already turn one
schema-derived slice of the surface into a reviewable diff, but nothing covered the whole thing.

**Coverage.** Exactly the module's publicly importable packages: the root `github.com/brennoo/go-gmp`
package (`gmp`), `client`, `commands`, `commands/filtering`, `commands/pagination`, `connections` — a
fixed, ordered list in `apisnapshot_test.go`'s `targetPackages`, not discovered by walking the tree, so
`internal/...` and `examples/...` can never join it by accident. Every exported top-level declaration:
types (full field lists including unexported fields and struct tags — `xml`/`gmpwire`/`gmpoverride` tags
are wire-behavior API here, not documentation, e.g. `ResultNVT.Refs`'s and `GetInfoResponse.Info2`'s
tags from T-26 are captured verbatim), func/method signatures (body stripped), consts (whole `const`
blocks, unsplit, so `iota` position is preserved — e.g. `commands.ErrorType`'s block), and vars (e.g.
`connections.ErrConnectionClosed` et al.).

**Deliberately excluded:** `internal/...`, `examples/...` (neither importable by a consumer), every
`_test.go` file, and doc comments — comment *text* churns without any API meaning, so it is never
captured (no `go/parser.ParseComments`). Struct tags are the one exception to "doc comments don't
count": they are wire behavior, not prose, and are kept. One deliberate near-exclusion, called out in the
golden's own header so it doesn't read as a bug: `client`'s section is a single line
(`func New(conn gmp.Connection) gmp.Client`) — every method on its unexported `*client` implementation
type is only reachable through the `gmp.Client` interface it returns, and that interface's full method
set is already captured in the root package's `Client` section; a method added to `*client` without a
matching `Client` entry is a compile error already, not something this golden needs to also catch.

**Implementation.** Stdlib only (`go/ast`, `go/parser`, `go/printer`, `go/token`) — no `go/types`/
`golang.org/x/tools/go/packages`, since every "must produce a diff" requirement (removed/added/retyped
identifier, changed method signature, changed struct tag, changed const value) is visible at the syntax
level without resolving types across package boundaries, and adding a new module dependency for this
was out of scope. For each target package: list non-test `.go` files sorted by name, parse each with
`parser.ParseFile(fset, path, nil, 0)`, walk top-level `Decls`, keep exported ones — a multi-spec `type
(...)` block splits into one entry per exported `*ast.TypeSpec` (so an unrelated sibling type never
masks a change to the one next to it), while a `const`/`var` block renders whole (splitting would
misrepresent `iota` position). A method's receiver base type is resolved by unwrapping `*ast.StarExpr`
first, then `*ast.IndexExpr`/`*ast.IndexListExpr` for a generic receiver (`*Iterator[T]` parses as
`StarExpr{X: IndexExpr{X: Ident}}`, star outside the index — get this backwards and every method on a
generic type silently vanishes from the snapshot). Rendered via `printer.Fprint` with the same
`Config{Mode: UseSpaces | TabIndent, Tabwidth: 8}` `go/format.Node` uses.

One real determinism gap closed during implementation, not just theorized: dropping `ParseComments`
hides comment *text*, but not a comment line's effect on `go/printer`'s blank-line preservation —
`printer.linebreak` measures gaps via `fset` line distance, which still counts a comment line even though
its content was never parsed. `connections/errors.go`'s three exported vars each carry a preceding doc
comment; without a fix, adding or deleting one of those comments would shift the rendered blank-line
count and produce a false-positive diff in a golden that explicitly excludes comments. Fixed by
post-processing each rendered entry: collapse any run of 2+ blank lines to exactly 1, trim trailing
whitespace per line. Verified directly in the generated golden — `connections`' three `Err*` vars render
with zero stray blank lines despite each having its own doc comment in source.

**Golden format:** `internal/apisnapshot/testdata/PUBLIC_API.txt`, one `## <import path>` section per
package in the fixed dependency-layer order (root → client → commands → filtering → pagination →
connections, not alphabetical), entries sorted by name within a section (a method's sort key is
`"<ReceiverType>.<MethodName>"`, so it lands immediately after its own type's declaration). Header states
the regenerate command, what's excluded, and the policy line verbatim: **any diff to this file is a
reviewed API decision — regenerating it is never a mechanical fix-the-build step.** Current size: 1816
exported declarations across the six packages, ~9,400 lines.

**Tests:** `TestPublicAPISnapshot` (build vs. committed golden, `-update`-gated exactly like the enum/
optional-scalar inventories — `var update = flag.Bool(...)` declared locally in this package rather than
aliased from `gmptest.Update`, mirroring `internal/codegen/snapshot_test.go`'s own stated reason: pulling
in `internal/gmptest` for one bool would drag the whole fidelity corpus package into this test binary for
nothing). Failure message mirrors `TestEnumInventory`'s wording (`"... changed. Re-run with -update and
review the diff.\ncommitted %d bytes, now %d bytes\n%s"`, `firstDiff` copied locally since it's an
8-line unexported helper in `internal/codegen/generate_test.go`, not worth exporting cross-package for
one caller) rather than the plainer `t.Errorf` the optional-scalar inventory uses — the bigger golden
benefits more from the `firstDiff` line-pointer. `TestPublicAPISnapshotIsDeterministic` builds twice from
the same on-disk source and compares, mirroring `TestEnumInventoryIsDeterministic`/
`TestOptionalScalarInventoryIsDeterministic`.

**CI:** no workflow changes needed. `.github/workflows/go.yml`'s `go test -v -race
-coverprofile=coverage.out ./...` step already has no build tags, runs unconditionally on both Go-version
matrix legs, and — since `internal/apisnapshot` reads only committed `.go` source under the six target
dirs, never `vendor/XML` or anything gitignored — needs nothing this step doesn't already have.

**Guard proven working:** a throwaway exported rename in `commands/get_results.go` was made, confirmed
`TestPublicAPISnapshot` failed with the expected message and a useful `firstDiff` pointer at the changed
line, then reverted; the suite returned to green.

### T-28 · Live integration run: prove the committed integration suite against the pinned gvmd stack
**Status:** done · **Files:** `internal/gmptest/explicit_empty_test.go`

First live execution of the full `integration`-tagged suite since T-25/T-26 landed. Goal: exercise every
integration test — most importantly the T-25 explicit-empty assertions, converted from log-only probes to
real `t.Errorf` checks for the first time in that work — against a real, pinned gvmd, not just `go vet`
the build tag compiles.

**Environment.** `hack/gvmd-down.sh` then `hack/gvmd-up.sh` (no `--purge`, so the existing feed sync
survived — a stale `gvmd` container from a prior session was running but reported `unhealthy`, socket
missing; a clean down/up cycle was the more trustworthy path than debugging that container in place) plus
`docker compose up -d ospd-openvas` (needed for `get_feeds`'s NVT entry to ever get a version — omitted by
default from `hack/gvmd-up.sh` unless `SCAN_DATA=1`, and the readiness ladder blocks forever without it).
`docker inspect greenbone-community-edition-gvmd-1` confirmed the running image digest
(`ghcr.io/brennoo/gvm-mirror/gvmd@sha256:cb82c501ca93809405a8d0ed4c21846703466802ce6462536e985625f102b497`,
tagged `v26.36.1` in `hack/docker-compose.yml`) matches the pinned reference exactly — also independently
confirmed by `internal/dockerstack`'s own `TestImageDigestLive`. `get_version` answered `22.7` (the GMP
protocol version; the daemon build is 26.36.1). TLS: `-gmp.insecure`, not `-gmp.servername` — `hack/`'s own
documentation states `gvm-manage-certs`' generated certificate carries no SAN, and Go's `crypto/x509`
refuses to fall back to CN-only verification, so no `-gmp.servername` value can ever pass against this
stack's cert; this was policy already recorded in `hack/README.md`/`hack/gvmd-up.sh`, not something this
run discovered.

**Command line caveat.** The task's literal invocation, `go test -tags integration ./... -gmp.pass=admin
-gmp.insecure -v`, fails in 13 of the repo's packages with `flag provided but not defined: -gmp.pass` —
not a test failure, a `go test` flag-parsing failure before any test code runs. `-gmp.*` flags are
registered by `flag.Bool`/`flag.String` calls inside `internal/gmptest/live.go` and
`internal/codegen/snapshot_live_test.go`; `go test ./...` builds one independent test binary per package,
and only a package whose test binary happens to link one of those two files (by importing the package, as
`internal/schema`'s tests do for `gmptest.Registry`) inherits the flag registration. Every other
package — the root module, `client`, `commands` and its subpackages, `connections`, `internal/apisnapshot`,
`internal/cmd/*`, `internal/imageref`, `internal/mirror`, `internal/dockerstack` (no `-gmp.*` needed at
all) — rejects the flags outright. This is exactly why `hack/README.md`'s own documented invocation, and
`hack/gvmd-up.sh`'s own printed "next steps", scope the run to `./internal/gmptest/...`: this is a
pre-existing, correct convention, not something this run's command line got wrong. Ran
`./internal/gmptest/...`, `./internal/codegen/...` and `./internal/dockerstack/...` separately (the three
packages with integration-tagged files) with the given flags; every other package still ran cleanly under
plain `go test ./...` (see Validation below), just without the live flags, since they need none.

**Totals.**
- `internal/gmptest`: full suite green, `ok` (52s). Two `SKIP`s, both deliberate opt-in gates unrelated to
  environment readiness: `TestCaptureLive` ("pass `-capture` to opt in" — writes to `testdata/live` and
  creates server resources, never run by default) and `TestTranscribeUpstream` ("pass `-transcribe` to
  regenerate `testdata/upstream`"). Zero unexpected skips — no missing NVTs, configs, or feed data; the
  preserved feed sync from the prior session's stack was already complete.
- `internal/codegen`: full suite green, `ok` (31s). Two `SKIP`s, same shape: `TestSnapshotLive` ("only
  writes `testdata/schema` under `-update`; nothing to assert without it") and `TestVendorParity` ("run
  with `-vendor-parity` to diff a local mirror" — needs the gitignored AGPL `vendor/XML` tree this repo
  deliberately doesn't ship).
- `internal/dockerstack`: full suite green, `ok` (0.7s), including the digest cross-check above.

**`TestExplicitEmptySemantics`: real defects found and fixed — not new evidence contradicting T-25.**
First run: 6 of 8 subtests failed. Investigated live (raw-XML probes against the real stack, decode
cross-checks) rather than accepting or weakening the assertions, per the task's hard rule. All six
failures traced to three real bugs in the test file itself, none of them a live-behavior contradiction of
what T-25 classified:

1. **Missing `Text` on `ModifyNote`/`ModifyOverride`.** `commands.ModifyNote.Text`/`ModifyOverride.Text`
   both lack `omitempty` — correctly, since `command_modify_note.xml`/`command_modify_override.xml`
   declare `<e>text</e>` unconditionally required, confirmed live (`A TEXT entity is required`, HTTP 400,
   reproduced via raw XML with `<text>` omitted entirely). Every "(set)"/"(reset)" call in the six
   note/override subtests built a `ModifyNote`/`ModifyOverride` literal with only the field under test —
   `Text` empty, so the client sent `<text></text>` and gvmd rejected it before the test could observe
   anything about `Hosts`/`Port`/`Threat`/`NewThreat`. Fix: every set/reset call now resends `Text` (and,
   for overrides, `NewThreat` — see finding 3).
2. **Missing `details="1"` on the read-back.** gvmd's default (non-detailed) `get_notes`/`get_overrides`
   view omits `hosts`/`port`/`threat`/`new_threat` from the `<note>`/`<override>` element entirely — not
   cleared, simply not present in that view — confirmed by comparing identical requests with and without
   `details="1"` side by side. Every subtest's `GetNotes`/`GetOverrides` call was reading "field absent
   from the summary view" as "field cleared to empty," which happened to match the EQUIVALENT-clears
   prediction by coincidence, not by measuring the right thing. Fix: `Details: &trueBool` (a
   `commands.GMPBool`) added to every read-back.
3. **`GetOverrides`/`GetConfigs`/`GetPortLists` silently ignore a struct passed as a filter arg.**
   `gmp.Client`'s interface declares these three as
   `Get*(ctx, args ...filtering.FilterArg) (*commands.Get*Response, error)` — unlike `GetNotes`, which
   takes a real `*commands.GetNotes` directly. `filtering.BuildFilterFromArgs`'s type switch
   (`internal/gmptest` has no export of it; see `commands/filtering/builder.go`) recognizes only
   `string`/`Option`/their slices, with no `default` case, so a `*commands.GetOverrides{OverrideID: ...}`
   passed as an arg compiles (`FilterArg` is `interface{}`) but is silently dropped — the request sent has
   no ID filter at all. Confirmed directly: a probe reproducing the config-comment subtest's exact call
   pattern fetched an arbitrary pre-existing stock config (`Base`, "Basic configuration template...")
   instead of the one just created, and — since both `afterOmit` and `afterEmpty` re-fetch that same
   unrelated config — the assertion `afterOmit == afterEmpty` passed trivially regardless of what the
   actual test config's comment really was. `modify_config_comment` and `modify_port_list_comment` both
   "passed" in the very first run for this reason: a false PASS, not a real check. The override subtests
   happened to still name the right resource by luck (only one test override exists on the stack at a
   time, so "arbitrary" and "correct" coincided) once bugs 1–2 stopped short-circuiting them first. Fixed
   by adding two local helpers (`getConfigByID`/`getPortListByID`) and one more (`getOverrideByID`, folding
   in the `details="1"` fix from finding 2) that issue the lookup via `RawXML` and decode the result
   directly — the file already used `RawXML` for the omit/explicit-empty steps, so this keeps one
   consistent access pattern instead of introducing a second, `*Raw`-method-based one that `gmp.Client`
   doesn't expose anyway.
4. **Incidental: invalid placeholder values for override fields with real validation.** Once 1–3 were
   fixed, `modify_note_port`/`modify_override_port` still failed live with `Error in port specification`,
   and an intermediate probe hit `Error in host specification`/`new_severity is required` for overrides.
   `Hosts`/`Port` on `modify_override` (unlike `modify_note`, which accepts arbitrary text) are validated
   against a real host/port-specification grammar, and gvmd enforces an undeclared cross-field
   requirement — sending `hosts`/`threat` without also sending `new_severity`/`new_threat` on
   `modify_override` fails closed, even though `command_modify_override.xml`'s `<pattern>` declares both
   independently optional. Neither is a schema defect worth a `CommandWarning` on its own (this is
   validation-strictness/cross-field-requirement territory already documented for other commands, e.g.
   `modify_schedule`'s timezone/icalendar pairing), just something the test's placeholder values needed to
   respect. Fixed by using `"80/tcp"` (a real port spec, distinct from `"22/tcp"` set on create) and
   resending `NewThreat` alongside every override `Hosts`/`Port`/`Threat` set/reset call, matching what
   `CreateOverride`'s own setup calls in the same file already did.

After all four fixes: **all 8 subtests pass**, and — critically — each now demonstrably exercises the
correct target resource with the correct response detail level, re-verified by hand (raw-XML probes
matching each fixed call site byte-for-byte) before trusting the green run. This is the first real proof
of T-25's `modify_note.{Hosts,Port}` full-replace-EQUIVALENT finding, `modify_override.{Hosts,Port,Threat}`
full-replace-EQUIVALENT finding, `modify_override.NewThreat` leave-unchanged-EQUIVALENT finding, and
`modify_config.comment`/`modify_port_list.comment` leave-unchanged/clear-EQUIVALENT findings — every one
now backed by an assertion that was actually checking what it claimed to, not by a manual probe's
transcript. No T-25 classification needed to change; the classifications were right, the test asserting
them was not.

**T-26 type round-trip confirmation.** `internal/gmptest`'s `TestRegistryCoversEveryCommandFile` (122 root
commands) and the capture/live-fixture machinery that exercises `get_info`/`get_results` both ran clean as
part of the full suite; `TestCaptureLive` itself is gated behind `-capture` (not exercised here, since
opting into new captures against a live server is out of scope for a verification run — see the hard
rule against writing fixtures), but the generated types those T-26 fixtures decode against
(`GetInfoResponse.Info2`, `ResultNVT.Refs` as `*ResultRefs`) are the same types every other live-hitting
test in this run (including the fixed `TestExplicitEmptySemantics`, which decodes `GetNotesResponse`/
`GetOverridesResponse` on every subtest) exercises without any decode error.

**Cleanup verification.** After the full run: `get_notes`/`get_overrides` (unfiltered) both return zero
resources; `get_port_lists`/`get_configs` show only gvmd's stock defaults (`All IANA assigned TCP`, `Base`,
`Discovery`, `Full and fast`, etc. — none named `test-explicit-empty-*`); `get_assets type="host"` returns
`asset_count=0`. Every `defer c.Delete*(...)` in the test file (and in every ad-hoc verification probe run
during triage) fired correctly.

**Validation.** `git status` after the full run showed only the `explicit_empty_test.go` fix — no fixture
under `internal/gmptest/testdata/`, `internal/codegen/testdata/`, or `internal/schema/testdata/` was
touched; no `-update` was ever passed. `git diff --check`, `go test ./...`, `go run ./internal/cmd/gengmp
-check` (152 files), `go vet ./...`, `go build -tags integration ./...`, `go vet -tags integration ./...`,
`gofmt -l .` all pass. Stack torn down with `hack/gvmd-down.sh` (volumes preserved, not `--purge`) at the
end of the run.

### T-29 · `filtering.FilterArg` silently drops any unrecognized argument type
**Status:** done · **Files:** `commands/filtering/builder.go`, `commands/filtering/builder_test.go`,
`commands/pagination/iterator.go`, `commands/pagination/iterator_test.go`, `client/client.go`,
`client/client_override_test.go`, `client/client_pagination_test.go`,
`internal/apisnapshot/testdata/PUBLIC_API.txt`

T-28's `TestExplicitEmptySemantics` false-passing `modify_config_comment`/`modify_port_list_comment`
surfaced a real defect in the public API, not just a test bug: `BuildFilterFromArgs`'s type switch
(`commands/filtering/builder.go:57`) recognized only `string`, `Option`, `[]string` and `[]Option`, with
no `default` case. `FilterArg` itself is declared `interface{}`, so any other value — most notably a
`*commands.GetOverrides`/`*commands.GetConfigs`/`*commands.GetPortLists` literal, which the compiler
happily accepts as a variadic `filtering.FilterArg` — was silently dropped from the filter with no error,
no panic, and no log line. The request that went out on the wire was simply unfiltered.

This was a fail-closed violation in the shipped, public `gmp.Client` interface, not confined to the test
file T-28 fixed: 17 `Get*` methods (`GetUsers`, `GetAssets`, `GetTargets`, `GetTasks`, `GetResults`,
`GetVulns`, `GetTags`, `GetFilters`, `GetTickets`, `GetOverrides`, `GetConfigs`, `GetSettings`,
`GetPortLists`, `GetScanners`, `GetTLSCertificates`, `GetAggregates`, `GetResourceNames` — corrected
count; the original write-up undercounted by one) and all 7 iterator constructors (`Tasks`, `Results`,
`Assets`, `Targets`, `Tickets`, `PortLists`, `Settings`) on `client.go`'s `Client` interface shared the
same `args ...filtering.FilterArg` signature and the same silent-drop behavior for any consumer who
passed the wrong thing — exactly the mistake T-28 made independently in three different subtests before
noticing the resulting query was unfiltered.

Caught pre-stabilization, which is the best time to fix it: this library's API is still alpha (per
`AGENTS.md`'s stability policy), so there was no compatibility cost to closing the hole now rather than
after callers started depending on the silent-drop behavior.

**Chosen design: option 1, runtime error.** `BuildFilterFromArgs` gained a `default` case in its type
switch returning `fmt.Errorf("filtering: unsupported FilterArg type %T — want string, Option, []string or
[]Option", v)`, and its signature changed from `(args ...FilterArg) string` to
`(args ...FilterArg) (string, error)`. Every recognized-type behavior is unchanged — empty args, `nil`
slices, an `Option` that renders an empty string, mixed string/`Option` args all still build the exact
same filter string as before (pinned by the existing `TestBuildFilterFromArgs*` suite before making any
change, so behavior drift would have failed loudly).

**Rejected: option 2, sealed interface.** Narrowing `FilterArg` from `interface{}` to a sealed interface
would catch the mistake at compile time instead of runtime, which is a real advantage — but it breaks the
library's own headline ergonomics example, `GetResults(ctx, "severity>5.0")`: a bare Go `string` does not
satisfy a sealed interface without an explicit conversion or wrapper type at every call site, including
the ones in this repo's own `examples/` and doc comments. Trading a compile-time guarantee against a
regression in the single most common, most-documented calling pattern was not a good trade — a runtime
error a caller finds on the very first call (this library has no retry/background-queue path where a
silent-drop-turned-error could lurk unnoticed) closes the same hole without that cost.

**Implementation.** `BuildFilterFromArgs`'s new `error` return is signature-compatible with every one of
the 17 `Get*` methods, which already return `(*commands.XResponse, error)` — each now does
`filter, err := filtering.BuildFilterFromArgs(args...); if err != nil { return nil, fmt.Errorf("<Method>:
%w", err) }` before building its request. The 7 iterator constructors (`Tasks`, `Results`, `Assets`,
`Targets`, `Tickets`, `PortLists`, `Settings`) are not themselves the 7 `commands/pagination`
`NewXIterator` functions — those already take `filters ...string` and never called
`BuildFilterFromArgs` at all; the actual callers are `client.go`'s 7 same-named methods, which return a
bare `*pagination.XIterator` with no `error` in the signature. Changing that return type would be a much
larger break for markedly less benefit (an iterator-returning method's error is conventionally deferred
to `Next()`/`Err()`, not returned eagerly, and every caller already checks `Err()` after the loop per
`commands/pagination/doc.go`'s own documented pattern) — instead added
`pagination.NewFailedIterator[T any](err error) *Iterator[T]`, a small exported constructor that returns
an iterator whose `Next()` reports false and `Err()` reports `err` immediately, reusing
`Iterator[T]`'s existing zero-value-plus-`err`-field behavior (already proven by
`TestIterator_RequiresFactoryFunctions`) rather than inventing a second failure path. Each of the 7
`client.go` methods now checks the `BuildFilterFromArgs` error first and returns
`pagination.NewFailedIterator[*commands.<Item>](fmt.Errorf("<Method>: %w", err))` instead of ever
proceeding to fetch a page.

**Tests.** `commands/filtering/builder_test.go` gained `TestBuildFilterFromArgsRejectsUnrecognizedTypes`
(table of `int`, `bool`, `struct{}{}`, a typed nil pointer, and the real-world case — an unrelated struct
pointer, the same shape as `*commands.GetOverrides`), and every existing test in the file was updated for
the new two-value return (pinning current behavior for recognized types first, as asked, before the
`default` case existed to change anything). `commands/pagination/iterator_test.go` gained
`TestNewFailedIterator`. `client/client_override_test.go` gained
`TestGetOverridesRejectsTypedRequestStruct`, reproducing T-28's exact accidental-misuse call
(`GetOverrides(ctx, &commands.GetOverrides{OverrideID: ...})`) and asserting it now errors.
`client/client_pagination_test.go` gained `TestIteratorConstructorsRejectUnrecognizedFilterArg`, one
subtest per iterator method, each passing a bare `int` and asserting `Next()` is false and `Err()` is
non-nil.

**Live-probe confirmation.** Per T-29's own stated requirement (and T-28's discipline), brought up
`hack/gvmd-up.sh` + `ospd-openvas` against the same pinned `gvmd` v26.36.1 stack T-28 used, authenticated,
and ran the exact accidental-misuse call from a scratch program:
`c.GetOverrides(ctx, &commands.GetOverrides{OverrideID: "00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000"})`.
Result: `resp=<nil> err=GetOverrides: filtering: unsupported FilterArg type *commands.GetOverrides — want
string, Option, []string or []Option` — the request never reached gvmd. As a regression control in the
same session, `c.GetResults(ctx, "severity>5.0")` (the documented ergonomics option 2 would have broken)
still succeeded with `status=200`. Stack torn down afterward (`hack/gvmd-down.sh`, volumes preserved).

**API snapshot diff** (`internal/apisnapshot/testdata/PUBLIC_API.txt`, regenerated via
`go test ./internal/apisnapshot -run TestPublicAPISnapshot -update`, reviewed per T-27's policy that any
diff here is a reviewed API decision):

```diff
 total packages: 6
-total exported declarations: 1816
+total exported declarations: 1817

-func BuildFilterFromArgs(args ...FilterArg) string
+func BuildFilterFromArgs(args ...FilterArg) (string, error)

 func NewAssetIterator(client Client, ctx context.Context, opts PaginationOptions, filters ...string) *AssetIterator

+func NewFailedIterator[T any](err error) *Iterator[T]

 func NewPortListIterator(client Client, ctx context.Context, opts PaginationOptions, filters ...string) *PortListIterator
```

### T-30 · Operationally prove the schema-drift (T-17) and mirror-images (T-22) workflows
**Status:** done · **Depends on:** T-17, T-22

Both T-17 and T-22 were code-review approved with a caveat: neither had ever actually run on GitHub.
Dispatching them turned that verification task into two real findings — one infra permissions issue that
led to extracting the mirror into its own repository, and one confirmed resource-fit problem GitHub
Actions has no easy answer for.

#### mirror-images.yml (T-22)

**Run history:** [32511485115](https://github.com/brennoo/go-gmp/actions/runs/32511485115) (failed, all
15 denied on read) → made all 15 `gvm-mirror/*` GHCR packages public →
[32513680623](https://github.com/brennoo/go-gmp/actions/runs/32513680623) (failed, 5/15 mirrored — all
5 were silent no-ops) → extracted to
[brennoo/gvm-mirror](https://github.com/brennoo/gvm-mirror) →
[32520032218](https://github.com/brennoo/gvm-mirror/actions/runs/32520032218) (failed, all denied on
write) → linked all 15 packages' Actions access to the new repo →
[32521830536](https://github.com/brennoo/gvm-mirror/actions/runs/32521830536) (failed, 9/15 real pushes,
2/15 permission gaps on 2 packages, 2/15 revision-tag collisions) → fixed the 2 remaining packages' role
→ [32525072072](https://github.com/brennoo/gvm-mirror/actions/runs/32525072072) (failed, 13/15 real
pushes, 2/15 revision-tag collisions — the terminal, expected state).

**Root cause 1 — GHCR permissions (infra).** The 15 `ghcr.io/brennoo/gvm-mirror/*` packages were pushed
by hand during development (`skopeo`/`docker login` with a personal token), so they exist in the
`brennoo` *user* namespace with `repository: null` and were private. GHCR requires a package be
explicitly public for anonymous read, and separately linked to a repository via "Manage Actions access"
(with a Write role, chosen per-repository) before that repository's `GITHUB_TOKEN` can push to it — a
repository that merely *shares an owner* with the package gets neither for free. Neither operation has a
REST or GraphQL endpoint; confirmed by testing `PATCH /user/packages/container/...` (404: the endpoint
does not exist) and by listing the GraphQL schema's mutation set, which contains only
`deletePackageVersion`. Both are UI-only. Making the 15 packages public fixed reads; linking each to
`brennoo/gvm-mirror` with Write fixed pushes — but the first attempt at linking left the role at its
default rather than explicitly Write on 2 of the 15, which is why run 32521830536 still denied writes on
`gvm-tools` and `ospd-openvas` specifically, while the other 13 succeeded.

**Root cause 2 — real upstream drift, the thing T-22 exists to catch.** In run 32513680623, 10 of the 15
upstream Greenbone channels had moved since the lock was last captured 2026-08-17. The 5 that appeared to
succeed (`gpg-data`, `gsa`, `gvmd`, `pg-gvm`, `redis-server`) were exactly the 5 whose upstream digest was
*unchanged* — `WriteOnceTag` found the destination tag already correct and no-op'd, producing zero log
output. `mirror.Copy`/`WriteOnceTag` printed nothing on success, so a no-op and a real push were
indistinguishable in the log — precisely what made that run misread as a partial success rather than a
total write failure. Fixed by adding `mirror.TagResult` (`TagCreated`/`TagMoved`/`TagUnchanged`), which
`cmd/mirrorimages` now aggregates into a per-image summary line
